Entries from [`docs/CHANGELOG.md`](../CHANGELOG.md) dated 2026-01-01 through 2026-06-30 — in
practice 2026-05-21 (the project's first entry) through 2026-06-30 — moved out here verbatim on

## 2026-06-30 — fabric live-verify on dev45 (cont.): control-plane PROVEN; Findings C + D

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed (second hardware run, after fixing the template-key blocker A).** Rebuilt the VyOS
template **offline** (guestfish/virt-customize on the disk, *without booting* — a first attempt that
booted before re-templatizing tainted first-boot so clones never DHCP'd) with the orchestrator key
baked into `config.boot`; new template `vyos-fabric-key2` vmid **9353**, dev45 binding repointed to
it. With (A) fixed, the fabric applies. Results:

- **Control plane PROVEN on hardware:** IS-IS L2 underlay across all spine/leaf/border legs; iBGP-EVPN
  with the spines as **route reflectors** and leaves as clients; **L2VNI + L3VNI** up
  (`show evpn vni` → Type L2/L3, State Up, RMAC set); the **anycast IRB distributed gateway** with a
  working **host→gateway data plane** (`ha`→`10.1.10.254`); **eBGP** (`border-1`↔`prov-1`, 65010↔65001)
  + mutual **redistribution**.
- **(C — FIXED) tenant-VRF egress syntax.** The static leak `route 0.0.0.0/0 next-hop <gw>
  next-hop-vrf default` is **invalid VyOS** (`[next-hop-vrf] is not valid / Set failed`). Removed from
  `fabric_render._render_irb`; VRF internet egress is a documented follow-on (border-originated default
  into the VRF, or BGP RT leaking). Guarded by `test_irb_no_invalid_egress_leak`.
- **(D — PARTIAL, open follow-on) cross-leaf symmetric-IRB Type-5 import.** The VyOS route-target form
  is **`route-target both <asn>:<l3vni>`** (NOT `route-target vpn both …` — VyOS 1.5 rejects the `vpn`
  token); it commits and maps to FRR `route-target import/export` (confirmed in both leaves' running
  FRR config). `fabric_render` now emits the correct form (was the rejected `vpn both`); test updated.
  **But cross-leaf `ha`→`hb` still does not route:** each leaf originates its own Type-5 and the L3VNI
  is healthy + `advertise-all-vni` is on, yet the **L3VNI operational export RT stays empty**
  (`show bgp l2vpn evpn vni <l3vni>` → blank), so the Type-5 is never advertised to peers. Survives an
  FRR restart → a VyOS-1.5/FRR L3VNI-RT **binding** quirk, not a syntax error. Tracked as
  `docs/PRODUCT_GAPS.md` **GAP-003**.
- **Teardown PASS again:** `axrange destroy` removed **30 resources**, 0 not cleared. Cleanup: broken
  first-attempt template 9352 removed; working 9353 kept; pre-existing 9351 untouched.

**Net:** the fabric **control plane and intra-leaf IRB are proven on real hardware**; two real config
bugs (C, D-syntax) and the earlier masking bug (B) are fixed. Open: cross-leaf L3VNI-RT binding (D)
and VRF-egress design (C). Full gate-by-gate log: `docs/FABRIC_VERIFICATION.md` §9.

## 2026-06-30 — fabric live-verify on dev45: SSH-failure masking FIXED; template-key blocker

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed (first hardware run, dev45 PVE).** Ran `docs/FABRIC_VERIFICATION.md` against the
`fabric-verify` rig. Deploy ran end-to-end (9 VMs cloned, all ops reported `completed`) and
`axrange destroy` cleanly removed **30 resources** (VMs / mirrors / gretap / 9 vnets / zone / OVS
bridge) — **teardown live-verified**. But the fabric did **not** actually apply, exposing two issues:

- **(B — FIXED) SSH-failure masking.** `configure-router` / `configure-routing` scanned the vbash
  output only for VyOS *config-error* markers and ignored the SSH exit code, so an SSH **auth/connect
  failure** (`Permission denied`, no marker) made the op **false-succeed against an unconfigured
  router**. Proof was data-plane: host `ha` had its IP + default route (cloud-init) but couldn't reach
  its IRB gateway `.254`, `hb`, or `8.8.8.8`. Fix: `ssh.ssh_connect_failed(rc, out)` (rc 255 /
  connection-auth markers) called BEFORE the config-marker scan in both handlers → they now raise
  `router-ssh-failed`. +2 tests; full suite **490**.
- **(A — BLOCKER, needs operator) VyOS template key.** `vyos-template2` (vmid 9351) does not authorize
  the orchestrator SSH key (`~/.ssh/id_ed25519`) for the `vyos` user, so the routers can't be
  configured. Fabric control-plane / IRB / egress verification is **blocked** until the template is
  rebuilt with the orchestrator key baked in (DHCP eth0 + sshd + key + hw-id strip per
  `docs/FABRIC_VERIFICATION.md` §1) or the authorized key is provided.

**Net:** deploy/teardown plumbing + the (B) honesty fix are proven on hardware; the fabric
control-plane itself remains live-verify-pending behind (A).

## 2026-06-30 — feat(fabric): L3VNI / symmetric IRB (anycast distributed gateway)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:** the fabric slice gains **symmetric IRB** so EVPN L2VNI segments are
L3-routed to each other across the fabric — the L3VNI/IRB follow-on from the OptraHealth
twin's documented limitation. VTEP leaves now get a tenant VRF bound to an L3VNI, an
**anycast distributed gateway** (an SVI on each L2VNI bridge — the network's `gateway`,
with a shared anycast MAC), and per-VRF BGP advertising the gateway subnets as **EVPN
Type-5**.

- **IR (additive):** `fabric.tenant: {name, l3vni, table}` enables IRB; an L2VNI
  network's existing `gateway` becomes its anycast SVI. `docs/IR_SCHEMA.md` §3.4/§3.5.
- **Renderer:** `fabric_render._render_irb` (tenant VRF + L3VNI transit VXLAN + anycast
  SVIs + per-VRF Type-5); `planner/fabric.build_fabric_inputs` threads the tenant + per-leg
  gateway/prefix. +2 render tests; full suite 486 passed.
- **OptraHealth twin:** declares `fabric.tenant: optra` + anycast gateways on the six EVPN
  access segments; the clinical/DMZ/data/infra tiers become L3-reachable across the fabric.
  Validates + plans; an end-to-end render of `leaf-e1` emits the full IRB config.

**Egress (same-session follow-on):** tenant-VRF hosts now reach the internet (apt/pip) —
`configure-router` discovers the WAN default gateway and returns it; `configure-routing`
threads it; the IRB renderer emits a per-leaf tenant-VRF default route leaking to the
global table (`0.0.0.0/0 next-hop <wan_gw> next-hop-vrf default`), where the leaf's WAN +
masquerade reach out. +3 egress/IRB-flow tests (full suite 488).

**Authored from the VyOS/FRR symmetric-IRB recipe (no dtDataCenter reference) — live
cluster verification pending, like the rest of the fabric slice.** Remaining fabric
follow-ons: cross-site EVPN stretch, replica-DC AD replication, multi-tenant VRFs.

## 2026-06-29 — feat(fabric): VyOS dynamic-routing / overlay slice (`fabric:`)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:** new **fabric slice** — axRange can now stand up a real DC network
fabric (dynamic routing + overlay) on `kind: router` (VyOS) nodes, not just static
routes + NAT. Ported from the sibling **dtDataCenter** lab's live-verified VyOS
rendering. Spec: `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_fabric.md`. Branch `wip/fabric-slice`.
Implemented + unit/plan-verified (full suite 483 passed); **live deploy verify
pending** (needs a VyOS template on a cluster).

- **IR (additive, stays `scenario_schema_version: 1`):** top-level
  `fabric: {regions[], fabric_asn}`; per-router `routing: {region, role, loopback,
  isis{net}, ospf{router_id}, bgp{asn, rr_server, rr_clients_of, evpn, vtep,
  ebgp_peers, originate}, redistribute, bfd}`; per-network `vni` (EVPN L2VNI) +
  `ospf_area`. `fabric.regions` merges across `includes`.
- **Protocols (VyOS-only):** IS-IS L2 underlay, OSPFv2 multi-area, iBGP +
  route-reflector, eBGP, BGP-EVPN (`l2vpn-evpn` AF + `advertise-all-vni`), VXLAN
  data-plane on VTEP leaves, mutual OSPF↔IS-IS + BGP→IGP redistribution
  (`axr-*` route-maps, tag 9998/9999), BFD.
- **Op catalog:** +`OP-HOST-100 configure-routing` (INFRA; after `host-ready` +
  `configure-router`, reuses its discovered `wan_ip` — no second arp-scan) and
  +`OP-BAR-060 fabric-ready`. 35 → 37 op types (31 action/wait + 6 barriers).
- **Validator:** new `FABRIC` category, `FAB-010..080` (Pass 3). 90 → 98 rules.
- **Adapter / planner:** `adapter_proxmox/fabric_render.py` (pure dict→`set`
  renderer) + `SshBackend._configure_routing` (vbash script-template envelope,
  marker-based error check); `planner/fabric.py` context builder resolves the IR
  topology so the renderer stays IR-free (boundary). `configure-router` now skips
  L3 addressing on `vni` legs (they become VXLAN bridge members) and declares
  `wan_ip`/`wan_iface`/`lan_ifaces` outputs.

**Why:** make a *complete* datacenter twin possible — fabric control-plane **and**
application workloads in one scenario. The multi-site OptraHealth healthcare twin's
WAN goes from static routes to real BGP/OSPF + convergence behavior. dtDataCenter
covers fabric pcap but no apps; axRange covered apps but no fabric — this closes the
gap. Scope locked with the user 2026-06-29: full fabric in one slice, VyOS-only.

## 2026-06-25 — fix(ui): Validate button — disposition case mismatch + --images requirement

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:** clicking **Validate** in the panel reported failure even on a passing scenario.
Two causes, both fixed (browser-verified):
- **Code bug (disposition case):** the validator emits the `Disposition` enum value
  `PASS` / `PASS-WITH-WARNINGS` / `FAIL` (uppercase, dash-separated), but the panel compared
  against `'pass'` / `'pass_with_warnings'` (lowercase/underscore — copied from a stale `dtos.py`
  comment), so every result fell through to "Validation failed: 0 error(s)." Fixed the literals in
  three places: the `ValidateResult.disposition` TS type (`io/api.ts`), the Validate banner
  (`ControlToolbar.tsx`), and the strip color (`DeployTree.tsx`). (Node/op/deploy *statuses* are
  genuinely lowercase on the wire and were already correct.)
- **Operational (`--images`):** `axrange serve` must be launched with `--images <root>` (the root
  holding `images/manifests/` + `images/bindings/`, e.g. `deploy-dev45`) or the manifest registry
  is empty and validation fails `REF-120 … does not resolve to a manifest entry` for every image.
  Documented in `observability/README.md` (control-panel section) with the full serve invocation.
- Plugin 0.1.2 → 0.1.3. Browser-verified: Validate on `ad-domain-lab` now shows
  "Passed with 2 warning(s)." (was "Validation failed: 0 error(s).").

**Affected docs:** `observability/README.md` — control-panel section (`--images`).

## 2026-06-25 — fix(ui): JSX-runtime load fixes — panel RENDERS in-browser (full visual live-verify)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:** loading the panel in a real browser surfaced two runtime faults a clean
`npm run build` + static review could not (the bundle compiles; the failure is in Grafana's
SystemJS loader / React scope). Both fixed; the panel now renders.
- **(1) `react/jsx-runtime` 404** — `.config/bundler/externals.ts` externalized `react/jsx-runtime`
  (+ `…/jsx-dev-runtime`), but Grafana 11.4's SystemJS loader does not expose that bare specifier,
  so the panel failed to load (SystemJS Error#7). Fixed by **not** externalizing them → webpack
  bundles the tiny shim (it still `require()`s the external `react`). Self-contained, version-proof.
- **(2) `ReferenceError: React is not defined`** — swc's default JSX runtime is **classic**
  (`React.createElement`), but our source imports only the hooks it uses, not the `React` default
  (it was written for the automatic runtime; the `react/jsx-runtime` refs in the first build came
  from `@xyflow`, not our code). Fixed by setting `jsc.transform.react.runtime: 'automatic'` in the
  swc-loader options (`.config/webpack/webpack.config.ts`).
- Plugin version bumped 0.1.0 → 0.1.2 across the two rebuilds (Grafana keys its plugin asset cache
  by version; a bump + restart is needed to pick up a rebuild — `deploy_plugin.sh` does this).
- **VISUAL LIVE-VERIFY (headless chromium) PASSED** on `http://10.80.72.52:3000/d/axr-control-console`:
  the `ad-domain-lab` topology renders as a **dagre hierarchical tree** (gw router → corp vnet
  10.60.10.0/24·vlan80 → attacker/dc01/detect/siem/ws01 hosts, IP-labeled edges; 7 nodes/6 edges),
  with the right-rail scenario/target pickers, Validate/Plan/Deploy/Run/Destroy controls, and the
  live DEPLOYS list (real dev45 deploys). Zero panel errors. **This closes the "visual render needs
  a browser" gap from the entry below — the panel is now fully live-verified.**

**Affected docs:** companion to `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ui.md` §12.

## 2026-06-25 — fix(ui): panel build fix + LIVE-VERIFIED data path on the real IP

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- **Build fix:** the scaffolded `.config/tsconfig.json` ts-node override sets `module: commonjs`
  but inherits `moduleResolution: bundler` from `@grafana/tsconfig` — TypeScript 5.9 rejects that
  combo (TS5095) when ts-node loads `webpack.config.ts`, so `npm run build` failed. Fixed by adding
  a ts-node override (`moduleResolution: node`) in the editable root `tsconfig.json`. The plugin now
  builds clean (webpack 5, `module.js` 308 KB; only bundle-size warnings).
- **LIVE-VERIFIED on this host** (`10.80.72.52`), no container runtime — standalone Grafana 11.4.0
  (tarball) + Infinity datasource v3.10.1 + `axrange serve --host 0.0.0.0` against the real
  `~/.axrange/state.db` (11 dev45 deploys) and the 7 `aiscenario/stage2` scenarios. Verified:
  - plugin **registered** (`Plugin registered pluginId=axellio-axrange-control-panel`, type panel,
    unsigned-permitted) and `module.js` served (HTTP 200, 315 KB);
  - `control-console.json` instantiates the panel (`type: axellio-axrange-control-panel`,
    `{apiDatasourceUid: axr-api, pollSeconds: 2.5}`); all 5 dashboards provisioned;
  - **datasource-proxy data path proven end-to-end** — Grafana `/api/datasources/proxy/uid/axr-api/...`
    → axRange API → real data: `GET` scenarios (7), topology (ad-domain-lab 7 nodes/6 edges),
    deploys (11), node detail (dc01 → windows-server-2022); and `POST` validate (real verdict).
    This confirms the pxAnalytics-pattern transport (server-side, auth-carrying) works for axRange.
- **Not machine-verifiable here:** the visual React render (xyflow hierarchical tree + control
  buttons) needs a browser — no headless chromium on this host. Open
  `http://10.80.72.52:3000/d/axr-control-console` to eyeball it (anonymous-admin lab config).

**Affected docs:** companion to `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ui.md` §12; supersedes the "build-pending verify"
note in the entry below.

## 2026-06-25 — design(ui): single-pane Grafana supersedes the SPA+Grafana hybrid

**Authority:** user

**What changed:**
- The UI direction is **revised from the option-B hybrid** (a bespoke React viewer SPA for
  topology/control **plus** Grafana for telemetry) **to a single-pane Grafana consolidation**:
  *all* UI — topology, lifecycle control, node inspector, and telemetry — moves **inside
  Grafana**. The bespoke viewer SPA (`ui/`, Phase 4, on `wip/ui-viewer-spa`) is **retired**.
- This **reopens the `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ui.md` §11 rejection** of a one-pane Grafana surface,
  *deliberately*, on new evidence: the sibling **`pxAnalytics/grafana`** project is a working
  "everything in Grafana" console whose custom panel plugin (`axellio-pxanalyticstraffic-panel`)
  **drives backend write-actions** (`PcapExtractModal` → `POST /api/extract/batch` + poll +
  download) from inside a Grafana panel via the **datasource proxy**. That disproves the
  "Grafana is read-only, can't drive control" premise §11 rested on.
- **Mechanism (mirrors pxAnalytics):** (1) provisioned dashboards over standard datasources
  (**Infinity** → `axrange serve` REST + Prometheus/Loki) for the read-only half — deploys
  op-tree table, pcap table, Suricata/health — **no code**; (2) **one custom React panel
  plugin** (`axellio-axrange-control-panel`) for the interactive half — topology + live
  op-status overlay + node inspector + Validate/Deploy/Run/Destroy — porting the `ui/` SPA's
  React/DTOs and fetching via the datasource proxy (server-side auth: bearer in
  `secureJsonData`; `GF_DATAPROXY_SEND_USER_HEADER` for audit). `axrange serve` plays pxQuery's
  role. Reuses the existing `observability/` stack; reproducible via a `deploy_stack.sh`-equivalent.
- **Settled sub-decisions (user, 2026-06-25):** full custom panel plugin (not dashboards-only);
  **topology rendered as a hierarchical tree layout, not Grafana Node Graph** (we own the layout
  inside the React panel — top-down WAN/router → segment → host; evolve `ui/src/canvas/layout.ts`).

**Why:**
- One component to run, deploy, and auth instead of two web surfaces. Telemetry + control +
  topology genuinely unified. Half the surface (tables, telemetry) becomes free dashboard JSON.
- Decision is reversible at the data layer — the `axrange serve` API contract is unchanged, so a
  standalone SPA could be reintroduced later against the same backend.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ui.md` — revision banner at top + new §12 (sections 1–11 retained as the
  prior hybrid design of record).

**Supersedes:** the UI portions of the 2026-06-19 hybrid convergence (option B) and
`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ui.md` §11's one-pane-Grafana rejection.

**Links:** reference implementation `pxAnalytics/grafana` (provisioning + `deploy_stack.sh` +
`plugins/axellio-pxanalyticstraffic-panel`).

## 2026-06-25 — feat(windows-ad): dangerous-ACL + delegation weakening (BloodHound edges) — LIVE-VERIFIED

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- **`IdentityEntry` gains the BloodHound attack-path primitives** (`docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md` §9):
  - **`acl: [{right, target}]`** — this principal is granted a dangerous right over another
    user/group: **GenericAll / GenericWrite / WriteDacl / WriteOwner** (broad `ActiveDirectoryRights`)
    or **ForceChangePassword / AddMember** (ExtendedRight / WriteProperty with the control-access GUID).
    `create-domain-user` resolves the principal SID, **waits for the target object** (cross-identity
    creation order), and adds the ACE to the target's `nTSecurityDescriptor` — the takeover edge.
  - **`delegation: unconstrained`** — `TRUSTED_FOR_DELEGATION` (the account can impersonate any user
    that authenticates to it — the printer-bug / coerce path).
  - **`delegation_to: [<spn>]`** — constrained delegation (`msDS-AllowedToDelegateTo` +
    `TRUSTED_TO_AUTH_FOR_DELEGATION`).
- Render maps right → the PowerShell `ActiveDirectoryAccessRule` constructor (`_acl_ace_expr`); an
  unsupported right is a logged skip, not a failure. Emission + backend thread the new fields.
- **Archetype content:** `ad-domain-lab` gains an `intern` with **GenericAll over the `helpdesk` DA**
  (reset-and-pivot edge) + `svc-sql` trusted for **unconstrained delegation**; `purple-team-range` gains
  a `jdoe-acl` with **WriteDacl over the `it-admin` DA** + `svc-backup` **constrained delegation** to the
  file server. Both validate + plan.

**Why:**
- Completes the Vulnerable-AD surface: beyond Kerberoast/AS-REP, the misconfigured-ACL and delegation
  edges are the corpus's core BloodHound attack paths (a low-priv foothold → GenericAll/WriteDacl → DA).
  The archetypes now model the full set of canonical AD escalation paths the red chain walks and the blue
  side detects.

**Status:** implemented + unit-tested (render: GenericAll/ForceChangePassword-GUID/unconstrained/
constrained + unsupported-right skip; emission threads acl/delegation). **466 pytest tests.**
**LIVE-VERIFIED on dev45** (`acl-deleg-verify`): on the DC, **Edge 1** `Get-Acl helpdesk` →
`CORP\intern -> GenericAll over helpdesk` (the ACL takeover edge); **Edge 2** `svc-unc.TrustedForDelegation
= True`; **Edge 3** `svc-con.msDS-AllowedToDelegateTo = CIFS/fs.corp.axr.lab`. One render fix during
live-verify: the ACE must use `[Type]::new(...)` (not `New-Object Type(...)`, which won't parse as a
method argument to `$acl.AddAccessRule(...)`); a resume cleared it. `axrange destroy` clean. **Affected
files:** `axrange/ir/schema.py`, `axrange/config_exec/render.py` + `backend.py`,
`axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`, the two archetypes.

## 2026-06-24 — feat(windows-ad): Vulnerable-AD weakening + SPN/OU modeling — LIVE-VERIFIED

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- **`IdentityEntry` gains Vulnerable-AD / SPN-OU fields** (`docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md` §9) that make a
  domain account an *exploitable target* — the OP-ACT AD attack chain's intended objectives:
  - **`spn`** — a `servicePrincipalName` → a **Kerberoastable** service account. `create-domain-user`
    sets the SPN and forces the account to **RC4** (`msDS-SupportedEncryptionTypes=4`) so the Kerberoast
    TGS is offline-crackable (and trips the RC4 IDS rule `9000006`, which couldn't fire before — there
    were no SPN accounts).
  - **`asrep_roastable`** — `DONT_REQUIRE_PREAUTH` → an **AS-REP-roastable** account (`GetNPUsers`
    returns a `$krb5asrep$` hash with no credential).
  - **`ou`** — an organizational unit, created idempotently (`New-ADOrganizationalUnit`) and the account
    placed under it (`-Path OU=…,DC=…`).
  - **`password`** — an explicit (often weak) per-account password, threaded into `New-ADUser` (falls
    back to the lab default). Makes the Kerberoast/AS-REP hashes crackable to a known value.
- The render `render_create_domain_user` re-asserts the weakenings on every run (idempotent
  convergence); emission threads the fields; `_domain_to_base_dn` derives `DC=…` for the OU path.
- **Archetype content:** `ad-domain-lab` (svc-sql → Kerberoastable `MSSQLSvc/...`, jdoe → AS-REP, OUs
  IT/Finance/Service Accounts) and `purple-team-range` (svc-backup → Kerberoastable `CIFS/...`, jdoe →
  AS-REP, OUs) now stand up an *attackable* domain; the chains' Kerberoast step authenticates as the
  lab-default DA (helpdesk/it-admin) and targets the SPN accounts. Both validate + plan.

**Why:**
- Closes the loop on "validate detections against repeatable attacks": before, the AD domain was clean,
  so Kerberoasting returned nothing and the RC4 detection rule never fired. The weakening creates the
  canonical corpus attack paths (Kerberoast, AS-REP roast) so the red chain succeeds and the blue rules
  trigger — the purple loop with a *real* exploitable target.

**Status:** implemented + unit-tested (render: SPN+RC4, OU create+place, AS-REP; emission: fields
threaded). **460 pytest tests.** **LIVE-VERIFIED on dev45** (`vulnad-verify`, COMPLETED first run): on the
DC, `svc-web` = `SPN=CIFS/web.corp.axr.lab, ENC=4 (RC4), DN=…,OU=Service Accounts,…`; `bob`
`DoesNotRequirePreAuth=True`; OUs created. The attack now SUCCEEDS where it found nothing before:
`GetUserSPNs` returns `svc-web` (was "No entries found"), `GetNPUsers` returns a real
`$krb5asrep$23$bob@CORP.AXR.LAB:…` hash — and the suricata-sensor's **RC4 Kerberoast rule `9000006`
FIRES (6×, `DC:88 → attacker`)** for the first time (no SPN account ⇒ no RC4 TGS before). `axrange
destroy` clean. **Affected files:** `axrange/ir/schema.py`, `axrange/config_exec/render.py` +
`backend.py`, `axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`, the two archetypes.

## 2026-06-24 — feat(defense): host-side AD detection (Sysmon/Wazuh substrate on the DC) — LIVE-VERIFIED

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- **`install-defense-agent` gains a Windows branch** (`_install_defense_agent_windows` +
  `render_windows_defense_agent`) — the host-side complement to the network IDS (the suricata-sensor).
  On a Windows DC it enables the **AD audit subcategories** that record the kill chain into the Security
  event log (the substrate Sysmon/Wazuh/Splunk forward): **4768/4769** (Kerberos AS/TGS — AS-REP roast +
  Kerberoast), **4662** (Directory Service Access — DCSync), **4624/4625** (logon), **4776** — via
  `auditpol` with locale-independent subcategory GUIDs. It also adds a best-effort **domain-root SACL**
  on the DS-Replication-Get-Changes[-All] rights so a DCSync yields 4662, drops an optional agent config
  (e.g. a Sysmon XML), and best-effort enables a baked EDR service (Wazuh/Sysmon).
- **Post-promotion ordering:** a defense agent on a DC must apply auditing *after* the promotion reboot
  (or it's wiped / overridden). The planner now scopes `install-defense-agent` with `domain` when the
  host is a DC (`_domain_provided_by_host`) and the op's registry `after` gains a conditional
  `domain-ready[$domain]` — it resolves only for DC-hosted agents (the create-domain-user pattern); a
  non-DC agent carries no `domain` and the pattern drops. `os_family` is now threaded from the host image.
- **Archetype content:** `ad-domain-lab` + `purple-team-range` gain an `overlays.defense` host agent
  `dc-ad-audit` (tool: sysmon, installed_on: [dc01]) — the host-detection half, paired with the network
  IDS (`ad-ids`) and the SIEM (`siem-stack`). Both validate + plan.

**Why:**
- Completes the blue-team coverage for AD: the network IDS sees the wire (Kerberos/LDAP/SMB/DRSUAPI), the
  host agent sees the DC's own audit log (4769 Kerberoast, 4662 DCSync) — what a real SOC correlates. The
  native audit policy IS the detection substrate; a baked Sysmon/Wazuh binary forwarding it is a follow-on.

**Fix landed during live-verify — Windows `write_file` PVE-9.2 wide-char regression.** The Windows
`GuestAgent.write_file` still used `input-data` (the path PVE 9.2 500s with "Wide character … Agent.pm:135"
on any non-ASCII content — e.g. an em-dash audit-comment or a Sysmon XML). Routed it through
**base64-in-command + `WriteAllBytes`** (the same fix the Linux path already had), making every Windows
config write encoding-proof. (The first install-defense-agent run failed on this; the fix + a resume
cleared it.)

**LIVE-VERIFIED on dev45** (`host-defense-verify`: router + DC + attacker, no sensor/tap). After the AD
attack ran against the live DC, the DC's **Security event log captured it**: **4768 ×3** (AS-REQ),
**4769 ×12** (TGS — Kerberoast), **4662 ×15** (DS-Access — DCSync, sample `Account Name: alice,
Properties: Control Access` = the replication right, so the DCSync SACL fired). `install-defense-agent`
ran post-promotion (gated on domain-ready), `auditpol` confirmed `Kerberos Service Ticket Operations:
Success and Failure`. Both blue planes now close the purple loop — network IDS (suricata-sensor) + host
audit log. `axrange destroy` clean. **456 pytest tests.** **Affected files:** `axrange/config_exec/render.py`
+ `backend.py`, `axrange/adapter_proxmox/guest_agent.py`, `axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`,
`axrange/ops/registry.py`, the two archetypes.

## 2026-06-24 — fix(windows-ad): harden wait-domain-ready to gate on ADWS + stability — LIVE-VERIFIED

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- **`wait-domain-ready` (`_wait_domain_ready`) now gates on the ADWS service, not just a one-shot
  `Get-ADDomain`.** The probe requires **ADWS** (the endpoint the ActiveDirectory PS module /
  `create-domain-user`'s `New-ADUser` talks to), **NTDS**, and **Netlogon** to be `Running`, *and*
  `Get-ADDomain` to return the forest root — and the backend requires **`_DOMAIN_READY_STABLE` (3)
  consecutive** successful probes (a failure resets the streak) at the 3s poll interval.
- **Why:** in the AD-defense verify, `wait-domain-ready` passed but `create-domain-user` then failed
  with *"Unable to find a default server with Active Directory Web Services running."* — ADWS flaps
  during post-DCPROMO settling, so a single `Get-ADDomain` success isn't a stable-ready signal. The
  resume re-run cleared it, but the gate should hold until ADWS is genuinely up and stable. Now it does.

**Status:** implemented + unit-tested (`test_wait_domain_ready_checks_adws_and_rides_out_flap` — probe
checks ADWS, a flap resets the consecutive-success streak; the existing poll/timeout tests still pass).
**451 pytest tests.** **LIVE-VERIFIED on dev45** (`adws-harden-verify`: a DC + one domain user) →
`COMPLETED` **first run, no resume**: `wait-domain-ready` held 27.4s (until ADWS+NTDS+Netlogon stably
Running) then `create-domain-user[alice]` succeeded on its **first attempt, zero failures** (previously
the op failed "ADWS not running" and needed a resume); `Get-ADUser alice` Enabled, ADWS Running; destroy
clean. **Affected files:** `axrange/config_exec/backend.py`.

## 2026-06-24 — feat(defense): the AD defense overlay (blue team) — LIVE-VERIFIED on dev45 (purple loop closes)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- **Authored the AD attack-chain detection ruleset** (`suricata_ad.rules`) — the blue-team content that
  scores the OP-ACT AD kill chain: AD service-port sweep (recon), LLMNR poisoning, SMB enum, LDAP
  enumeration, Kerberos roasting tooling, Kerberoast RC4 (etype 23), and the gold-standard **DCSync
  DRSUAPI replication-bind signature** (interface UUID e3514235-… little-endian). Direction-agnostic +
  content/threshold so they fire on mirrored capture regardless of Suricata HOME_NET. Local SID block 9000000+.

  `suricata-sensor`/`zeek-sensor`, read at emit time into the deploy-sensor `config_content` (mirrors
  the defense overlay's `config:` file read). The in-scenario suricata-sensor becomes a rule-driven IDS —
  the modern, self-contained replacement for the standing Suricata sidecar (Option C, whose appliance was
  retired). One new test (`test_suricata_sensor_rules_file_threaded`).
- **Archetype wiring** — `ad-domain-lab` + `purple-team-range` gain a `sensors:` `suricata-sensor` (`ad-ids`,
  primary) carrying `rules: ./scripts/suricata_ad.rules`, capturing the tapped DC segment. Both validate + plan.
- **Live-verified on dev45 (`ad-defense-verify`: router + responding DC + attacker running the AD chain + a
  suricata-sensor with the AD rules).** After the attack ran against the live DC, the sensor's `fast.log`
  fired **every AD signature**: recon sweep (9000001), LLMNR (9000002 ×5), SMB (9000003), LDAP (9000004),
  Kerberos roasting (9000005), and **DCSYNC DRSUAPI (9000007 ×2 — Priority 1, "Successful Administrator
  Privilege Gain")** on the exact `10.73.0.66 → DC:135/:49668` bind that dumped krbtgt. The red half
  (OP-ACT) and blue half (this) close the **purple-team loop**: attack runs *and is measured*. `axrange
  destroy` clean. The suricata-sensor clones the pre-baked image (suricata present, no egress).

**Why:**
- This is the blue-team half the OP-ACT AD attack chain is scored against (SLICE_OVERLAY §3) — the project's
  whole "validate detections against repeatable attacks" purpose, now demonstrated end-to-end for AD.

**Known follow-ons:** host-side AD detection (Windows Sysmon/Wazuh on the DC for event IDs 4769/4662/4768)
needs a Windows `install-defense-agent` branch (current `_DEFENSE_TOOLS` are Linux); the Kerberoast-RC4 rule
(9000006) needs an actual SPN service account to fire (none in the minimal verify domain).

**Affected files:** `axrange/ir/schema.py` (`Sensor.rules`), `axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py` (rules read),
the two archetypes + `scripts/suricata_ad.rules`; **docs:** `docs/SLICE_OVERLAY.md`, CLAUDE.md.

## 2026-06-24 — fix(router): configure-router NAT is now reboot-persistent — LIVE-VERIFIED on dev45

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- **`op_configure_router` (Debian/QGA path) rewritten for reboot-persistence.** Forwarding + LAN→WAN
  masquerade are installed as a **systemd oneshot** (`axr-router-nat.service`, After=network-online,
  RemainAfterExit) whose helper (`/usr/local/sbin/axr-router-nat.sh`) **re-detects the WAN and
  re-applies on every boot**, plus an immediate apply at configure time. `ip_forward` is set both via
  `/etc/sysctl.d/99-axr-router.conf` and the service.
- **Dropped the `apt-get install nftables` dependency** — it was egress-fragile (a chicken-and-egg:
  installing the tool that *provides* egress) and a silent no-op when it failed. The helper now prefers
  `nft` if already present, else falls back to `iptables` (present on the base `debian-12-cloud` image).

**Why (the bug):** surfaced during the AD-attack-chain live-verify (entry below). A `debian-12-cloud`
router clone reboots once on first boot; the old config set `ip_forward`/NAT **at runtime only**, so the
reboot left the gw with `ip_forward=0` and **no NAT** — LAN hosts silently lost egress (the attacker's
apt hung). The NAT was never persisted, and `apt-get install nftables` had silently no-op'd (no `nft`
on the base image, and the iptables fallback was also runtime-only).

**Live-verified on dev45** (`router-nat-verify`: a `kind: router` gw + a plain LAN host): after deploy
h-a has egress; **after rebooting the gw, `axr-router-nat.service` comes up `active`, `ip_forward=1` and
the masquerade are restored, and h-a's `ping 8.8.8.8` still works (3/3)** — egress survives the reboot.
`axrange destroy` clean. 2 new tests in `tests/test_adapter_proxmox.py` (448 total).

**Affected files:** `axrange/adapter_proxmox/rest.py` (`op_configure_router`); **docs:**
`docs/SLICE_ROUTER.md`. The VyOS path is unchanged.

## 2026-06-24 — feat(activity): the AD attack chain (OP-ACT) — LIVE-VERIFIED on dev45

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- **Authored the corpus Active Directory attack chain** as `OP-ACT` inline-steps activities (one-shot,
  local actor) on `ad-domain-lab` (`ad-killchain`) and `purple-team-range` (`purple-killchain`), run
  from the LAN `attacker` against the corp.lab domain: recon (nmap full AD surface) → LLMNR/NBT-NS
  poison (Responder) → SMB enum → LDAP enum → AS-REP roast (GetNPUsers) → Kerberoast (GetUserSPNs) →
  DCSync (secretsdump). Every step is best-effort (logs + continues) so a missing tool/cred never
  aborts the chain — and the AD-protocol TRAFFIC is the captured artifact. **No engine change** — the
  inline-steps engine (CHANGELOG 2026-06-12) renders these `tool: nmap` + `{run: …}` steps verbatim;
  this is the SLICE_ACTIVITY §9 follow-on #2 (domain-actor activities), now unblocked by the shipped
  identity overlay. The attacker kits gained `python3-impacket` (apt) so the chain works off router
  egress without pip.
- **Live-verified on dev45 (`ad-attack-verify`: router gw + a responding `windows-server-2022` DC
  promoting corp.axr.lab with a Domain Admins user + attacker + `tcpdump-sensor`).** Deploy
  `ad-attack-verify-a76ae3bb` → `COMPLETED`, 58/58 ops. The activity ran in-guest (runner + marker +
  `wait-actor-ready`): nmap found the DC's full AD surface OPEN, ldapsearch returned the real naming
  contexts, smbclient logged in anonymously, GetNPUsers/GetUserSPNs drove real Kerberos AS-REQ/TGS-REQ,
  and **secretsdump SUCCEEDED — krbtgt hash dumped via DRSUAPI** (the domain-admin objective). The
  in-scenario sensor captured it: **543 attacker↔DC packets** — Kerberos 88 (20 AS-REQ/TGS-REQ payload
  pkts), LDAP 389/636, SMB 445, GC 3268, kpasswd 464, **131 DRSUAPI/RPC (DCSync) pkts** — with the DC's
  responses. `axrange destroy` clean.
- **Two content corrections found at live-verify** (folded into both archetypes): (1) Debian's
  `python3-impacket` ships `GetNPUsers.py`/`GetUserSPNs.py` under `/usr/share/doc/python3-impacket/examples/`
  (NOT in PATH — only `impacket-secretsdump` etc. are), so the AS-REP/Kerberoast steps invoke them by
  path; (2) the domain creds use the lab-default `Axr4nge-Lab!` that `create-domain-user` (W2b) actually
  sets, not a placeholder.

**Why:**
- Activity is what makes the capture plane meaningful (SLICE_ACTIVITY §1). The AD chain turns the
  identity-archetypes from "a domain stands up" into "a domain stands up *and is attacked*, on the
  wire, for the SOC to detect" — the corpus's dominant theme and the input to the (next) defense-overlay
  scoring loop.

**Known gap (FIXED — see the 2026-06-24 router entry above):** on this deploy `configure-router` left
the gw with `ip_forward=0` and **no NAT** — the gw had rebooted and the runtime forwarding/NAT weren't
persisted (and `apt-get install nftables` had silently no-op'd: no `nft` on the base image). Worked
around live by manually enabling `ip_forward` + an iptables masquerade. **Now fixed**: `op_configure_router`
installs a reboot-persistent `axr-router-nat.service` (nft-or-iptables, no apt) — live-verified.

**Affected files:** the two archetype scenarios (+ their attacker apps); **affected docs:**
`docs/SLICE_ACTIVITY.md` (status banner), CLAUDE.md.

## 2026-06-24 — feat(windows-ad): local users (`create-domain-user` local branch) + archetype content

  host-ready[$host])`. `_resolve_pattern` drops a pattern whose scope var is absent, so a domain user
  resolves both (host-ready[$host=DC] redundant-but-valid) and a local user resolves only

  identities** to both: `ad-domain-lab` gains `wsadmin` (local `Administrators` on win11 `ws01` — the
  local-admin-reuse / PtH lateral target); `purple-team-range` gains `wks-admin` (local Windows admin
  on `ws1`) + `ops-local` (local Linux `sudo` account on `file-srv`) — exercising both OS paths. Both
  validate + plan.
- **Live-verified on dev45 — BOTH OS paths.** Linux: `localuser-verify-d2c6c2fa` → `COMPLETED`,
  14/14 ops; `opsuser` (uid 1001, home `/home/opsuser`, shell `/bin/bash`) in group `sudo`. Windows:
  `winlocaluser-verify-09a8c9f6` (one `windows-server-2022@v1` host, `wsadmin` host: ws01) →
  `COMPLETED`, 14/14 ops; `Get-LocalUser wsadmin` Enabled + never-expiring, local `Administrators` =
  `WS01\Administrator, WS01\wsadmin` (the `Add-LocalGroupMember` landed). Both destroy clean.

**Why:**
- Completes the Identity increment's user surface: domain users (W2b) + host joins (W2 §6.3) + local
  users, on both OS paths. Local accounts (a workstation break-glass admin, a Linux service account)
  are first-class attack/detection content the AD/purple archetypes need.

**Affected files:** `ir/schema.py` (`IdentityEntry.host`), `ops/registry.py` (`after` + `redo_on`),
`planner/pass1_emit.py` (local branch), `config_exec/render.py` (`render_create_local_user`),
`config_exec/backend.py` (`_create_local_user`), the two archetypes; 7 tests (446 total).

## 2026-06-23 — feat(sensors): suricata-sensor via a pre-baked image — LIVE-VERIFIED on dev45

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- **Bake suricata into a sensor image** (the production fix for "suricata uninstallable in the isolated scenario VM"). New **`suricata-sensor` image**: manifest (`provides: linux, sensor:suricata`) + binding with a build recipe that enables `bookworm-backports` and installs the backports suricata (+ tcpdump) via an inline provision script, with a `sensor:suricata` conformance probe. The build runs on the build host's **vmbr0 egress**, where backports IS reachable — unlike the isolated scenario sensor VM (local mirror).
- **Registry/emission wiring**: the sensor-type registry gains a per-type `default_image` (`default_image_for`): `suricata-sensor` → `suricata-sensor@v1` (pre-baked), tcpdump/zeek → `debian-12-cloud@v1`. A sensor with no explicit `image:` clones its type's image, so a suricata-sensor pulls the baked template; `deploy-sensor` then just starts the already-present suricata.
- **Two `deploy-sensor` start bugs fixed** (found live, after baking): suricata's process comm is `Suricata-Main`, so `pgrep -x suricata` never matched (start-check wrongly failed) → use `pgrep -f 'suricata.*axrange'`; and `pkill` leaves a stale `/run/suricata-axrange.pid` that aborts a re-run → `rm -f` it first.
- **Live-verified end-to-end on dev45:** `axrange image build suricata-sensor` → template 100 (probes 2/2 incl. `sensor:suricata`, smoke 13/13) → a suricata-sensor scenario deploys, clones the baked template, the **suricata daemon RUNS on `gre-cap`** (`device: gre-cap: packets, drops: 0`), `verify-capture` passes (Result: COMPLETED), `axrange destroy` cleaned up. **439 pytest tests.**

**Why:**
- Closes the suricata-sensor follow-on with the production-correct approach: bake the tool at image-build time (egress available) instead of a runtime apt-install the isolated sensor VM can't do. zeek-sensor follows the same pattern (a baked image) when needed.

**Affected docs:** CLAUDE.md (follow-on resolved); new image manifest/binding under `tests/fixtures/images/`.

**Links:** bake `ee077ff`, start-fix `<this>`; `docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md`, `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_sensors.md` §10.

## 2026-06-23 — feat(sensors): Phase C — multi-sensor fan-out, Loki export, sidecar reconcile (Phase C complete)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed (sensors design Phase C, docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_sensors.md §10/§11):**
- **C1 — captures-driven multi-sensor fan-out.** A tap maps to a SET of sensors. Default (no sensor declares `captures:`) keeps the exclusive one-tap→one-sensor model (backward-compatible). When ANY sensor declares `captures:`, a tap feeds **every** sensor whose `captures` lists its net/host; `install-ovs-mirror` emits one mirror per (NIC, capturing sensor). **Live-verified on dev45 (C4b):** a 2-`tcpdump-sensor` scenario both capturing `corp` — two gretaps off the bridge, h-a's tap mirrored into both, and **both** `verify-capture`s passed (Result: COMPLETED).
- **Fan-out mirror fix (found by the C4 live run).** Two sensors on one net was first emitted as one `matchall` filter *per gretap* — but two separate matchall filters don't both fire (matchall matches + stops filter traversal; only the first runs). Fixed: per tapped NIC, ONE matchall filter per direction whose action list **chains** a `mirred mirror` per gretap (`...mirror dev g1 pipe action mirred ...mirror dev g2`) — chained actions all run. Both the post-start hook and the in-band re-assert.
- **C2 — `export.ship: loki`.** A sensor with `export: { ship: loki }` ships its structured artifact (Suricata `eve.json`) to Loki via a self-contained python tail-and-push delivered by `deploy-sensor` (new `LOKI_URL` config; python3 on the base image, no Alloy/promtail install). No-op note for types with no Loki artifact (tcpdump pcaps) or unset `LOKI_URL`. Unit-tested; **live-verify deferred** (needs the observability Loki stack standing).
- **C3 — reconcile.** `deploy-network-sensor` (OP-OVL-060 Suricata sidecar) marked SUPERSEDED by the `suricata-sensor` type; kept for back-compat (soc-detection-lab), not extended.
- **C5 — docs.** The capture-plane migration banners (ARCHITECTURE/OP_CATALOG/SLICE_OVERLAY/sdn_capture) updated: the mechanics here (tc → gretap → decap) are UNCHANGED by the refactor; only the receiver moved to an in-scenario sensor VM. (No mass prose rename — the mechanics are the same.)
- **433→438 pytest tests** (C1 + C2 cases).

**Known follow-ons (honest):**
- `suricata-sensor` **deploys** (VM + decap + rules) and passes `verify-capture` (the confirm reads with tcpdump), but the **suricata daemon didn't start cleanly** on dev45 in the C4 run — needs live debugging. `tcpdump-sensor` is fully cluster-proven; `suricata-sensor`/`zeek-sensor` tool-start is a follow-on.
- `export.ship: loki` live-verify (needs Loki up); zeek multi-file → Loki; VLAN-zone sensor egress (SNAT rejected on VLAN zones).

**Affected docs:** CHANGELOG; CLAUDE.md; the four banner docs; OP_CATALOG (OP-MIR-050 already; deploy-network-sensor deprecation).

**Links:** C1 `a6dc816`, C2 `4860657`, C3/C5 `136aae9`, fan-out fix `<this>`; branch context: master.

## 2026-06-23 — feat(sensors): Phase B4 — in-scenario sensors LIVE-VERIFIED on dev45 (Phase B complete)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- **B4 — live-verified in-scenario sensors end-to-end on dev45 (PVE 9.2).** A `tcpdump-sensor` scenario (one tapped Linux host + one sensor) deployed clean: both VMs cloned/configured/started; the planner auto-provisioned the sensor transport vnet (+ SNAT gateway); `deploy-sensor` brought up the in-guest `gre-cap` flow-based gretap decap and started tcpdump on it; **`verify-capture` read the sensor VM over QGA and confirmed a sentinel traversed tap → host gretap → sensor `gre-cap`** (Result: COMPLETED). `axrange destroy` then tore the whole deploy down — 2 VMs, transport vnet + subnet, SDN zone, gretap, mirror snippets (Result: DESTROYED, SDN empty). **Phase B (B1–B4) is complete.**
- **Three fixes found + fixed during live-verify:**
  1. **`verify-capture` inject ordering** (`config_exec/backend.py`): the injector was fired *blocking* (the QGA exec waits while it sends all its pings), *then* the sensor read started — racing the frames past a not-yet-open reader (capture is real-time). Now the sentinel is fired in the **background** (`nohup &`) so it emits *while* the read window is open. A latent bug on every prior verify-capture run, masked by the QGA-500.
  2. **Sensor transport egress** (`planner/pass1_emit.py`): the auto-provisioned transport vnet is now `egress=True` + DNS so the sensor can `apt-get install` its tool (not baked into the base image). SNAT via create-subnet; Simple-zone only (PVE rejects exitnodes on VLAN zones — VLAN-zone sensors need a pre-baked tool or router egress, a documented follow-on).
  3. **Subnet-aware `axrange destroy`** (`adapter_proxmox/teardown.py`): delete a vnet's subnets before the vnet (PVE 400s otherwise, then the zone delete fails). The sensor transport vnet carries a subnet, unlike capture vnets. Closes the API-brief §10 gap.
- Earlier in the same dev45 bring-up, the **PVE-9.2 `agent/exec` "Wide character" regression** (committed with B3) was proven fixed — every in-guest write succeeded where they all 500'd before.

**Why:**
- Closes the sensors design's load-bearing claim (D2: sensors deploy *with* the scenario as VMs) with a real cluster proof, and shakes out the three integration bugs that only a live run surfaces.

**Affected docs:** `CLAUDE.md` (Phase B complete + the fixes).

**Links:** B1 `88d0bff`, B2 `63a3d26`, B3 `2ae310e`, B4 fixes `7f4cfad` + `de8369c`; `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_sensors.md`; branch `wip/scenario-sensors`.

## 2026-06-23 — feat(sensors): Phase B3 — verify-capture reads the sensor over QGA + PVE-9.2 "Wide character" fix

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- **B3 — `verify-capture` reads the in-scenario sensor VM over QGA.** When a sensor is an in-scenario VM (declared `sensors:`), the capture heartbeat now confirms the sentinel by reading the **sensor VM's `gre-cap` decap iface over the QGA exec channel** (not `SENSOR_SSH_*`). Emission threads the sensor's `vmid` (from `clone-vm[$sensor]`) + `sensor_iface` and **orders verify-capture after `deploy-sensor[$sensor]`** (the tool must be capturing). No `sensors:` block ⇒ the `SENSOR_SSH_*` config-sink read path is kept (backward compat). verify-capture catalog gains optional `sensor_vmid`/`sensor_iface`/`sensor_ready` inputs.
- **Fix — PVE 9.2 `agent/exec` "Wide character" regression.** `GuestAgent.write_file` (Linux) now delivers file content as **base64 embedded in the command** (`printf %s <b64> | base64 -d > path`), with **no `input-data` parameter**. Root cause (diagnosed from the dev45 state DB + `Agent.pm` on the host): PVE 9.2 dies with `Wide character in subroutine entry at PVE/QemuServer/Agent.pm line 135` — `encode_base64($input_data)` — 500-ing **every** in-guest file write on dev45 (PVE 9.2), while the same path works on PVE 8.4 (pxtwin1, where provision-app was verified). This blocked both prior dynamic-tap live runs at `verify-capture`. Routing the payload through the command `arg` array (base64 is pure ASCII; args bypass line 135) sidesteps the regression for **all** config-class file delivery — verify-capture, deploy-sensor, provision-app, seed-dataset, etc. `test_guest_agent` updated to assert the new shape.
- **Tests:** B3 emission (verify-capture threads the sensor vmid/iface + orders after deploy-sensor) + backend (confirms over QGA on the sensor vmid). **433 pytest tests** (was 431).

**Why:**
- B3 completes the in-scenario sensor's read path (the sensor is a scenario VM, so QGA — the channel everything else uses — replaces the dedicated SSH-to-a-standing-host). The QGA fix is the prerequisite for **any** B4 live-verify on dev45 (PVE 9.2), since the regression broke every in-guest write there.

**Affected docs:** `CLAUDE.md` (Phase B status + the fix), counts.

**Links:** prior B1 `88d0bff`, B2 `63a3d26`; `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_sensors.md` §5; branch `wip/scenario-sensors`.

## 2026-06-23 — feat(sensors): Phase B (B1+B2) — sensors deploy as in-scenario VMs

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed (Phase B of `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_sensors.md` §5/§12; unit-verified, dev45 live-verify pending):**
- **B1 — the in-guest mechanism.** `OP-MIR-050 deploy-sensor` (config-class, after `host-ready[$host]`): `render_sensor_setup` brings up the in-guest **flow-based gretap decap** iface (`type gretap external` — the receiver of the per-tap keyed gretaps) then installs + (re)starts the tool the `type` selects — `tcpdump-sensor` (rotating pcaps), `suricata-sensor` (eve.json IDS), `zeek-sensor` (logs); `ConfigBackend._deploy_sensor` delivers it over QGA. **35 op types** (30 action/wait, was 29).
- **B2 — emission.** Each declared `sensors:` entry **expands to an in-scenario VM**: a synthetic `kind: host` node (image = `sensor.image` or `debian-12-cloud@v1`, one NIC on the transport vnet) flows through the **existing host lifecycle** (clone→configure→start→wait-os-ready→`host-ready`), then `deploy-sensor[$sensor]`. The planner **auto-provisions an isolated transport vnet** (synthesized `transport: true` net + `create-subnet` for a **host-held gateway**, SNAT off) and assigns each sensor a **deterministic static transport IP**. **`attach-gre-tunnel` re-points** its gretap `remote` from `ctx.sensor_ip()` (config) to the deployed sensor's assigned IP. Sensor VMs are placed in `plan.placement` at emit (they live in the plan, not the IR).
- **Backward compatible:** a scenario with **no `sensors:` block** is byte-identical to before — config-IP resolution, no sensor VM (verified by `test_no_sensors_block_keeps_config_ip`). The transport-IP-required guard now also passes when `sensors:` is declared (the VM's IP replaces the config IP).
- **Tests:** `tests/test_sensor_exec.py` (8): per-type render + the QGA handler (B1); sensor-VM emission + transport-vnet auto-provision + gretap re-point + placement + backward-compat (B2). One `test_sink_routing` assertion updated — a declared two-sensor scenario now resolves the gretap remote to the **assigned** VM IPs (`10.255.250.10/.11`), not the config IPs. **431 pytest tests** (was 423).

**Why:**
- Realizes the load-bearing D2 (sensors deploy *with* the scenario as VMs). Reusing the proven host-lifecycle emission for the synthetic sensor node + the existing gretap framework (only the `remote` IP moves) keeps the change small and the no-sensors path untouched.

**Affected docs:** `CLAUDE.md` (Phase B status, counts); `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` (OP-MIR-050, counts — landed with B1).

**Links:** commits `88d0bff` (B1), this (B2); `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_sensors.md`; branch `wip/scenario-sensors`.

## 2026-06-23 — design: sensors — unified typed in-scenario capture nodes (supersedes the standing sink/sidecar)

**Authority:** design (chat session)

**What changed (design ratified; not yet built — `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_sensors.md`):**
- **Unify the capture plane under one typed `sensor`.** The standing **sink** (tcpdump→pcaps) and the standing **Suricata sidecar** (`OP-OVL deploy-network-sensor`, Option C) collapse into a single `sensor` concept with a **`type`** selecting the tool: `tcpdump-sensor` (= today's sink), `suricata-sensor` (= today's sidecar), `zeek-sensor`, … from an **open type registry** (new types are additive, not architecture changes).
- **Sensors become first-class Scenario IR, deployed in-scenario.** A top-level **`sensors:`** block (evolving `sinks:`) declares ≥1 typed sensor; the planner materializes each as a **VM deployed with the scenario** (clone → provision the typed tool → transport NIC → in-guest gretap decap → start → export), instead of standing cluster furniture. Taps route to sensors via `sensors[].captures` (the renamed successor to per-tap sink routing); a tap fans out to every subscribing sensor.
- **The mirror framework is kept verbatim, only re-pointed.** Capture stays Option 1 (per-tap `tc` mirred → keyed gretap → flow-based decap); the gretap **`remote` moves from `SINK_TRANSPORT_IP` (config) to the deployed sensor VM's transport IP** (planner-resolved). `verify-capture` reads the sensor over **QGA** (retiring the `SINK_SSH_*` channel). Multiple sensors = the existing per-`(bridge, sink)` fan-out generalized to per-`(bridge, sensor)`.
- **Decisions settled with the user (2026-06-23):** (1) top-level `sensors:` block, not `kind: sensor` nodes; (2) **in-guest flow-based gretap decap (Option A)** — host sources GRE from a transport-vnet gateway it holds via `create-subnet`; same-host no-GRE shortcut (Option B) deferred as a perf refinement; (3) **no "referenced / standing sensor" mode in v1** — sensors are always cloned+deployed; (4) the top-level `sink/` appliance scripts move under a **sensor-recipe dir** (`sensors/recipes/tcpdump/`) and become the `tcpdump-sensor` payload.
- **Phasing:** A — `sensors:` IR + `Sensor` model + typed registry (validation) + `sink`→`sensor` rename across code/docs, gretap kept pointed at a config IP (nothing breaks); B — `deploy-sensor` op + in-scenario sensor lifecycle, planner resolves gretap remote from the sensor, `verify-capture` over QGA (live-verify on dev45); C — `suricata-sensor` (absorbs `deploy-network-sensor`) + `zeek-sensor`, multi-sensor routing, `export.ship: loki`, reconcile production-topology.

**Why:**
- A sink *is* a sensor (a node that turns mirrored traffic into an analysis artifact), so one typed concept removes the sink/sensor split and makes new analyzers additive. Putting sensors in the IR makes a scenario carry its own reproducible observation stack (multiple sensors, scoped per-tap) rather than depending on out-of-band cluster appliances — and the existing, live-verified mirror plane is reused unchanged but for the receiver IP.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_sensors.md` — new (the ratified design).
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_production_topology.md` — Tier-2 standing-appliance data plane superseded (reconciling note added).
- `CLAUDE.md` — capture-plane direction pointer.
- *(at build)* `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §3/§5, `docs/IR_SCHEMA.md` (`sinks:`→`sensors:`), `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` (OP-MIR / `deploy-sensor`), `docs/SLICE_OVERLAY.md` §4b (Suricata folds in).

**Supersedes:** the standing capture-sink + Suricata-sidecar **appliance** model of `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_production_topology.md` §3/§4 (data plane only; Tier-1 control+observability unaffected). Absorbs `OP-OVL deploy-network-sensor` (CHANGELOG 2026-06-16) as the `suricata-sensor` type.

## 2026-06-23 — feat: `axrange destroy` — reverse-of-create teardown (engine + CLI + DELETE wiring)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- Built the teardown path axRange never had (`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_api.md` §8; CLAUDE.md flagged "no `axrange destroy` verb yet"). Three pieces:
  - **Orchestrator** `axrange/planner/teardown.py` — `destroy_deploy(store, backend, deploy_id)`. **State-driven** (the §8 "resolve at build" choice, settled to the teardown-routine path, not decommission ops): it reads each infra op's recorded `outputs_json` (`clone-vm`→vmid, `create-vnet`→vnet_id, `create-sdn-zone`→zone_id, `create-ovs-bridge`→bridge_name, `attach-gre-tunnel`→gre_port) — faithful to what was actually built, immune to a since-edited scenario/adapter.conf. Tears down in **reverse dependency order**: VMs → mirror snippets → gretaps → SDN vnets → SDN zone → cluster SDN apply → OVS underlay bridge → state transition + lease/lock release.
  - **Backend** `axrange/adapter_proxmox/teardown.py` — `ProxmoxTeardown`, the concrete REST/SSH reverse ops (reuses `RestClient` + `run_ssh`); behind a `TeardownBackend` protocol so tests inject a fake (no Proxmox), the same boundary the executor keeps with `Adapter`.
  - **Surfaces** — the `axrange destroy <deploy-id> --allow-destroy` CLI verb (+ `--node`/`--adapter-config`/`--db`), and the previously-stubbed `DELETE /api/deploys/{id}` now wired to the orchestrator (returns a `DestroyResponse` action report; injectable `app.state.teardown_factory`).
- **Hard name-guards** (the §8 blast-radius requirement): every resource name is re-checked against the deterministic-naming invariants before it is touched (`vmid` ∈ 100000–999999; `vn[0-9a-f]{6}`; `axr[0-9a-f]{5}`; `gre_[0-9a-f]{6}`). The OVS-bridge step is gated to *exactly* `underlay_bridge_for_plan(plan_id)` for that deploy — a bare `vmbr0` / operator bridge / another scenario's bridge can never be removed (a Simple-zone `vn*` bridge_name is SDN-managed and correctly skipped). A guard violation is reported, never executed, and blocks the clean sweep.
- **Idempotent / resume-safe**: each backend op is check-then-act ("already gone" == success); the deploy is transitioned to the new `DeployStatus.DESTROYED` (+ ops → `OpStatus.DESTROYED`, via `StateStore.mark_destroyed`) **only on a clean sweep**, so a partial teardown is finished by re-running. **Lease-guarded**: destroy takes the per-scenario lease (409 if a deploy is in flight) and releases it (+ resource locks) at the end. API destroy is synchronous in v1 (matches the CLI verb).
- **Tests**: `tests/test_teardown.py` (orchestrator over a fake backend: full reverse-order sweep; idempotent re-run; lease release; foreign-vmid guard; `vmbr0` never reaches the bridge delete; error-continues-without-sweep; unknown deploy) + `tests/test_api.py` (the 501 stub replaced with a real teardown via an injected fake; 409-on-lease). **418 pytest tests** (was 410).
- **Live-verified on dev45 (PVE 9.2.2, 2026-06-23).** Deployed an isolated 2-host tapped VLAN scenario (24/26 ops OK, only the pre-existing `verify-capture` sink-sentinel QGA-500 failing — all resources created), then `axrange destroy <id> --allow-destroy`: **8 resources removed, 0 errors, exit 0** — both running VMs stopped+purged, the SDN vnet + VLAN zone removed, the keyed gretap deleted, the per-VM mirror snippets removed, and the **VLAN underlay OVS bridge removed from the node network**. Verified after: every one of this deploy's resources gone; deploy + 26 ops → `destroyed`; lease released; a re-run is a clean no-op (all `absent`, exit 0). **Blast-radius guard held**: the 5 unrelated pre-existing `vbr_*` bridges, the concurrent Windows-AD deploy's VMs (340926/534743) + its zone/vnet, and all three standing appliances (sink/sensor/sidecar) were untouched.

**Why:**
- The UI's destroy button + a disposable-range lifecycle need real teardown; OVS bridges persisting in `/etc/network/interfaces` was the standing gap. Driving from recorded state (not a re-planned DAG) keeps teardown correct even when the scenario file has moved on, and the deterministic-name guards make "only axr-created resources" enforceable rather than aspirational.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_api.md` — §8 (destroy now implemented) + §9 done-criteria.
- `CLAUDE.md` — CLI verb list (+ `serve`, `destroy`), implementation-status + test count, the "no `axrange destroy` verb yet" caveat.

**Links:** `axrange/planner/teardown.py`, `axrange/adapter_proxmox/teardown.py`, `axrange/api/service.py` (`destroy_deploy`), `axrange/cli/main.py` (`cmd_destroy`).

## 2026-06-23 — feat: D2 fold-in — observability deploy/op dashboard reads the API (host-agnostic)

human-gated (no Docker on the author box). Executes decision **D2** of

  deploy-op-status.json`) was repointed from a **direct `state.db` read** (frser-sqlite) onto

## 2026-06-23 — feat: Phase-2 control-plane API (`axrange/api/` + `axrange serve`)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**Status:** implemented + tested (410 pytest tests, +27 API tests; full suite green).
Live-verify against a real dev45 deploy is human-gated. Implements **Phase 2** of the
UI plan (`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ui.md` §8) per `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_api.md`.

**What was built:**
- **New submodule `axrange/api/`** — a `cli/`-class consumer that **imports** the
  validator/planner/adapter/state store and calls the **same library entry points the
  CLI calls** (no `subprocess`). Modules: `settings.py` (env/flag-resolved config),
  `dtos.py` (purpose-shaped UI-agnostic Pydantic DTOs — never raw IR/Proxmox/`OpRow`),
  `service.py` (engine glue, no FastAPI import — unit-testable), `app.py` (FastAPI routes
  + `ApiError`→HTTP mapping + write-token dep), `serve.py` (uvicorn launcher).
- **`axrange serve` CLI verb** — runs the FastAPI app (uvicorn); `--host`/`--port`/`--db`/
  `--scenarios-dir`/`--images`/`--adapter-config`/`--api-token`/`--spa-dir`. Default bind
  `127.0.0.1`; headless by default (SPA co-serve is a Phase-4 hook, no-op until then).
  Degrades cleanly (exit 2 + install hint) if the API extra isn't installed.
- **Endpoint surface (`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_api.md` §3), all live + OpenAPI-documented:**
  `GET /api/health`, `/api/scenarios`, `/api/scenarios/{id}/topology` (bipartite host↔net
  graph DTO + **live op-status overlay** from state.db), `/api/scenarios/{id}/nodes/{node}`
  (node-detail; config + state.db tiers, live tier marked not-yet), `POST /api/validate`
  (pure), `POST /api/plan` (pure), `GET /api/deploys` + `/api/deploys/{id}` (op-tree DTO
  from `deploys`/`ops`/`op_attempts`), `POST /api/deploys` (async), `POST /…/run`,
  `DELETE /…/{id}`, `GET /api/targets` (redacted — never secrets), `GET /api/pcaps` (stub).
- **Async deploy (§5)** rides the existing `--detach`/per-scenario-**lease**/state-store
  machinery: `POST /api/deploys` builds the plan, takes the lease (**409** on a concurrent
  deploy of the same scenario), records the deploy, runs the executor in a daemon thread,
  returns the `deploy_id` immediately; clients poll `GET /api/deploys/{id}`. A completed
  unchanged scenario is an idempotent no-op. The deploy runner + background are injectable
  (`app.state`) so tests drive the **real planner/executor with a fake adapter** — no Proxmox.
- **Auth (§6):** write endpoints (deploy/run/destroy) require a bearer token when
  `AXRANGE_API_TOKEN`/`--api-token` is set; reads stay open on a trusted bind. A non-loopback
  bind without a token warns.
- **Dependencies:** new optional extra `api = [fastapi, uvicorn[standard]]` (lazy-imported,
  so the engine/CLI run without a web stack); `httpx` added to `dev` for the TestClient.

**Deferred (explicit, honest endpoints — not silent gaps):**
- **`axrange destroy` / `DELETE` teardown is Phase 3** (`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_api.md` §8,
  UI brief §8). The endpoint exists: refuses without `allow_destroy` (400), else returns
  **501** ("no resources touched") — the real reverse-of-create teardown (VMs/SDN/OVS-bridge
  removal/gretaps/state transitions) is sequenced next.
- **`POST /…/run`** (re-fire activities) returns `not_implemented` — the engine has no
  re-run-activities affordance yet (UI brief §9); endpoint present so the UI can disable it.
- **`/api/pcaps`** returns a stub (`implemented: false`) — pcap browse/download home is an
  open Phase-2 increment (UI brief §7); **live tier** of node-detail (QGA/Proxmox) likewise.

**Why:** the API is the keystone the UI/AI-core/CI all consume (`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_api.md`
§1). Building it now unblocks live-testing Phase-1 observability with the control plane in
the loop, and is the prerequisite for repointing the Phase-1 deploy/op dashboard off a direct
`state.db` read onto `GET /api/deploys` (production-topology brief D2).

**Affected docs:**
- `observability/README.md` — note added that the API now exists and the deploy/op dashboard
  can optionally repoint to `/api/deploys` (the SQLite path remains the working default).
- (No change to `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s diagram or `CLAUDE.md` — architectural fold-in is a
  separate step.)

**Links:** `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_api.md` (the contract), `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ui.md` §8 (Phase 2),
`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_production_topology.md` D2 (the dashboard→API migration this unblocks),
`docs/PLANNER.md` §6/§7 (lease + state store the async model rides).

## 2026-06-23 — design: production topology — containerized control+observability plane, appliance data plane

**Authority:** user — the objective and direction are user calls (8100 is a *dev* VM;
production runs the api/orchestrator as **containers deployed locally** on whatever host,
with observability co-located, "whichever works"); the tiering/migration decomposition is
Claude Code's.

**Status:** design-only. Full design in `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_production_topology.md`; no code
on this track yet. Resolves the deploy-host caveat raised while building Phase-1 observability
(`observability/README.md` "Host options").

**What changed (the decisions):**
- **Two planes (D1):** a **containerized control+observability plane** — `axrange-api`
  (the Phase-2 API/orchestrator-as-a-service) + Grafana + Loki + Prometheus, all containers
  under **one compose bring-up** on a single host/VM, dev (8100) and prod interchangeable —
  and an **appliance data plane** — the capture sink + sensor sidecar stay on their own hosts
  (they need a real promiscuous NIC / gretap / raw capture; already modeled as standing infra).
- **The orchestrator containerizes cleanly (the key insight):** every action it takes is a
  *network* action (Proxmox REST, SSH to PVE for `ovs-vsctl`/`tc`/`ip link`/`qm`/VyOS `vbash`,
  QGA exec) — it does **not** run on a PVE node or need host privilege, only outbound net +
  the Proxmox token + an SSH key + a `state.db` volume. So it is a container, not a
  host-privileged daemon.
- **Grafana's production control-plane source is the API (D2), not a direct `state.db` read.**
  The deploy/op dashboard repoints to `GET /api/deploys` (Infinity/JSON datasource) at the
  Phase-2 fold-in; the Phase-1 `frser-sqlite-datasource` is a **bootstrap-only** affordance.
  This is the load-bearing move that makes "where does `state.db` live" stop mattering — it
  extends `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_api.md` §7 ("migrate the direct readers to the API once it lands").
- **`state.db` is owned by the `axrange-api` container** on a named volume; **no cross-container
  SQLite sharing** (D3) — SQLite is a single-writer local file; the dashboard must not contend
  on the engine's live DB.
- **docker-compose, not k8s, for v1 production (D4)** — single-host appliance; matches
  CLAUDE.md conventions. k8s is a future door.
- **No throwaway glue now (D5):** the orchestrator-side `state.db` Prometheus exporter floated
  during the Phase-1 observability build is explicitly **not** pursued — the API supersedes it.

**Why:**
- The Phase-1 deploy-host caveat (stack on 8100 can't read `state.db` on the orchestrator)
  is an artifact of the *dev* split, not production. Picturing production — api/orchestrator as
  local containers on the prod host — collapses it once Grafana reads the API.
- Phase-1 observability was already built forward-compatibly: production Tier 1 = the existing
  `observability/compose/docker-compose.yml` + an added `axrange-api` service + swapping one
  dashboard's datasource. Two of three dashboards and both shippers are unchanged. Nothing is
  thrown away.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_production_topology.md` — new brief (the canonical doc for this).
- (No change yet to `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s diagram or `CLAUDE.md` — architectural fold-in is
  a separate step when Phase 2 builds.)

**Links:** `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_api.md` (§2/§6/§7 — the API this containerizes),
`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ui.md` §8 (phase sequencing), `observability/README.md` (Phase-1 stack +
the caveat resolved), `docs/SLICE_OVERLAY.md` §4b (sensor sidecar appliance),
`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §5 (sink appliance).

## 2026-06-21 — feat: Phase-1 observability stack (Grafana + Loki + Prometheus) authored under `observability/`

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**Status:** authored + config-validated; **not stood up** (live bring-up is human-gated).
New self-contained top-level component `observability/` (sibling to `sink/`). Implements
**Phase 1** of the UI plan (`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ui.md` §6, §8) — the telemetry/observe half —
which is independent of the Phase-2 API and the ported frontend.

**What was built:**
- **Stack via docker-compose, pinned versions:** Grafana OSS `11.4.0` + Loki `3.3.2` +
  Prometheus `v3.1.0` (`observability/compose/docker-compose.yml`; Loki single-binary
  filesystem config). Docker is acceptable per `CLAUDE.md` (not a kernel dep).
- **Suricata `eve.json` → Loki via Grafana Alloy** (`observability/alloy/config.alloy` +
  `systemd/alloy-axrange.service`), tailing `/var/log/suricata/eve.json` on the sensor
  sidecar, JSON-parsing, pushing with labels `event_type` / `severity` / `signature` /
  `src_ip` / `dest_ip` / `proto`. **Promtail documented as the simpler fallback.**
- **Capture-sink health → Prometheus** via a node_exporter textfile exporter
  (`observability/exporters/sink-health-exporter.sh` + a 30s systemd timer) wrapping
  `capture-healthcheck`. Metrics: `capture_up`, `newest_pcap_age_seconds`, `rx_drops_total`,
  `disk_pct`, `promisc_on`. **Complements `sink/` (no `sink/` changes)** — the sink README
  had floated exactly this exporter.
- **Grafana provisioned datasources** (Loki, Prometheus, `frser-sqlite-datasource` → the
  orchestrator's `state.db`, read-only bind-mount) and **3 provisioned dashboards (JSON):**
  (1) *Suricata alerts* — the marquee (alerts over time, top-signatures, by-severity, live
  `eve.json` logs); (2) *Capture sink health*; (3) *Deploy / op status* (deploys list +
  op-status counts, queried against the real `deploys`/`ops`/`op_attempts` schema).
- **Everything parameterized** in `observability/observability.conf.example`
  (ports, hosts, `STATE_DB_PATH`, image pins) — config-over-flags, self-contained like `sink/`.
  A parameterized bring-up runbook + per-component install notes; nothing auto-runs.

**Decisions recorded (Phase 1):**
- **Default deploy host = the orchestrator** (co-located with `state.db` so the deploy/op
  dashboard reads a local file). Documented alternative: the dev45 `axr-sidecar` VM (vmid
  8100, 64 GB/250 GB) — **caveat:** `state.db` lives on the orchestrator, so dashboard #3
  there needs the Phase-2 API (or a mounted `state.db`). **This deploy-host choice is left
  open for the user.**
- **pcap browse/download is deferred to Phase 2 (the API)** — noted, not built.

**Validation (author box):** all 3 dashboards `python3 -m json.tool` clean; all YAML
(compose/prometheus/loki/datasources/dashboard-provider) parse; compose + prometheus pass a
structural check; dashboard datasource UIDs match the provisioned datasource UIDs; every
compose `${VAR}` has a backing conf key; exporter `bash -n` + `--healthcheck`/`--stdout`
produce valid Prometheus exposition. **Untested until bring-up:** `docker compose config -q`,
`promtool check config`, `alloy validate` (none of `docker`/`promtool`/`alloy` are installed
on the author box) and all live paths (scrape/push/real `state.db`).

**Out of scope (unchanged):** did not modify `axrange/`, `sink/`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s
diagram, or `CLAUDE.md` (architectural fold-in handled separately); did not build the
Phase-2 API.

**Affected docs:**
- `observability/README.md` — new component overview, data-flow diagram, runbook, decisions,
  host options/caveat (the canonical doc for this component).

**Links:** `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ui.md` §6/§8 (the Phase-1 spec); `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_api.md`
(the separate Phase-2 the deploy/op dashboard migrates onto); `docs/SLICE_OVERLAY.md` §4b
(the Suricata sidecar producing `eve.json`); `sink/README.md` (the health source).
