## 2026-06-19 — design: UI control surface — control-plane API + Grafana/viewer shell (convergence B) + `axrange destroy`

**Authority:** user — the direction and the load-bearing forks are user calls; the
endpoint/DTO/teardown/sequencing decomposition is Claude Code's.

**Status:** design-only. Full designs in `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_api.md` and
`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ui.md`; no code on this track yet.

**What changed (the decisions):**
- axRange grows an operator UI as a **viewer + control + telemetry** surface — **not** an
  authoring tool (authoring stays in Claude / the AI scenario sidecar). This is the v1
  direction for OPEN_QUESTIONS Q1 (canvas) and Q11 (operator interface).
- **Convergence (option B):** keep axRange's engine whole; **adopt the sibling
  `axDigitalTwin`'s shell** (React `@xyflow` canvas + dashboard + WS log tail + target
  picker + FastAPI `/api/*` + the `axdt edit` SPA-serving pattern + `destroy`/multi-target);
  **drop** aDT's engine (its compiler/driver/schemas/recipe-registry/file-cache state are
  superseded by axRange's validator/planner/adapter/state.db).
- **The control-plane API is a separate, UI-agnostic layer** (`axrange/api/` + a new
  `axrange serve` verb co-serving SPA + API, headless-capable) — a `cli/`-class consumer of
  validator/planner/adapter/state.db with clean DTOs. The UI consumes it now; the **AI core**
  (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §6) and CI/scripts consume it later. Async deploy rides the existing
  `--detach` + per-scenario-lease model (409 on concurrent); plus a `/topology` DTO + WS logs.
- **`axrange destroy`** (the teardown verb axRange lacked) is in scope: reverse-of-create,
  state.db-driven, deterministic-name-guarded, gated by `--allow-destroy`; full teardown
  incl. **removing persistent OVS bridges from `/etc/network/interfaces`**; exposed as
  `DELETE /api/deploys/{id}`.
- **UI forks (settled):** hybrid (Grafana + a control shell, not a single Grafana
  app-plugin); a **functional** topology graph suffices; **canvas = viewer-only** (renders
  the IR + a live op-status overlay from state.db; no IR emit-back); **port aDT's frontend
  into axRange** (one product); **Grafana** carries the telemetry half aDT lacks (Suricata
  `eve.json` → Loki; pcap browse/download over the sink mgmt NIC; sink/sensor `--healthcheck`
  → Prometheus).
- **Node-detail on hover = desired + observed, no per-node agent.** The canvas hover card is
  built from config (IR + manifest + plan) + the state store (applied-op status), enriched
  on-demand with live fields via the **existing QGA channel** (SSH op-mode for VyOS) + Proxmox
  — not a new in-guest agent (which would hit the isolation wall that defeated push-SSH /
  pull-Ansible and would duplicate QGA). Exposed as `GET /api/scenarios/{id}/nodes/{node}`
  (`?live=true` for the live tier). API brief §4.1.
- **Sequencing:** telemetry → API → `destroy` → frontend (each independently useful;
  telemetry is cheapest and engine-independent).

**Why:** the operator "get started" loop (author in Claude → view topology → validate →
select host → deploy → run → see sensor data + pcaps) needs a standing surface over the
engine; reusing aDT's mature shell over axRange's mature engine avoids rebuilding a canvas +
API + dashboard from scratch and hands axRange the `destroy`/multi-target it lacked.

**Design inputs (sibling projects, read 2026-06-19):** `axDigitalTwin` (the shell + API +
destroy/multi-target patterns adopted; its engine dropped); `dtDataCenter` (capture-transport
context). `axr-canvas` is an independent experimental branch and is **not** referenced.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_api.md` — new (control plane + `axrange serve` + `axrange destroy`).
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ui.md` — new (convergence B + viewer canvas + Grafana telemetry + phases).
- Fold-in deferred to implementation: `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (a UI/API plane + `destroy`),
  `docs/PLANNER.md` §6.3 (the `destroy` verb), `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` Q1/Q11, `CLAUDE.md`.

**Links:** `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ui.md` consumes `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_api.md`.

## 2026-06-19 — implementation: dynamic taps + capture heartbeat (verify-capture)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code) — realizing the user-authored design
(the 2026-06-19 design entry below; `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_dynamic_tap.md` §10 scope).

**Status:** implemented + unit-verified (383 pytest tests green; +34 new in
`tests/test_dynamic_tap.py`). **dev45 live-verify remains the human-gated next step.**

**What changed:**

- **IR (`ir/schema.py`):** `tap: bool = False` added to `NetRef` (a `nodes[].nets[]`
  leg) and `Network` (a whole segment). `nodes[].tap` is byte-for-byte unchanged.
  The parser is data-driven (`model_validate`), so it parses the new locations with
  no code change.
- **Planner pass1 (`planner/pass1_emit.py`):** new tapped-interface-set resolver
  (`_resolve_all_tapped_interfaces`) — the deduped union of {all NICs of each
  `nodes[].tap` host} ∪ {the one NIC per `nodes[].nets[].tap` leg} ∪ {every attached
  node's NIC per `networks[].tap` segment}, keyed `(host, net, nic_index)` (a frozen
  `TapInterface`). OP-MIR emission is driven by this set instead of "nodes with
  `tap: true`". Whole-segment **expands to per-VM tap mirrors** (not one bridge
  mirror), preserving east-west capture. `nic_index` follows the attach loop exactly
  (VyOS LAN shift to eth1.. honored).
- **`install-ovs-mirror`** now carries a per-host `tapped_nics` list and per-NIC
  `gre_port__<n>` inputs, mirroring **each** tapped NIC into its own `(net, sink)`
  gretap; the legacy single-NIC `gre_port`/`bridge_name` inputs are kept (back-compat).
  The Proxmox hook script (`adapter_proxmox/ssh.py`) loops over `nic:gretap` pairs;
  `_resolve_nic_gretaps` falls back to the legacy single-NIC path when `tapped_nics`
  is absent.
- **New op `OP-MIR-040 verify-capture`** (`ops/registry.py`): one per (bridge, sink)
  with ≥1 tap. `mirror-ready` (OP-BAR-030) gains `verify-capture[*]` as a predecessor —
  so `start-activity` (already gated on `mirror-ready`) cannot run before capture is
  *proven* live. Catalog totals: 28→**29** action/wait ops, 33→**34** total.
- **verify-capture backend (`config_exec/backend.py` + `render.py`):** renders a small
  ICMP sentinel (`ping -p <hex>`, token `AXR<8hex>` = sha256(deploy_id|bridge)),
  injects it from the injector guest over QGA, and confirms it at the sink over
  mgmt-SSH (`run_ssh_sink` in `ssh.py`) with a `tshark frame contains`/`tcpdump -A|grep`
  filter — fail-loud (`capture-not-verified`) when no sentinel arrives. New
  `SINK_SSH_*`/`SINK_CAPTURE_IFACE` adapter config; **no change to the sink's
  black-box contract** (it reads existing pcap output).
- **Validator:** `STR-190` (every `tap:` is a boolean at the three locations),
  `REF-200` extended to recognize a host tapped via leg or segment (not just
  `nodes[].tap`), and `NET-110` (a `networks[].tap` segment must have nodes + a
  reachable sink). Catalog totals: 87→**89** rules (STR 19→20, NET 10→11).

**Build-time decisions (resolving `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_dynamic_tap.md` §11):**

- **verify-capture op class + routing:** **config-class**, holding only `vm:$injector_vmid`
  (NOT the ansible-pool slot), routed to `ConfigBackend` by `idempotency_class` like
  `wait-actor-ready`; the sink read is done via the adapter SSH backend (`run_ssh_sink`,
  injectable as `sink_confirm_fn` for tests).
- **Injector selection:** prefer a tapped, QGA-capable **Linux** host on the bridge
  (deterministic lowest id) — its own egress mirror catches the sentinel; else any
  QGA-capable Linux host on the bridge even if untapped (it still emits frames a tap
  sees); else **skip with a loud `warnings.warn`** (e.g. a bridge whose only taps are
  VyOS router legs with no QGA). Windows QGA hosts and VyOS routers are excluded as
  injectors (the sentinel is a POSIX `sh`/`ping`).
- **Sentinel form:** small ICMP echo with the token in the **payload** (`ping -p` hex;
  the ICMP id field isn't settable via stock `ping`), ASCII-grep-friendly so both the
  tshark and tcpdump confirm paths match; capped at `ping -p`'s 16 bytes (fits the GRE
  MTU).
- **Confirm latency:** a short `tcpdump`/`tshark`-with-timeout **on the sink's
  decapsulated capture iface** over mgmt-SSH (lower latency than waiting for the
  rotated pcap to flush), prefer `tshark -a duration -c 1`, fall back to
  `timeout … tcpdump -A | grep`.
- **verify-capture predecessors:** `attach-gre-tunnel[$net@$sink]` + `install-ovs-mirror[*]`
  (every tap on the bridge live before injecting) + `host-ready[$host]` (injector OS-ready).

**Affected docs:** `docs/IR_SCHEMA.md` §3.1/§3.2/§6; `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` §5.3/§5.7/§8
(+OP-MIR-040, counts); `docs/VALIDATOR.md` §5 (STR-190, REF-200, NET-110, counts);
`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §3 (three tap granularities + verify-capture liveness gate);
`CLAUDE.md` (op/rule/test counts + slice state).

**Links:** realizes the 2026-06-19 design entry below; builds on CHANGELOG 2026-06-03
(per-tap sink routing) and 2026-06-04 (Option 1 capture).

## 2026-06-19 — design: dynamic (declarative) tap points + capture heartbeat

**Authority:** user — the four load-bearing forks are user decisions; decomposition, the
`tap:` placement, and the `verify-capture` mechanics are Claude Code's.

**Status:** design-only. No code landed; formal-spec fold-in (IR_SCHEMA / OP_CATALOG /
VALIDATOR / ARCHITECTURE) happens at implementation time. Full design in
`docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_dynamic_tap.md`.

**What changed (the decisions):**
- Capture gains a **more configurable tap point** as a *lighter* extension of the existing
  `tap:` boolean — allowed at three IR locations, all realized by the **current** mechanism
  (host-side `tc` → keyed gretap → merged sink, Option 1): `nodes[].tap` (whole host —
  **unchanged default**), `nodes[].nets[].tap` (one leg of a multi-homed node — NEW),
  `networks[].tap` (whole segment — NEW). `networks[].tap` also backs the already-existing
  `tap_net` tap→sink target (REF-200).
- **Fork 1 — declarative-per-scenario**, not runtime-mutable (taps resolved from the IR at
  plan time; no apply-to-running path).
- **Fork 2 — lighter `tap:` extension**, not the full `cap_nets[*].taps[]` block.
- **Fork 3 — keep the merged sink**; no ERSPAN/per-segment attribution; GRE key stays
  collision-avoidance-only; the no-correlation boundary holds.
- **Fork 4 — borrow dtDataCenter's heartbeat** as a new deploy-time op **`verify-capture`
  (OP-MIR-040)**: per (bridge, sink) with a tap, inject a QGA sentinel from a tapped guest,
  confirm it at the sink by reading the newest pcap over mgmt (no sink-contract change), and
  feed `mirror-ready`. Upgrades `mirror-ready` from "mirror commands issued" to "a frame
  provably reached the sink"; since `start-activity` already gates on `mirror-ready`,
  activity now cannot run before capture is proven live.
- **Whole-segment expands to per-VM tap mirrors**, not a single bridge mirror — preserves
  Option 1's east-west capture guarantee.
- **Router/link taps realize host-side `tc`** (no VRF, no in-guest VyOS mirror), so axRange
  inherits dtDataCenter's two ERSPAN-on-VyOS landmines (tunnel-in-VRF silent failure; VyOS
  `egress`/`ingress`→ingress-only) as non-issues.

**Why:** the structural/security-twin direction wants the capture point chosen freely
(host / segment / link) per scenario, with the host-tap default unchanged; and the
deploy-time heartbeat closes the silent-zero-capture failure mode that already bit the
project (CHANGELOG 2026-06-03 SDN-broke-the-mirror: 25/25 COMPLETED, zero frames, silently).

**Design inputs (sibling projects, read 2026-06-19):** `axDigitalTwin`'s declarative
`cap_nets[*].taps[]` multi-granularity tap model (we take the *target model*, not the full
block; its Phase-4b install is not live-verified there); `dtDataCenter`'s portable
`docs/CAPTURE_TRANSPORT.md` pattern + heartbeat practice + the two inherited-as-non-issue
landmines.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_dynamic_tap.md` — new (the full design + slice scope + open questions).
- Fold-in deferred to implementation: `docs/IR_SCHEMA.md` §3.1/§3.2/§6, `docs/OP_CATALOG.md`
  §5.3/§5.7/§8 (new OP-MIR-040), `docs/VALIDATOR.md` (REF-200 + shape rules),
  `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §3, `CLAUDE.md`.

**Links:** builds on CHANGELOG 2026-06-03 (per-tap sink routing) and 2026-06-04 (Option 1
capture via per-tap `tc` mirror).

## 2026-06-17 — implementation: join-host-to-domain via explicit `node.joins:` (supersedes I1=infer)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code) — realizing the user-authored explicit-join decision
(CHANGELOG 2026-06-16, W2 scope).

**What changed:**

`join-host-to-domain` (OP-OVL-030) is implemented — member hosts join the AD domain W2a/W2b stand
up. Driven by an **explicit IR field**, not the ratified `I1=infer` activity inference.
Unit-tested + plan-verified against the dev45 catalog; live-verify pending.

- **IR:** a new optional `joins: <fqdn>` on `Node` (`ir/schema.py`). Pydantic `extra=allow`, so no
  validator break; documented in IR_SCHEMA.
- **Planner (`pass1_emit._emit_overlays`):** per node with `joins:`, emit `join-host-to-domain`
  (scope `{host, domain}`) gated on `domain-ready[$domain]` + `host-ready[$host]`, threading the
  domain's DC IP (`_dc_host_for_domain` → the DC node's static IP — the host's DNS) + the joining
  host's vmid. Non-Windows `joins:` or a domain with no DC → clear EmissionError. Emitted before the
  overlays-block guard (it's a node field, not an `overlays.*` entry).
- **Render/backend:** `render_join_to_domain` points the host's physical-NIC DNS at the DC then
  `Add-Computer -Credential <NETBIOS>\Administrator -Force` (idempotent — skips if already joined);
  `_join_host_to_domain` runs it and drives the reboot reboot-tolerantly (reuses W2a's boot-id wait).
- **Deterministic domain-admin credential:** the W2a promotion (`render_windows_adds_promotion`) now
  sets the local Administrator password to the lab default (`net user Administrator`) before
  promoting, so the post-promotion *domain* Administrator credential the join authenticates with is
  reliable regardless of the template's build password. (A small change to live-verified W2a — the
  promotion is unchanged otherwise; deploy-side, no re-bake.)
- **Registry:** OP-OVL-030 description updated (explicit field, not inferred); `dc_ip` input added.

**Why:** the explicit field is deterministic, has no activity-layer coupling (the OP-ACT engine
does not resolve domain actors), and is visible in the IR — the user's W2-scope decision.

**Verification:** +9 tests in `tests/test_windows_ad.py` (render / backend / emission incl. the
domain-ready + host-ready edges, non-Windows + no-DC rejection); 3 W2a promotion tests updated for
the new `admin_password` arg. **340 pytest pass.** Plan-verified: `scenario-w2-join-verify.yaml`
(DC + member with `joins:`) emits `provision-app → wait-domain-ready → domain-ready →
join-host-to-domain`.

**Affected docs:** `docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md` §6.3 (+ banner, §6.4); `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` §5.5
(I1=infer marker → implemented); `docs/IR_SCHEMA.md` (the `joins:` node field).

**Supersedes:** the `I1=infer` decision (OP_CATALOG §5.5) — for host-domain join, now an explicit
`node.joins:` field (the trigger; the op signature is unchanged).

**Follow-on:** live-verify on dev45 (deploy → `Get-ADComputer` on the DC); wire the archetypes'
workstations (`ad-domain-lab` ws01, `purple-team-range` ws1/ws2) to `joins:` their domains.


## 2026-06-17 — implementation: W2b — create-domain-user (domain branch)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

W2b of the Windows-AD identity slice (docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md §6.2) — the second half of the
first identity increment: creating AD **domain users** on the DC W2a stands up. **Implemented +
unit-tested + plan-verified + LIVE-VERIFIED on dev45 (2026-06-17).**

- **Planner (`pass1_emit`):** lifted the wholesale `overlays.identity` reject; added an identity
  branch to `_emit_overlays`. Per non-`external` identity with a `domain:`, it resolves the DC host
  (`_dc_host_for_domain` — the `role: adds` app providing that domain) and emits `create-domain-user`
  (OP-OVL-020) scoped `{identity, host, domain}`, `target_vmid` from the DC's clone-vm, gated on
  `domain-ready[$domain]` (the W2a barrier). `external:` → no op (decision I2); no-`domain:` (local)
  identities are a clear deferred EmissionError.
- **Render (`config_exec/render.py`):** `render_create_domain_user` — idempotent `New-ADUser`
  (`Get-ADUser` guard, enabled + non-expiring) + best-effort `Add-ADGroupMember` per group;
  SamAccountName sanitized to ≤20 chars; single-quote-escaped.
- **Backend (`config_exec/backend.py`):** `_create_domain_user` runs the script on the DC over
  QGA-PowerShell, lab-default password (`_WINDOWS_DEFAULT_USER_PW`).

**Scope notes / small deviations from the §6.2 draft:**
- `create-domain-user` is scoped `{identity, host, domain}` (not `{identity, host}`) — the `domain`
  scope key is what makes the registry `after domain-ready[$domain]` resolve.
- `overlay-ready[identity:*]` is **not** emitted, matching the existing data/defense overlays
  (nothing consumes it under the inline-steps activity MVP); it lands when an activity depends on a
  specific identity.

**Verification:** +10 tests in `tests/test_windows_ad.py` (render / backend / emission incl. the
`domain-ready` edge); the stale overlay test `test_identity_overlay_rejected` updated to the new
no-DC-rejection behavior. **331 pytest pass.** Plan-verified: a `windows-server-2022` DC + an
`overlays.identity` domain user emit `provision-app → wait-domain-ready → domain-ready →
create-domain-user`.

**Live-verify (dev45, 2026-06-17):** scenario `deploy-dev45/scenario-w2b-verify.yaml` (a
`windows-server-2022` DC providing `corp.axr.lab` + `overlays.identity` user `alice` in
`Domain Admins`) → deploy **`win-w2b-v1-42e030b3`** → **`Result: COMPLETED`, 17/17 ops, 0 failed,
0 halted** — including `create-domain-user[corp.axr.lab@dc01@alice]` after the W2a chain
(`provision-app → wait-domain-ready → domain-ready`). First try, no fixes needed (the W2a DC
promotion ran unchanged; W2b added the one new op). Done-criterion confirmed on the DC over
QGA-PowerShell: `Get-ADUser alice` → `SamAccountName=alice, Enabled=True,
DistinguishedName=CN=alice,CN=Users,DC=corp,DC=axr,DC=lab`; `Get-ADPrincipalGroupMembership alice`
→ `Domain Users, Domain Admins` (the `Add-ADGroupMember` group-add landed). The verify clone +
its SDN zone/vnet were torn down after.

**Affected docs:** `docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md` §6.2 + STATUS banner (W2b → live-verified).

## 2026-06-17 — implementation: W2a live-verified on dev45 + the W1 Windows-substrate OOBE fix

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

W2a (Windows-AD `role: adds` DC promotion + `wait-domain-ready`, docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md §6.1) is
now **live-verified end-to-end on dev45 (PVE 9.2)** — a `windows-server-2022` clone specializes
cleanly, `provision-app[role: adds]` promotes it to the first DC of a new forest, and inside the
DC `(Get-ADDomain).DNSRoot == corp.axr.lab` (NetBIOS `CORP`, `Windows2016Domain`, ADWS + NTDS
running). Deploy `win-w2a-v3-059da956`: 16/16 ops COMPLETED, 0 failed. **Iterations: 1.**

The W2a op logic (commit 8955f8b) was already correct — the blocker was the **W1 Windows
substrate**: a sysprepped clone never reached `IMAGE_STATE_COMPLETE`. A read-only guest probe
showed `ImageState=IMAGE_STATE_UNDEPLOYABLE` (stuck/looping in OOBE-specialize) with the os-ready
marker never dropped, so `wait-os-ready` failed and the DC promotion never started. Two
template-side fixes (bake; commit 0b606fa):

- **Clone OOBE actually auto-completes now.** `render_sysprep_unattend` (`bake_windows.py`) adds a
  `Microsoft-Windows-International-Core` component (Input/System/UI/UserLocale) to the
  `oobeSystem` pass plus `<SkipMachineOOBE>`/`<SkipUserOOBE>`. Server's first OOBE screen ("Hi
  there") is region/keyboard, governed by **International-Core, NOT the `<OOBE><Hide*>` flags** —
  the Hide* flags alone left the clone stalled there forever. With International-Core + Skip*OOBE,
  OOBE skips straight through to `IMAGE_STATE_COMPLETE`.
- **The os-ready signal moved to a SYSTEM-context hook.** New `render_setup_complete_cmd` +
  `proxmox_build.stage_setup_complete` bake `C:\Windows\Setup\Scripts\SetupComplete.cmd` into the
  template before sysprep (`builder.py` step 7). It runs once as SYSTEM at the very end of Setup —
  **no interactive logon required** — and ensures QGA auto-start + drops the `axr-osready` marker.
  This decouples readiness from AutoLogon/FirstLogonCommands/cloudbase-init, all of which were
  unreliable: the clone never reached an interactive logon, so the FirstLogonCommands that
  previously dropped the marker never ran. The marker is no longer in the clone unattend.

**cloudbase-init dropped as a W2a dependency.** The W0 bake's cloudbase-init MSI install silently
never produced an installed service (build-VM egress, unverified), but cloudbase-init is not
needed for W2a — the generalized computer name is fine for a single-DC forest, and SetupComplete.cmd
covers readiness. Re-instating cloudbase-init (or bundling its MSI on an attached ISO) is a
follow-up for per-deploy hostname/password.

**Why:** the W2a done-criteria (docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md §6.5) needed a cleanly-specialized,
QGA-answering, AD-promotable Windows clone, which the W1 substrate did not reliably produce. The
fix is on the readiness path only; the W2a/promotion logic was unchanged.

**Affected docs:** `docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md` §6.1 + STATUS banner (W2a → live-verified; W1 OOBE
fix recorded); `docs/CHANGELOG.md` (this entry).

**Follow-on:** re-instate cloudbase-init for per-deploy hostname/password (W1.3); W2b
(`create-domain-user`); provision-app-vs-router-ready ordering already tracked separately.

**Links:** template_vmid 101 (re-baked, 4/4 probes); commits 0b606fa (bake fix).


## 2026-06-16 — implementation: W2a — role: adds DC promotion + wait-domain-ready (Windows AD)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

W2a of the Windows-AD identity slice (docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md §6.1) — standing an AD forest
up. **Implemented + unit-tested (16 tests in tests/test_windows_ad.py; 318 total). Live-verify
on dev45 is PENDING** (a real Install-ADDSForest run).

- **`role: adds` lifted through `provision-app`.** `pass1_emit._classify_install` now passes
  `role: adds` into `provision-app`'s `install_actions` (other `role:` values stay a clear
  error); the forest FQDN is resolved from the DC app's `provides:[{domain}]` and threaded as
  `domain_fqdn`. `provision-app` stays config-class — `role: adds` is just another install-action
  kind it dispatches (OP_CATALOG §5.5 line 673: "domain creation is the DC's `provision-app`").
- **Reboot-tolerant promotion** (`config_exec`): `render_windows_adds_promotion`
  (Install-WindowsFeature AD-DS → Install-ADDSForest `-NoRebootOnCompletion -InstallDns`,
  guarded against an already-promoted DC, NetBIOS ≤15); `_promote_dc_windows` runs it, drives the
  reboot, and waits for the guest to return by a boot-id (`LastBootUpTime.Ticks`) change — the
  QGA-drop/return handling, mirroring W0 `sysprep_seal` but waiting for *return*, not stop.
- **`wait-domain-ready`** (OP-OVL-010) emitted after the DC's `provision-app`; polls `Get-ADDomain`
  inside the DC over QGA until AD serves; feeds the `domain-ready[$fqdn]` barrier.

**Registry corrections (same class as the OP-ACT star-tail fixes — latent: these ops were never
emitted before):**

- `wait-domain-ready` is **config-class** (`idempotency_class=CONFIG`), so the executor routes its
  in-guest poll to the ConfigBackend (like `wait-actor-ready`); it holds only `vm:$dc_vmid`, never
  `ansible-pool`.
- `wait-domain-ready.after` → `provision-app[$dc_app@*]`; the `domain-ready` predecessor →
  `wait-domain-ready[$domain@*]`. The single-scope forms never resolved against the multi-scope
  `provision-app` / `{domain,dc_app}`-scoped wait op.

**Why:** the scoped W2a+W2b first increment (the W2-scope entry below). W2a is the foundation —
a forest must serve before users can be created (W2b).

**Affected docs:** `docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md` §6.1 (+ STATUS banner); `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` §5.5
(implemented note + the `I1=infer` supersession marker).

**Follow-on:** W2b (`create-domain-user` + unblock the identity overlay reject); live-verify W2a
on dev45 (DC networking — own DNS / static IP; the real reboot timing; SafeMode password source).


## 2026-06-16 — user: W2 (Windows-AD identity) scoped → W2a+W2b first; host-join goes explicit (supersedes I1=infer)

**Authority:** user (the two load-bearing scope forks) — decomposition, code-location mapping,
and done-criteria are Claude Code's.

**What changed:**

The W2 increment of the Windows-AD identity slice (docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md §6) is scoped. Two
user forks set its shape:

- **First increment = W2a + W2b only**, not the whole identity slice:
  - **W2a — `role: adds` DC promotion + `wait-domain-ready`.** Lift the `role` gate in
    `_classify_install` (`pass1_emit.py`) for `role: adds` so the domain stands up through the
    *existing* config-class `provision-app` (Windows branch, W1.4), per OP_CATALOG §5.5 line 673
    ("domain creation is the DC's `provision-app` with `role: adds`" — an OP-APP op, not OP-OVL).
    The Windows body is `Install-WindowsFeature AD-Domain-Services` + `Install-ADDSForest`,
    **reboot-tolerant** (mirrors W0's `sysprep_seal` QGA-drop handling, but waits for the guest
    to *return*). `wait-domain-ready` (OP-OVL-010) polls `Get-ADDomain` inside the DC over QGA;
    feeds `domain-ready[$fqdn]` (OP-BAR-040). Domain FQDN resolves from the DC app's
    `provides: [{domain: <fqdn>}]`.
  - **W2b — `create-domain-user` (domain branch).** Unblock the `_emit_overlays` identity reject
    (`pass1_emit.py:299`); emit `create-domain-user` (OP-OVL-020) for non-`external` domain
    identities, after `domain-ready`; `New-ADUser` + group adds on the DC over QGA.
  - **Deferred to later W2 increments (will be built):** `join-host-to-domain`, local users
    (`New-LocalUser`), and the `ad-domain-lab` / `purple-team-range` archetype content.
- **`join-host-to-domain` becomes an explicit IR field** (e.g. `node.joins: <domain-fqdn>` on
  the joining node), **superseding the ratified decision `I1=infer`** (OP_CATALOG §5.5/§5.6 —
  infer a host→domain join from an activity placing a domain actor on the host). The
  `join-host-to-domain` op signature (OP-OVL-030) is unchanged; only its *trigger* changes from
  inferred to an explicit field. The same explicit shape is the likely answer for local-user
  host binding (`IdentityEntry` has no `host:` field) — decided when local users are built.

**Why:**

- The ratified ordering chain (OP_CATALOG §5.5/§5.7) already separates standing the domain up
  (`role: adds` → `wait-domain-ready` → `domain-ready`) from populating it (`create-domain-user`)
  from joining hosts (`join-host-to-domain`). W2a+W2b take the first two links and give a
  live-verifiable milestone (a DC + a domain user) without the activity-layer coupling the join
  inference needs.
- `I1=infer` couples identity bring-up to activity-actor resolution (§5.6), which the OP-ACT
  engine does not implement for domain actors. An explicit field is deterministic, has no
  activity-layer coupling, and is visible in the IR — a small honest schema change vs. the
  catalog's single largest "the IR doesn't say it" inference.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md` — §6 rewritten into W2a / W2b / explicit-join / deferred /
  done-criteria; STATUS banner, §2 decomposition row, §3 M3 milestone, §7/§8 reconciled.
- `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` §5.5/§5.6 — decision `I1=infer` superseded for host-domain join (the
  catalog op signature stays; this entry records the supersession of its *trigger*).

**Supersedes:** the `I1=infer` portion of the OP-OVL identity decomposition (OP_CATALOG §5.5,
"`join-host-to-domain` is planner-inferred (decision I1=infer)") — for host-domain join, replaced
by an explicit `node.joins:` IR field.

**Links:** docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md §6; docs/OP_CATALOG.md §5.5/§5.6/§5.7; CHANGELOG 2026-06-16
(W0 imagebuild, W1 substrate).


## 2026-06-16 — implementation: W1 Windows substrate — live-verified on dev45

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

W1 of the Windows-AD identity slice (docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md §5) — the **Windows
substrate** a Windows clone deploys + configures over, built on W0's template. Four
pieces, each OS-family-branched off a default-Linux path so every existing Linux caller
is byte-for-byte unchanged:

- **W1.1 — OS-aware QGA channel** (`adapter_proxmox/guest_agent.py`): `GuestAgent` gains an
  `os_family`; `write_file` uses PowerShell `[IO.File]::WriteAllText` on Windows (vs Linux
  `cat >`), and a new `shell_exec` wraps a command in `sh -lc` / `powershell -Command`.
- **W1.2 — Windows `wait-os-ready`** (`rest.py`): branches on the already-threaded
  `os_family` — Windows polls QGA-up + a CIM/PowerShell OS-responsive probe instead of
  `cloud-init status --wait` (which doesn't exist on Windows).
- **W1.3 — Windows first-boot specialization** (`rest.py`): `op_configure_cloud_init`
  renders a cloudbase-init `<powershell>` snippet (Administrator password, ComputerName
  NetBIOS≤15, QGA, os-ready marker) and attaches it via cicustom + ciuser=Administrator;
  `pass1_emit` threads `os_family` into the op.
- **W1.4 — Windows `provision-app` / `run-script`** (`config_exec/`): packages install via
  chocolatey/PowerShell (`render_windows_provision`), scripts run as `.ps1` via
  `powershell -File`; no Ansible on Windows.

+16 unit tests (channel / wait / first-boot / provision); 302 total.

**Live-verified on dev45 (PVE 9.2, 2026-06-16) — first try, no iterations** (vs. W0's six).
Deployed a 1-host Windows scenario (`deploy-dev45/scenario-win-verify.yaml`: a Windows
Server 2022 host cloned from template 102, untapped): **13/13 ops COMPLETED**, including
`configure-cloud-init[winhost]` (the W1.3 Windows snippet path), `wait-guest-agent` (QGA
came up), `wait-os-ready[winhost]` (the W1.2 Windows branch cleared), and `host-ready`. This
also exercises W0's previously-skipped smoke-test path (M1's deploy half). Note:
`wait-os-ready` clears on QGA-up + a CIM probe — a slightly weaker signal than "cloudbase-init
fully specialized"; tightening it to poll the cloudbase-init completion marker is a follow-on.

**Affected docs:** `docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md` (W1 status), `CLAUDE.md`.

**Follow-on:** W2 (the AD identity ops + `role: adds` DC promotion); tighten Windows
`wait-os-ready` to a cloudbase-init-completion marker; Windows `provision-app` live-verify
(needs guest egress for choco).


## 2026-06-16 — implementation: W0 Windows imagebuild pipeline (ISO + autounattend)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

First slice of the Windows-AD identity increment (docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md) — **W0, the
Windows image-build pipeline**. `axrange image build` can now build a reproducible **Windows
Server** template from an install ISO + virtio-win, alongside the existing Linux cloud-image
path. **Implemented + 26 unit tests; live-verified on dev45 — `axrange image build
windows-server-2022` produced template VMID 102, 4/4 probes (the per-iteration log is below).**

- **`axrange/imagebuild/bake_windows.py`** (new) — the Windows analog of `bake.py`:
  `render_autounattend()` (deterministic autounattend.xml — UEFI/GPT disk layout, edition
  select by /IMAGE/NAME or /IMAGE/INDEX, virtio storage-driver injection in windowsPE, admin
  password + autologon, FirstLogonCommands baking QGA + cloudbase-init + provisioning + a
  completion marker); `windows_provision_to_commands()` (pkg→choco with a one-time bootstrap,
  pkg@version, script→base64 PowerShell; `role`/`config`/`container` a loud error);
  `windows_sysprep_command()` (/generalize /oobe /shutdown — the seal); `_WINDOWS_PROBES`
  (PowerShell, one per Windows `provides:` keyword).
- **`axrange/imagebuild/recipe.py`** — a `build.windows:` sub-block (install ISO as base_image
  + a required `virtio_iso` + edition/image_index/locale/timezone/admin_password/product_key);
  `_parse_image_source` shared by both ISOs. Linux recipes unchanged (`windows=None`).
- **`axrange/imagebuild/conformance.py`** — `run_probes(..., os_family=)` wraps probes in the
  OS shell (`sh -c` vs `powershell -Command`) + the right probe table. Linux call sites
  unchanged (default `os_family="linux"`).
- **`axrange/imagebuild/proxmox_build.py`** — Windows build-VM ops: `create_windows_vm`
  (EFI + vTPM + empty virtio-scsi disk, `--ostype win11`), `upload_iso`,
  `make_autounattend_iso` (genisoimage), `attach_windows_media`, `wait_windows_ready` (poll
  the marker over QGA), `sysprep_seal` + `wait_vm_stopped`, `detach_windows_media`.
- **`axrange/imagebuild/builder.py`** — `build_image` branches steps 2-7 on `os_family`
  (`_linux_stage_to_seal` is the prior path verbatim; `_windows_stage_to_seal` is the
  ISO/autounattend/sysprep path). Steps 1, 8-9 + the failure-unwind are shared. The Windows
  **smoke-test is skipped pending W1** (the planner's wait-os-ready is Linux-only until then —
  the §3 M1 interlock).
- **`tests/fixtures/images/{manifests,bindings/proxmox}/windows-server-2022.yaml`** (new) — the
  AD-DS-capable DC baseline (`os_family: windows`, `install_support.role: [adds, dns, iis]`, a
  Windows `build:` recipe with placeholder ISO sources). `template_vmid: 0` (a real build writes it).

**Verification:** 290 pytest tests pass (was 264; +26 in `tests/test_imagebuild_windows.py`).
The 35 Linux imagebuild tests are unchanged (the Linux path moved verbatim into
`_linux_stage_to_seal`).

**Live-verified on dev45 (PVE 9.2, 2026-06-16): `axrange image build windows-server-2022`
produced template VMID 102, 4/4 capability probes passed.** The ISOs were staged on dev45 (real
checksums wired in `deploy-dev45/images/.../windows-server-2022.yaml`) and the orchestrator
cache pre-seeded from the node, so `fetch` is a cache-hit (`genisoimage` + the node-local-ISO
shortcut both worked). Six build iterations, each a real "resolve by building" issue surfaced
via a console screendump and fixed in-code (suite stays green; the fixes carry inline
`live-verify dev45 2026-06-16` markers):

1. **UEFI "Press any key to boot from CD or DVD"** — an unattended VM has no one to press it, so
   OVMF fell through to the empty disk and Setup never started. Fix: `BuildProxmox.press_boot_key`
   sends a keypress burst (`qm monitor sendkey`) after start, **first-boot-only** (later reboots
   must boot the installed disk).
2. **autounattend invalid for `windowsPE`** — missing `publicKeyToken`/`versionScope` on
   components; `Disk` element order (DiskID/WillWipeDisk must follow the partition lists); and the
   eval ISO names its WIM images "…Evaluation (Desktop Experience)", not "…SERVERSTANDARD". Fixes:
   add the component identity attrs, reorder `Disk`, select by `/IMAGE/INDEX 2`.
3. **`DiskConfiguration` couldn't apply** — the virtio-scsi target disk was invisible because the
   storage driver never loaded: the virtio ISO mounts as `D:` in WinPE (not the assumed `E:`).
   Fixes: multi-letter `DriverPaths` (C–G) so Setup finds the driver whatever the ISO's letter;
   plus a **volume-search** for the guest-tools/QGA install (the ISO's letter differs again in the
   installed OS).
4. **autounattend invalid for `specialize`** — Shell-Setup elements must be in the schema's
   (alphabetical) order, and the **ComputerName exceeded the 15-char NetBIOS limit** (the 32-char
   build-VM name). Fixes: reorder the `specialize` + `oobeSystem` Shell-Setup components; derive a
   ≤15-char NetBIOS ComputerName (`_netbios_name`).

After those, the run completed clean: install → OOBE → FirstLogonCommands (guest-tools/QGA via the
volume search) → QGA up → 4/4 PowerShell probes (`windows` / `host:uefi` / `kernel:powershell-5plus`
/ `kernel:dotnet-clr`) → `sysprep /generalize` → `qm template` → binding `template_vmid: 102`. The
Windows smoke-test stays skipped pending W1 (the M1 interlock).

**Affected docs:** `docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md` (W0 status), `docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md` §1/§2 (Windows
no longer out of v1), `CLAUDE.md`.

**Supersedes:** the "Windows image building is explicitly out of v1" scope in
`docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md` §1/§2 (now implemented as W0).

**Follow-on:** W1 (Windows substrate — first-boot specialization, QGA-PowerShell channel,
wait-os-ready windows branch, Windows provision-app), then W2 (AD identity). The W0 live-verify
(M1) lands with W1.


## 2026-06-16 — Identity increment scoped: Windows AD via a Windows imagebuild pipeline

**Authority:** user

**What changed:**

The overlay slice's third increment — **Identity** (`docs/SLICE_OVERLAY.md` §3/§8) — is
scoped. Two load-bearing forks were the user's calls (elicited in a Claude Code scoping
session); decomposition + sequencing are Claude Code's. **Design only — not built**; the build
awaits the user's go-ahead. Full design: `docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md`.

- **Target Windows Active Directory**, not Samba-AD-on-Linux. Faithful to the AD-centric
  archetypes (`ad-domain-lab`, `purple-team-range`) and the Windows-native AD kill-chain, at
  the cost of not being live-verifiable until a Windows Server image exists.
- **Extend the imagebuild pipeline for Windows** (not a hand-built template, not design-only):
  a reproducible Windows Server template from ISO + autounattend + virtio-win + sysprep seal.
  This **reopens the door `docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md` §1/§2 explicitly closed** ("Windows image
  building is explicitly out of v1").
- **Decomposition — three stacked slices** (the AD logic is the smallest): **W0** Windows
  imagebuild → **W1** Windows substrate (first-boot specialization, QGA-PowerShell channel,
  Windows `wait-os-ready`, Windows `provision-app`) → **W2** AD identity (`role: adds` DC
  promotion + the three OP-OVL identity ops + the activity actor→host-join inference +
  archetype content). Sequenced by live-verify milestone M1/M2/M3 (`SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md` §3).
- **Channel decision:** QGA-PowerShell, **not WinRM** (WinRM hits the same isolated-SDN
  reachability wall that put `wait-os-ready` on QGA, and reuses no existing auth).
- **Key finding driving the scope:** the manifest/binding *declare* Windows (`os_family:
  windows`, `install_support.role: [adds]`, `cloud_init.supported`, `sysprep_required`) but
  **every execution path is Linux-only** — first-boot, guest channel, `wait-os-ready`,
  `role`-class install, config backend, imagebuild. And `win11-finance@v4` (today's `dc01` in
  both AD archetypes) is a Windows 11 *client* that cannot be a DC. So the increment must build
  the Windows execution substrate before the AD logic.

**Why:**
- The user prioritized archetype fidelity (real Windows AD) and the reusable, principled image
  path over the faster-to-live-verify Samba-on-Linux / hand-built-template options.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md` — new; the full scope design (W0/W1/W2, milestones, done-criteria).
- `docs/SLICE_OVERLAY.md` §3/§8 — Identity increment status now points here.
- `docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md` §1/§2 — Windows-out-of-v1 superseded for the imagebuild scope (doc-body
  edit **pending the user's go-ahead** — flagged, not yet made).
- `CLAUDE.md` — Active Threads §1 Identity status (edit **pending the user's go-ahead** — flagged).

**Supersedes:** the "Windows image building is explicitly out of v1" scope in
`docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md` §1/§2 (ratified with Q6, CHANGELOG 2026-06-01), for the imagebuild scope only.

**Links:** `docs/SLICE_WINDOWS_AD.md`; `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` §5.5; `docs/IR_SCHEMA.md` §8.1.


## 2026-06-16 — implementation: OP-OVL network sensor (deploy-network-sensor, sink-sidecar)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

Completes the **Defense** overlay increment — the network-sensor op
(`deploy-network-sensor`), built as **Option C (sink-sidecar Suricata)** per
`docs/SLICE_OVERLAY.md` §4b. Reconsidered the A/B per-scenario-sensor designs away (user
call): the sink already decapsulates the entire mirror stream onto one iface, so detection
at the capture tier needs **none** of A/B's transport-NIC / tap-rerouting plumbing. A
standing Suricata sidecar next to the sink, fed a mirror of the sink's capture iface,
inspects the whole aggregated stream. The scenario + capture plane are unchanged. Maps to
the IR's reserved `tap_from: external-mirror` mode.

- **`axrange/config_exec/render.py`** — `render_network_sensor_setup(entry, *, iface,
  rules_content)`: a `DefenseEntry` → a Suricata setup script (drop the ruleset, run
  `suricata -S <rules> -i <iface> -l /var/log/suricata -D` on the sidecar's mirror iface,
  exclusive rules; default recon/probe rules when no `config:` ruleset is given).
- **`axrange/config_exec/backend.py`** — `deploy-network-sensor`: resolves the sidecar
  vmid/iface from adapter config **at deploy time** (so `plan` stays sidecar-agnostic; a
  missing sidecar fails loudly here, not at plan), then configures Suricata over QGA.
- **`axrange/adapter_proxmox/config.py`** + `adapter.conf.example` — `SENSOR_SIDECAR_VMID`
  / `SENSOR_SIDECAR_IFACE` (the standing sidecar, like the sink — resolved, not deployed).
- **`axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`** — `_emit_overlays` emits `deploy-network-sensor` for
  `overlays.defense` entries with `observes:` (the prior "rejected" stub is gone), scoped
  `{defense, sink}` and gated on `mirror-ready[$sink]`; a sensor with no capture in the
  scenario is a clear error. `_emit_overlays` now also takes `capture_sink_id`.
- **`axrange/ops/registry.py`** — `deploy-network-sensor.after` → `mirror-ready[$sink]`
  (drops the A/B `host-ready`/`attach-gre-tunnel`/`install-ovs-mirror` predecessors);
  `resource_categories` → `("ansible-pool",)` (drops `ovs:$sensor_node` + `vm:$sensor_vmid`
  — it does no host OVS work and targets standing infra).
- **`aiscenario/stage2/scenarios/soc-detection-lab`** — a Suricata network sensor observing
  dmz+corp via `tap_from: external-mirror`, with a ruleset (`suricata_axrange.rules`) tuned
  to the red-team attack chain. (`DEF-040` warns on external-mirror — expected, "confirm the
  sink is configured".)

**Verification:** 264 pytest tests pass (was 259; +5 sensor render/backend/emission tests).
`validate`/`plan` PASS on all seven archetypes; soc plans `deploy-network-sensor[ids-suricata]`
gated on `mirror-ready` alongside the two host agents. Render is `bash -n` clean.

**Live-verified on dev45 (PVE 9.2, 2026-06-16).** Provisioned the standing sidecar (a
Suricata VM, `8001`) + the `gtcap0`→sidecar mirror (a `tc`-mirror → gretap, decapped on the
sidecar's flow-based `cap-mirror`), set `SENSOR_SIDECAR_VMID`, then deployed a 2-tapped-host
scenario with an inline recon activity + a network sensor. End-to-end proven: the activity's
ICMP + TCP-SYN traffic → tapped hosts → sink → mirrored to the sidecar → Suricata fired the
attack-chain rules (`fast.log`: `axRange ICMP ping` + `axRange TCP SYN probe` from
`10.93.83.10` → the victim's probed ports). The live run caught a **render bug (fixed):** the
setup hid Suricata launch errors and lost to the packaged `suricata.service` (which sniffs the
default iface) — the render now stops/disables the packaged unit, uses a dedicated pidfile, and
doesn't swallow errors (regression-tested).

**Affected docs:** `docs/SLICE_OVERLAY.md` (sensor design + status), `CLAUDE.md`.

**Follow-on:** Identity increment; a first-class teardown/`destroy` path (and a documented
sidecar-provisioning runbook in `sink/`).


## 2026-06-15 — implementation: OP-OVL defense host-agent (install-defense-agent)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

Second overlay increment — the **Defense** overlay's host-agent op
(`install-defense-agent`), per `docs/SLICE_OVERLAY.md` §4b. The Defense increment is
sequenced **agent-first, then live sensor feed** (user-confirmed); this lands the agent
half. A defense overlay can now enroll/configure a host EDR/agent (Wazuh, auditd, …) on
existing nodes, shipping telemetry to the SIEM. The **network-sensor** half
(`deploy-network-sensor`, the capture-coupled live feed) is the next sub-increment and
stays a clear emission error; identity stays deferred.

- **`axrange/config_exec/render.py`** — `render_defense_agent_config(entry, *, config_content)`:
  a `DefenseEntry` → a config/enable script. A small tool registry maps `tool:` → in-guest
  config destination + enable command (`wazuh-agent` → `/var/ossec/etc/ossec.conf`, `auditd`
  → `/etc/audit/rules.d`, `filebeat` → …); unknown tools get a generic dest +
  `systemctl enable`. The agent *binary* comes from an app/template — the overlay drops the
  config + enables best-effort (a not-yet-installed agent leaves the config staged, not a
  hard failure).
- **`axrange/config_exec/backend.py`** — `install-defense-agent`: render the script, write
  it into the guest, run it.
- **`axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`** — `_emit_overlays` extended: per `overlays.defense`
  entry with `installed_on:`, emit `install-defense-agent` per host (the `config:` file read
  at emit, like run-script). `observes:` (network sensors) raise a clear "next sub-increment"
  error. `_emit_overlays` now takes `scenario_dir` for the config read.
- **`axrange/ops/registry.py`** — `install-defense-agent.after` gains `provision-app[$host@*]`
  + `run-script[$host@*]` (gate on the host's apps; the agent binary is app-installed).
- **`axrange/validator/checks/defense.py`** — DEF-010 reconciled: defense host-agents are
  **script/config-class**, not `role`-class (the earlier `role` placeholder predated the
  implementation — its own docstring flagged the follow-up). The rule now checks the image
  supports `script`, which is what `install-defense-agent` actually needs.
- **`aiscenario/stage2/scenarios/soc-detection-lab`** — first Defense content: a Wazuh host
  agent on the monitored Linux endpoints (`web`, `sensor`) shipping to the SIEM (a
  `wazuh_agent.conf` pointing at `10.50.30.20`).

**Verification:** 259 pytest tests pass (was 252; +7 defense tests — render/backend/emission/
rejection). `validate`/`plan` PASS on the overlay fixture and soc-detection-lab (2
`install-defense-agent` ops gated on host-ready + the host's apps). All seven archetypes
still validate.

**Live-verified on dev45 (PVE 9.2, 2026-06-15)** alongside the data overlay (one deploy,
`deploy-dev45/scenario-overlay2-verify.yaml`). QGA confirmed: the `auditd` config landed at
`/etc/audit/rules.d/axrange.rules` with the exact ruleset, and the metadata env file
(`TOOL=auditd`, `RULESET=…`, `CONFIG_DEST`) was written. The agent binary wasn't installed
(no app on the host), so the best-effort enable correctly **no-op'd without failing** — the
config is staged for when an app provides the binary, exactly the designed behavior.

**Affected docs:** `docs/SLICE_OVERLAY.md` (Defense agent implemented + live-verified),
`CLAUDE.md`.

**Follow-on:** `deploy-network-sensor` with the live feed (Option L — the SOC value); then
identity. Live-verify the defense agent alongside the sensor.


## 2026-06-15 — implementation: OP-OVL data overlay engine (first overlay increment)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

First **overlay** increment — the OP-OVL **data** overlay (`seed-dataset`), per the new
`docs/SLICE_OVERLAY.md` design. Overlays can now materialize synthetic datasets onto a
deployed range (crown-jewel PII, DFIR evidence, lateral loot). This is the data-first
slice of the 3-increment plan (data → defense → identity); defense + identity stay a
clear deferred error.

Design (resolved in `docs/SLICE_OVERLAY.md`, mirroring the activity slice): an archetype
survey confirmed **overlays configure *existing* nodes, never add them** — every overlay
target across the seven archetypes already exists in `infra.nodes`. So overlays are a
separate IR block emitting config-class ops over the QGA channel after `host-ready`,
siblings of apps/activity. The IR `overlays.{identity,data,defense}` surface and the six
OP-OVL ops were already ratified + validated; only emission was missing.

- **`axrange/config_exec/render.py`** — `render_dataset_seeder(entry, *, seed)`: a
  `DataEntry` → a deterministic in-guest `python3` seeder that writes `synthetic_size` of
  seeded bytes (header recording id/classification/crown_jewel) to `path`. Seed = scenario
  seed folded with the data id via `zlib.crc32` (process-independent), so `reproducible:
  true` + same seed → byte-identical output. `parse_size()` for the size grammar; a 100-MiB
  guard. `DatasetRenderError` for authored `content:`/`from:` (deferred) and bad shapes.
- **`axrange/config_exec/backend.py`** — `seed-dataset`: render the seeder, `mkdir` its
  parent, write it, check `python3` is present (baked by imagebuild; loud error if absent,
  like OP-APP's ansible check), run it. Idempotent.
- **`axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`** — `_emit_overlays` replaces the overlays reject;
  emits `seed-dataset` per `overlays.data` entry (host must resolve to a defined node; path
  required), threading the entry + scenario seed as data (backend renders). identity/defense
  overlays raise a clear "follow-on" error.
- **`axrange/ops/registry.py`** — `seed-dataset.after` gains `provision-app[$host@*]` +
  `run-script[$host@*]` (the OP-ACT star-tail fix): a dataset may land in a dir an app
  creates, so it gates on the host's apps.
- **`aiscenario/stage2/scenarios/purple-team-range`** — first real overlay content: a
  crown-jewel synthetic-PII dataset on `app-srv` (the exfil objective) + corp docs on
  `file-srv`, deterministic from a new `scenario.seed`.

**Verification:** 252 pytest tests pass (was 238; +14 overlay tests — render determinism,
backend seed, planner emission/rejection). `validate`/`plan` PASS on the overlay fixture
and on purple-team-range (both `seed-dataset` ops gated on host-ready + the host's apps).
All seven archetypes still validate. The seeder runs locally producing a correctly-sized,
byte-reproducible file.

**Live-verified on dev45 (PVE 9.2, 2026-06-15).** A minimal 1-host scenario
(`deploy-dev45/scenario-overlay-verify.yaml`, no taps/egress) deployed end-to-end;
`seed-dataset` ran after `host-ready` and the baked `python3` seeder materialized the file
in-guest. QGA confirmed: the file exists at `path`, is exactly the requested size (16384 B =
16Ki), carries the classification header, and its **SHA256 matches the locally-rendered seeder
byte-for-byte** — determinism/reproducibility proven on the cluster (done-criterion #5). No
bug surfaced (the OP-ACT mkdir-parent fix was already applied to `seed-dataset`).

**Affected docs:** `docs/SLICE_OVERLAY.md` (status → data increment implemented),
`CLAUDE.md` (test count, overlay status).

**Follow-on:** live-verify on dev45; the Defense increment (`install-defense-agent` +
`deploy-network-sensor` for soc-detection-lab); Identity (needs `role: adds` DC
promotion); `overlay-ready` → OP-ACT dependency; authored `content:` datasets (CTF flags).


## 2026-06-12 — implementation: OP-ACT inline-steps activity engine (first activity slice)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

The first **activity** slice — the OP-ACT inline-steps engine (docs/SLICE_ACTIVITY.md,
Option A) — is implemented + unit-tested (live-verify pending). Activities now
**make traffic happen** in a deployed range; previously the stack stood up infra +
apps but nothing generated load. Scope: `inline.steps` only, rendered to a shell
runner and delivered over the existing QGA config channel; local/external actors;
**one-shot** (no `schedule:` windows). Library `behavior:`/`overrides:` and
domain-actor activities stay a clear deferred error (not a silent skip).

- **`axrange/config_exec/render.py`** — `render_activity_runner()` turns a closed
  step vocabulary (`tool: nmap|curl|hydra|nc|ssh` + a `{run: <shell>}` escape) into
  one self-contained bash runner: each step brace-grouped so its log redirect +
  nonzero-exit handler wrap the whole (possibly operator-laden) command; no
  `set -e`/`set -u` (steps are best-effort, recon tools exit nonzero); a completion
  marker `touch`ed last. `activity_paths()` gives deterministic in-guest paths
  shared by render + backend.
- **`axrange/config_exec/backend.py`** — three new ConfigBackend ops:
  `deploy-behavior-agent` (render + write the runner, idempotent), `start-activity`
  (launch it detached via `setsid`, one-shot — re-running re-fires, the point of
  attack replay), `wait-actor-ready` (poll the completion marker over QGA until
  present or timeout).
- **`axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`** — the `layers.activity` reject is replaced by
  `_emit_activity`, emitting the trio per activity, threading the inline steps as
  data (backend renders — keeps the planner free of a `config_exec` import, same as
  provision-app).
- **`axrange/ops/registry.py`** — two catalog corrections (both latent: OP-ACT was
  never emitted before). (1) The cross-op `after:` patterns now use the **star-tail**
  form — `deploy-behavior-agent[$act@*]`, `start-activity[$act@*]`,
  `provision-app[$host@*]`, `run-script[$host@*]` — because the pass-3 resolver
  matches on the *full* scope-value set, so single-scope `[$act]`/`[$target_app]`
  never resolved against multi-scope ops. This also gates the activity on its host's
  app installs (a recon step waits for nmap). (2) `wait-actor-ready` is now
  **config-class** so the executor routes its marker-poll to the ConfigBackend (the
  executor calls `backend.run()` for wait ops too); it holds only `vm:$vmid`, never
  `ansible-pool`.
- **Two op-registry invariants relaxed** to admit the first config-class wait op:
  waits may now be `idempotency_class ∈ {None, config}` (config = in-guest poll over
  QGA), and the ansible-pool requirement applies to config-class **actions** only —
  a polling wait must *not* hold the throughput slot for its whole timeout.
- **`aiscenario/stage2/scenarios/soc-detection-lab`** — gains a one-shot inline
  attack chain on the attacker host (nmap recon of the DMZ web victim → HTTP probe →
  corp-segment sweep across the router → lateral probe of the finance workstation),
  using tools the `offensive` app installs; traffic crosses the tapped dmz/corp hosts
  so it lands in the capture sink. The richer library-driven chain (Atomic Red Team,
  exploit + ransomware/exfil) remains a follow-on.

**Verification:** 238 pytest tests pass (was 218; +20 activity tests covering render,
backend, and planner emission). `axrange validate|plan` PASS on the soc scenario with
the activity (DAG confirmed: `deploy-behavior-agent` gates on host-ready +
provision-app + run-script; `start-activity` gates on `mirror-ready`; all three route
to the ConfigBackend). All seven Stage-2 archetypes still validate. The rendered
runner passes `bash -n` and runs to completion (marker created, missing tools logged
but non-fatal).

**Live-verified on dev45 (PVE 9.2, 2026-06-12).** A minimal egress-free 2-host Debian
scenario (`deploy-dev45/scenario-activity-verify.yaml`, baked-in tools only) deployed
end-to-end: all infra green, then the activity trio ran in order
(`deploy-behavior-agent` → `start-activity` (gated on `mirror-ready`) → `wait-actor-ready`).
QGA confirmed the runner executed in-guest (the activity log shows the ping + `/dev/tcp`
probe steps; the completion marker was written). **The generated traffic was captured at
the sink**: the newest pcap on `axr-sink` holds the ICMP echo req/reply between attacker
(`10.90.80.10`) and victim (`10.90.80.11`) and the TCP SYNs to the four probed ports
(22/80/443/8080) — the full OP-ACT ↔ capture loop, done-criterion #2.

**Live-verify caught one bug (fixed):** `deploy-behavior-agent` `mkdir`'d the *provision*
dir but wrote the runner under the *activity* dir, so the first write failed ENOENT on a
fresh guest. The backend now creates the runner's parent dir; the test fake-QGA now
rejects writes into an un-`mkdir`'d dir so this can't regress.

**Affected docs:** `docs/SLICE_ACTIVITY.md` (status → implemented + live-verified),
`docs/OP_CATALOG.md` §5.6 (after-pattern corrections + config-class wait), `CLAUDE.md`
(test count, activity engine status).

**Follow-on:** behavior library (Option B); domain-actor activities (needs the identity
overlay); `schedule:` windows; the richer soc-detection-lab chain over a router/egress.


## 2026-06-11 — Project-wide scope-boundary gates removed; user routes work case-by-case

**Authority:** user

**What changed:**
- The "What NOT to Do" scope-boundaries that have lived in `CLAUDE.md` (and as
  status markers in `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md`) are **removed across the project**.
  The doc-enforced gates that flagged specific work as "deferred / out of v1 scope /
  not yet authorized" are retired in favor of user-directed routing.
- Specifically retired as blockers (live state, not historical):
  - Overlays (`OP-OVL` — identity / data / defense)
  - Activity (`OP-ACT`)
  - Install kinds: `config`, `role`, `container`
  - Canvas (Q1) — still not in active work, but no longer doc-flagged as "out of v1"
  - AI core (Q8) — same
  - Host-side telemetry (Q9), multi-sink HA (Q10), operator UI beyond CLI (Q11),
    scenario library design (Q12) — all open and user-directed
  - Per-rule firewall-policy IR fields (router-slice Phase 2 follow-on)
  - Parallel intra-deploy executor + throughput semaphores (`docs/PLANNER.md` §8)
  - Plan-diff / re-planning (`docs/PLANNER.md` §9)
  - Escalate-walk-back (`docs/PLANNER.md` §4.4)
  - Windows image building (Q6 v1 was Linux-only — now user-directed)
- **Routing between Claude (chat) and Claude Code** is the user's call per work item,
  not a doc-enforced gate. The previous default ("design originates in chat,
  implementation lives in Claude Code") is now a soft preference rather than a hard
  rule. Either surface can take on either kind of work when the user routes it there.
- `CHANGELOG.md` Authority markers stay as they are — entries are still tagged
  `design (chat session)` / `implementation (Claude Code)` / `user` / `retrofit` for
  traceability. The change is to the *routing policy*, not the *attribution*.

**Why:**
- The project has progressed past the point where doc-enforced scope gates earn
  their keep. Three slices live-verified on two clusters (`pxtwin1` PVE 8.4 and
  `dev45` PVE 9.2); 218 tests; 33 ops; 87 catalog rules; the architecture has held
  up under sustained implementation pressure. The guardrails were appropriate when
  everything was conceptual; less appropriate now.
- Empirically the gates were already being routed-around as decisions outpaced the
  cadence of formal chat ratification (sidecar shipped under PROPOSED status; the
  2026-06-03 scope-boundary on overlays/activity/install-kinds sat unmoved for ~8
  days). Recording the new policy explicitly is honest about how work is already
  being routed.
- This is also a delegation: per-scenario overlay (identity/data/defense) and
  activity work for the `aiscenario/stage2/scenarios/` archetypes can land
  through Claude Code without a chat round-trip. Chat remains available for any
  work the user routes there, including foundational schema mechanics if the user
  decides the first archetype's overlay needs that level of review.

**Affected docs:**
- `CLAUDE.md` — "What NOT to Do" section removed; "Do not implement against"
  and "Scope note" paragraphs in Immediate Next Actions removed; sidecar surfaced as
  a now-ratified component; routing language softened.
- `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` — status headers updated: "DEFERRED — out of v1 scope"
  replaced with "open — user-directed" where the gate was the blocker.
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ai_scenario_sidecar.md` — separately ratified (see adjacent entry).

**Supersedes:** the 2026-06-03 "scope note" carried in `CLAUDE.md` (overlays /
activity / install kinds boundary deferred); the v1 "Linux-only" gate in Q6 (no
longer a *blocker*, but Q6's pipeline is still Linux-only as built — see
`docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md`). Historical CHANGELOG entries with their own scope decisions
are **not** rewritten; they record the decisions of their time. The supersession is
only of the **live forward-looking gates** in CLAUDE.md / OPEN_QUESTIONS.md.

**Links:** chat-20260611 (this session).

## 2026-06-11 — AI scenario sidecar ratified

**Authority:** user

**What changed:**
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ai_scenario_sidecar.md` flipped from **PROPOSED** to
  **RATIFIED**. The sidecar — Stage 1 (OSS local ingestion / corpus build) +
  Stage 2 (Claude-Code-driven scenario library) — is an authorized v1 component.
- The sidecar lives at top-level `aiscenario/` on `wip/ai-scenario-sidecar`.
  Five Stage-2 archetype scenarios already exist under `aiscenario/stage2/scenarios/`:
  `soc-detection-lab`, `ad-domain-lab`, `ctf-range`, `ot-ics-range`, `dfir-hunt-lab`.
  All five validate, plan, and deploy through the standard `axrange` pipeline.
- **Coupling rule** (unchanged from the brief): one-directional. Stage 2 may import
  axRange's `validator` / `planner` / op catalog; axRange must not import anything
  from `aiscenario/`. The sidecar is a *consumer* of axRange, never the other way.
- Master-merge timing is **not** decided by this entry; the WIP-branch staging stays
  the user's call. Ratification authorizes the package on the WIP branch and
  acknowledges the live coupling without committing the user to a merge gate.
- `CLAUDE.md` may now describe the sidecar as a top-level component. The earlier
  (same-session) "stay silent" framing — appropriate while the brief was PROPOSED —
  is retired.

**Why:**
- The brief was drafted 2026-06-09. Stage 1 and Stage 2 have been shipping on this
  branch since; the 2026-06-11 DFIR archetype is the most recent. Code has clearly
  outrun the brief's "Not yet authorized for implementation" caveat. Ratifying
  closes the doc/code gap honestly rather than pretending implementation was
  ahead of a still-pending decision.
- The Stage-2 archetypes are the concrete input set for the per-scenario overlays
  + activity work delegated to Claude Code in the adjacent entry. Without
  ratification, that delegation rests on an implicit gate.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_ai_scenario_sidecar.md` — status header flipped to RATIFIED;
  authority changed from "design (Claude Code drafting) — pending a chat-session
  ratification pass" to "user (ratification, 2026-06-11)".
- `CLAUDE.md` — sidecar surfaced as a top-level component (concise; the brief
  remains the authoritative spec).

**Supersedes:** the brief's own 2026-06-09 status line ("🔵 PROPOSED — draft for
chat ratification. Not yet authorized for implementation. No CLAUDE.md /
ARCHITECTURE scope change until ratified.") and the same-session 2026-06-11
"stay silent on the sidecar in CLAUDE.md" framing.

## 2026-06-10 — VyOS router unblocked: `vyos-ssh-set` config delivery (resolves the cloud-init blocker)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)
**Branch:** `wip/ai-scenario-sidecar`.

**What changed:**
- Replaced the dead VyOS **cloud-init** config path with **`vyos-ssh-set`** — flat `set`
  commands applied over SSH through VyOS's vbash `script-template` (the approach the sibling
  `axDigitalTwin` project uses; the generic ISO has **sshd** even though it lacks cloud-init/QGA).
  `op_configure_router` now branches on `router_os`: Debian → nftables over QGA (unchanged);
  VyOS → `_configure_vyos_router_ssh` (`adapter_proxmox/rest.py`).
- **Delivery mechanism** (`adapter_proxmox/ssh.py`): `discover_guest_ip_by_mac` resolves the
  router's WAN/mgmt IP from its net0 MAC via `arp-scan` on the PVE host (VyOS has no QGA to ask;
  the orchestrator is routed off the uplink L2, the host is on it); `run_ssh_guest` SSHes from the
  orchestrator straight to the router and pipes the `set` script to `vbash -s`. Errors are detected
  by **stderr markers**, not exit code — VyOS `commit` returns non-zero on an idempotent "No
  configuration changes" re-run.
- **Template prep** (dev45 template **9351**, replaces 9302): clean `eth0` → `address dhcp` (WAN/mgmt
  bootstrap so the router is SSH-reachable on a DHCP lease right after boot) + `service ssh` + the
  operator pubkey baked into the `vyos` user; **`hw-id` stripped from `config.boot` as a raw edit
  with no subsequent commit** (a `commit` re-pins hw-id) so clones enumerate NICs deterministically
  by PCI/bus order — verified `netN → ethN` (WAN=net0=eth0; LAN net i = eth(i+1)).
- **Emission** (`planner/pass1_emit.py`): VyOS routers attach the WAN on **net0/eth0** (LANs shift
  to net1..); **no `configure-cloud-init`** op is emitted for VyOS; `configure-router` carries
  `router_os=vyos` + ordered `lan_ifaces` (eth→address) + nameservers + static routes. The binding
  sets `cloud_init.supported=false`.
- **Ordering fix** (`ops/registry.py`): the **`host-ready` barrier now lists `start-vm[$host]` as a
  predecessor**. Agent nodes already chained start-vm→wait-guest-agent→wait-os-ready→host-ready, but
  `agent:false` nodes skip the `wait-*` ops, leaving the barrier with **no predecessors** — so it
  (and `configure-router` after it) fired before the VM ever started. Caught on the first VyOS
  deploy (configure-router ran against a not-yet-started router and hung on IP discovery).

**Live-verified on dev45 (`vyos-router-test`, 2-segment VLAN-zone scenario):** `--fresh` deploy
**COMPLETED 36/36**; VyOS router applied eth0=WAN-dhcp / eth1=10.121.0.1 / eth2=10.121.1.1 +
masquerade out eth0 + `ip_forward=1`; host **internet egress via NAT** (h1→8.8.8.8 0% loss) and
**cross-segment routing** (h1→h2, one router hop) both pass; tapped-host frames captured
decapsulated at the sink (`gtcap0`). 218 tests pass (+1 emission, +2 adapter).

**Supersedes:** the 2026-06-10 "blocked on the generic ISO's lack of cloud-init" entry below — the
blocker is resolved without a different image: SSH (not cloud-init/QGA) is the config channel, and
the `hw-id`-strip handles the non-deterministic NIC naming. The Debian/nftables router remains a
supported alternative; VyOS is now a working peer via the same `kind: router` abstraction.

**Affected:** `axrange/adapter_proxmox/rest.py`, `axrange/adapter_proxmox/ssh.py`,
`axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py`, `axrange/ops/registry.py`,
`tests/fixtures/images/bindings/proxmox/vyos.yaml` (→ template 9351, cloud_init false),
`tests/test_planner_dag.py`, `tests/test_adapter_proxmox.py`, `docs/SLICE_ROUTER.md`.

**Prerequisite:** `arp-scan` on the PVE host (documented alongside openvswitch).

---

## 2026-06-10 — VyOS fold-in: code path built; blocked on the generic ISO's lack of cloud-init

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)
**Branch:** `wip/ai-scenario-sidecar`.

**What changed:**
- Implemented the **VyOS router config path**: `op_configure_cloud_init` renders
  `#cloud-config vyos_config_commands` (per-NIC `set interfaces ethernet ethN address`, WAN
  `dhcp`, `set nat source rule … masquerade`) and attaches it via `cicustom`; emission tags a
  router's `router_os` and **skips the QGA ops** (`wait-guest-agent`/`wait-os-ready`/
  `configure-router`) for `agent:false` images (VyOS). Built a VyOS template (9302) on dev45 by
  **automating `install image` over the serial console**; pointed the `vyos` binding at it.

**Blocker (live-verify, dev45):** the VyOS **generic ISO ships no cloud-init and no
qemu-guest-agent** — so there is **no remote-config channel**. A deployed VyOS router clones +
boots but never receives its interfaces/NAT (verified: `dpkg`/`cloud-init`/`qemu-ga` all absent;
the PVE `cidata` drive is present but unread), so it doesn't route. Secondary: VyOS persists
`hw-id` interface bindings → clones enumerate `eth1/2/3` non-deterministically.

**Resolution path (not done):** a **cloud-init-capable VyOS image** (a `vyos-build` *cloud*
flavor, or an LTS/subscription cloud image) + clear `hw-id` before templatizing. The code path is
ready for such an image. **The Debian/nftables router (Phase 2, 2026-06-10) remains the verified,
production-grade working router**; VyOS is a drop-in once a suitable image exists.

**Affected:** `axrange/adapter_proxmox/rest.py` (`_configure_vyos_router` + cloud-init branch),
`axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py` (router_os tag, skip QGA ops for `agent:false`),
`tests/fixtures/images/bindings/proxmox/vyos.yaml` (→ template 9302), `docs/SLICE_ROUTER.md`.
215 tests pass.


## 2026-06-10 — Router slice Phase 2: router node (egress NAT + inter-segment routing)

**Authority:** user (ratified) + implementation (Claude Code)
**Branch:** `wip/ai-scenario-sidecar` (not yet merged to `master`).

**What changed:**
- **`kind: router` nodes are now deployable** (the planner's host-only gate is lifted). A
  router emits the normal host-ops (clone/hw/cloud-init/attach/start/wait) **multi-homed**
  across its LAN segments, **plus** an adapter-injected **WAN NIC on the uplink bridge**
  (`vmbr0`, DHCP) and a new **`OP-HOST-090 configure-router`** op. Catalog now 33 ops
  (28 action/wait + 5 barriers).
- **`configure-router`** (REST/QGA, image-agnostic) enables IPv4 forwarding and masquerades
  **LAN→WAN** in-guest, so the router's segments get **internet/apt egress + inter-segment
  routing**. Implemented on the **Debian** template (nftables; **VyOS-swappable** — VyOS isn't
  on the cluster and its binding has `agent: false`, so this reuses the proven QGA path).
- **`op_configure_cloud_init` generalized to multi-homed** (`ipconfig0..N`, incl. a DHCP WAN).
  Single-homed hosts are unchanged. A router's LAN NICs take **no gateway** (it *is* the
  gateway; its default route comes from the WAN).
- **NET-080 relaxed** from "router needs ≥2 nets" to **≥1** — a 1-LAN router is a valid
  egress/NAT gateway (its WAN is the other side); 2+ nets = inter-segment router.
- **All four Stage-2 archetypes now use a router** (`fw`/`gw`/`conduit`) and dropped the inert
  `egress: true` — they get **real VLAN-zone egress** (which Phase-1 SDN-SNAT couldn't do).

**Why:**
- Phase-1 SDN-SNAT egress doesn't work on VLAN zones (PVE limitation, CHANGELOG 2026-06-09),
  and all archetypes are VLAN-zoned. A router VM provides egress NAT **and** the inter-segment
  segmentation chokepoint the corpus calls for (firewall/conduit).

**Live-verify on dev45 (PVE 9.2):** a 2-segment VLAN router scenario deployed (39/39 ops). The
router came up multi-homed (`eth0/eth1` LAN, `eth2` WAN DHCP `10.80.72.49`). A VLAN-zone host
**reached the internet via the router's NAT** (`ping 8.8.8.8` 0% loss, 23ms) and **another host
across the other segment** (`ping` 0% loss) — and the **sink captured both** the inter-segment
and the egress (pre-NAT) traffic. Egress + segmentation + capture all confirmed.

**Follow-on (not built):** per-rule firewall policy (default-deny conduit / OT air-gap) needs an
IR firewall-rule extension — the router ships a permissive default forward policy for now.
Provision-app ordering vs. router-ready (apt-on-host should wait for `configure-router`) is a
small emission refinement.

**Affected:** `axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py` (router emission, WAN, multi-homed cloud-init),
`axrange/ops/registry.py` (OP-HOST-090 + count), `axrange/adapter_proxmox/rest.py`
(`op_configure_router`, multi-homed `configure-cloud-init`) + `adapter.py` (routing),
`axrange/validator/{catalog,checks/network}.py` (NET-080), the 4 archetypes,
`docs/{SLICE_ROUTER,OP_CATALOG}.md`, tests (215 pass).


## 2026-06-09 — Router slice Phase 1: SDN subnet + SNAT egress (ratified + implemented)

**Authority:** user (ratified) + implementation (Claude Code)
**Branch:** `wip/ai-scenario-sidecar` (not yet merged to `master`).

**What changed:**
- New op **`OP-NET-050 create-subnet`** (catalog now 32 ops: 27 action/wait + 5 barriers). Emits
  a PVE SDN subnet on a network's vnet with the network's `gateway` + **source-NAT (SNAT)** —
  giving the network a real L3 gateway and internet/apt egress, committed by the existing
  cluster-wide `apply-sdn-config` (which now also gates on `create-subnet[*]`).
- New IR field **`network.egress: bool`** (default off). The planner emits `create-subnet`
  **only** for networks with `egress: true` **and** a `gateway` — so every existing scenario is
  unchanged (no egress, no new op, no DAG change).
- Adapter `op_create_subnet` (REST, Model C: POST is idempotent / "already exists" tolerated);
  routed via `_REST_OPS`.

**Why:**
- All four Stage-2 archetypes (`aiscenario/stage2/`) validate but couldn't fully deploy:
  isolated SDN vnets have no internet egress for `provision-app` package installs. Phase 1
  fixes that SDN-natively (no router VM), the highest-leverage / lowest-risk part of the router
  slice (`docs/SLICE_ROUTER.md`). Phase 2 (a VyOS router node for inter-segment routing +
  firewall) remains proposed, not authorized.

**Capture interaction (the gating requirement):** the subnet/SNAT is L3 on the vnet bridge and
is applied **before** VMs start (subnet → apply-sdn-config → network-ready → VM attach/start),
so it does not touch the per-tap `tc` mirror (L2, on the VM tap) or the GRE→sink transport (over
the host's `vmbr0`). Live-verify on dev45 confirms capture-to-sink still works with egress on.

**Live-verify finding (dev45, 2026-06-09) — SNAT egress is SIMPLE-zone only.** On a Simple zone,
PVE materializes the subnet gateway on the vnet bridge (`10.99.0.1/24`) + a SNAT rule → a host
reaches the internet (`ping 8.8.8.8` 0% loss via SNAT), and the sink captures both east-west and
the (pre-SNAT) egress traffic. On a **VLAN zone**, PVE materializes **neither** gateway nor SNAT
(VLAN zones reject the `exitnodes` property and create no gateway IP / masquerade rule). So
`create-subnet` on a VLAN zone creates the subnet definition but yields **no functional egress**.
All four Stage-2 archetypes are VLAN-zoned, so their `egress: true` is declared-but-inert until
**Phase 2 (VyOS router)** routes/NATs for them — or they run as Simple zones. The egress wiring
+ this caveat are recorded in `aiscenario/stage2/scenarios/README.md` and `docs/SLICE_ROUTER.md`.

**Affected:** `axrange/ops/registry.py` (OP-NET-050 + count 26→27), `axrange/ir/schema.py`
(`Network.egress`), `axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py` (emit), `axrange/adapter_proxmox/rest.py`
+ `adapter.py` (op + routing), `docs/{SLICE_ROUTER,OP_CATALOG,IR_SCHEMA}.md`, tests
(+3; 215 pass).

was returned to manual-only. The current operative policy is the "Git Policy" section in

## 2026-06-09 — implementation: greenfield end-to-end bring-up on dev45 (PVE 9.2) + four fixes

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

First full end-to-end verification on a **second, greenfield cluster** (`dev45`,
**PVE 9.2.2 / kernel 7.0** — the prior verification was `pxtwin1`, PVE 8.4 / kernel 6.x).
`axrange image build` → binding update → `validate`/`plan`/`deploy` of the VLAN slice
fixture → east-west ping → **pcaps at a real sink**, all green. The build pipeline,
SDN-native infra, the per-tap `tc`/gretap mirror (Option 1), and a `type gretap external`
flow-based sink all worked unmodified on PVE 9. Four issues surfaced and were fixed:

- **#1 — `adapter.conf` inline comments (`config.py`).** `_read_conf_file` did not strip
  inline `# comment` text, so `BUILD_STORAGE=axrpool   # note` yielded the value
  `axrpool   # note` and broke `qm disk import`. Added `_strip_value()`: drops an inline
  comment only after whitespace, keeps a `#` that is part of the value, takes quoted
  values verbatim. (Committed `c031298`.)
- **#2 — build pipeline left a stale `cicustom` on the sealed template.** `set_cloudinit`
  injects a `cicustom` build snippet + `ipconfig0`; these survived into the template, so
  every clone (incl. manual `qm clone`) inherited the build's hostname/user/keys. Deployed
  scenario VMs were unaffected (the deploy adapter sets its own per-VM `cicustom`), but a
  pipeline template must be as clean as a hand-built one. Seal now calls
  `BuildProxmox.clear_build_cloudinit()` (`qm set --delete cicustom,ipconfig0` + snippet-file
  removal) before `templatize`.
- **#3 — sink docs used `gretap0` for the flow-based capture device.** The `ip_gre` module
  auto-creates non-flow-based fallback devices `gre0`/`gretap0` on load, so
  `ip link add gretap0 type gretap external` fails "File exists". Both READMEs now use a
  distinct name (`gtcap0`/`cap-gre`) and document why. (Committed `c031298`.)
- **#4 — image-build smoke-test leaked SDN objects.** `_smoke_teardown` deleted
  `bridge_name_for_net("smoke-net")` (`vbr_smoke_net`), a **pre-SDN-native** name that no
  longer exists; the SDN-native deploy (the smoke scenario uses `vlan: 4000` → VLAN zone)
  actually creates an SDN zone + vnet + `underlay_bridge_for_plan` OVS bridge, all of which
  leaked. Teardown now removes the vnet + zone (new `BuildProxmox.delete_sdn()` + cluster
  apply) and the underlay bridge.

**Why:**
- Validates the v1 stack on PVE 9 (the version drift was the main risk for this host).
- #1/#2 are silent-failure or latent-correctness traps; #4 leaked cluster state on every build.

**Affected code/docs:**
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/config.py` — `_strip_value()` (#1).
- `axrange/imagebuild/proxmox_build.py` — `clear_build_cloudinit()` (#2), `delete_sdn()` (#4).
- `axrange/imagebuild/builder.py` — seal calls `clear_build_cloudinit` before templatize (#2).
- `axrange/imagebuild/conformance.py` — `_smoke_teardown` rewritten SDN-native (#4).
- `docs/sink/README.md`, `sink/README.md` — gretap device naming (#3).
- `tests/test_imagebuild.py` — `FakeBuildProxmox` gains `clear_build_cloudinit`/`delete_sdn`;
  +2 regression assertions/tests. `scripts/probe_host.py` — read-only host inventory tool.
- **207 pytest tests pass.** Live remnants of #2/#4 on `dev45` (template 620 cicustom; the
  leaked `axr38f7f`/`vn52aac7`/`vbr_38f7f9`) were cleaned by hand.

**Note:** `dev45` setup not in code — OVS install, `snippets` on `local`, the `axrpool`
raidz1 pool, template 620, and sink VM 8000 are operator-side cluster state.

**Follow-up note (redeploy teardown, 2026-06-09) — OVS-bridge cleanup gap.** Removing a
scenario OVS bridge requires deleting it from the **node network config**
(`DELETE /nodes/<node>/network/<bridge>` then `PUT /nodes/<node>/network` to apply) — NOT
just `ovs-vsctl del-br`. The `ovs-vsctl` form clears only live OVS datapath state; the
bridge stays defined in `/etc/network/interfaces`, so the next network reload (e.g. an SDN
apply / `reloadnetworkall`) silently **recreates** it. Observed tearing down the slice to
redeploy: a manual `ovs-vsctl del-br` was undone by the subsequent `pvesh set /cluster/sdn`.
The image-build `BuildProxmox.delete_bridge()` is already correct here (it issues the REST
`DELETE /network` first); only its `ovs-vsctl` *fallback* is live-only and would leave node
config behind if the REST path failed. Takeaway: any future teardown/`axrange destroy`
verb must delete bridges via the node-network API, and manual teardown should use
`pvesh delete /nodes/<node>/network/<bridge>` + apply.
