## 2026-06-04 — implementation: OP-MIR ops re-mechanized to per-tap `tc` / gretap netdev (Option 1)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

Implements the 2026-06-04 Option 1 design (entry below): the two OP-MIR ops in `axrange/adapter_proxmox/ssh.py` are rewritten from `ovs-vsctl` port-mirroring to a per-tap Linux `tc` mirror feeding a keyed gretap netdev. Op ids/names kept stable (`attach-gre-tunnel` / `install-ovs-mirror`); no planner, IR, validator, or lock changes (item B's per-(bridge, sink) keying carries over unchanged — only the tunnel mechanism is re-pointed). Unit-tested **and live-verified end-to-end on `pxtwin1`** (gates below).

- **`attach-gre-tunnel` (OP-MIR-010)** now creates the keyed gretap **netdev** at deploy time instead of an OVS GRE port:
  `ip link add <dev> type gretap remote <sink_transport_ip> key <k>` then `ip link set <dev> mtu 1500 up`, where `<dev>` = `gre_port_for_bridge_sink(bridge, sink)` and `<k>` = `gre_key_for_bridge_sink(bridge, sink)` (the existing per-(bridge, sink) naming, reused). Model C: adopt an existing netdev (`ip -o link show`) rather than tear down a live tunnel; MTU+up re-asserted idempotently so a partial create heals on retry.
- **`install-ovs-mirror` (OP-MIR-020)** writes a post-start/pre-stop hook that installs the per-tap mirror with `tc`:
  `tc qdisc add dev tap<vmid>i0 clsact`, then `matchall action mirred egress mirror dev <gretap>` on **both** the `ingress` and `egress` hooks; pre-stop deletes the clsact qdisc. The hook is idempotent (drops any prior clsact first) and **fails loud** (`tc filter show … | grep -q mirred` under `set -e`) so a missing mirror surfaces in the Proxmox task log instead of capturing nothing silently. The in-band re-deploy path (VM already running → hook won't fire) applies the same `tc` rules directly.
- **Gretap lives at deploy time, not in the hook (implementation split).** The hook installs only the tap-side `tc` rules and points at the pre-created gretap. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §3 (which the design pass illustrated with gretap-create *in* the hook) is corrected to match, with a **node-reboot caveat**: an `ip link` gretap netdev does not persist across a Proxmox node reboot (the retired OVS GRE port did, via OVSDB); a resume-safe `axrange deploy` re-run re-creates it. A boot-time recreate unit is a possible follow-up.
- **`create-ovs-bridge` left unchanged (reconciliation decision).** Capture no longer needs OVS, but a VLAN zone still needs *a* backing bridge and the OVS underlay is already live-verified working (25/25, 2026-06-03); changing it to a Linux bridge would be an out-of-scope infra change needing its own live-verify. The op keeps creating an OVS underlay for VLAN zones (Simple zones already use the PVE-materialized Linux vnet bridge). The "scenario bridge MUST be OVS" invariant is retired regardless (capture is tap-side now).
- **Doc reconciliation (prompt §3 item 2).** `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` §5.1 OP-NET YAML reconciled to the shipped registry: `create-ovs-bridge` `after: []` (runs first); `create-sdn-zone` `after: [create-ovs-bridge[*]]` + `bridge_name` input; `create-vnet` drops `bridge_name`. The "reconciliation pending" flag is cleared. OP-MIR-010/020 registry descriptions + the start-vm description updated to tc/gretap.
- **Liveness gate (prompt §3 item 3) — partially adopted, full version deferred.** The cheap in-hook self-check (above) is in. The heavier `mirror-ready` barrier liveness gate (synthetic probe + sink RX-counter delta) is **deferred** — it expands v1 scope and the prompt said to confirm first; recorded as a follow-up.

**Why:**

- Realizes the design decision so capture actually works under SDN-native (the prompt's authorized next build).
- The implementation split (gretap at deploy time, tc in the hook) keeps the change `ssh.py`-only and avoids touching item B's planner emission, at the cost of the node-reboot caveat (documented, healed by re-deploy).

**Affected code/docs:**
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/ssh.py` — hook template + `_attach_gre_tunnel` + `_install_ovs_mirror` rewritten; `_tc_mirror_cmd` helper added; dropped unused `gre_port_for_bridge` / `mirror_name_for_vmid` imports; module docstring.
- `axrange/ops/registry.py` — OP-MIR-010/020 descriptions (tc/gretap).
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/adapter.py`, `config.py` — SSH-backend docstring + example-conf comment (tc/ip link).
- `tests/test_adapter_proxmox.py` — replaced the OVS `attach-gre-tunnel` test with four: gretap-netdev create, Model-C adopt, tc hook content (both directions + fail-loud, no OVS), in-band tc when running. **206 tests pass.**
- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §3 (gretap-at-deploy split + node-reboot caveat), `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` §5.1/§5.2 (OP-NET reconciliation + start-vm).

**Live-verify on `pxtwin1` (PVE 8.4.0, kernel 6.8.12-9-pve, OVS 3.1.0, iproute2-6.1.0) — gates 1–3 PASS; gate 4 mechanism proven; gate 5 planner-verified.** A clean deploy of `tests/fixtures/ir/valid_slice` (one VLAN-zone network, two tapped hosts h-a/h-b) ran 25/25 → COMPLETED with the new ops, then:

- **Gate 1 (tc-mirred → gretap) PASS.** Both VM taps (`tap607525i0`, `tap851340i0`) carry a `clsact` qdisc with `matchall action mirred Egress Mirror to device gre_742705` on **both** ingress and egress; the gretap netdev `gre_742705` is `remote 10.0.0.86 … ikey/okey 116.39.5.136 mtu 1500 UP`. Its TX counter climbed 641→695 under traffic — the mirror is live.
- **Gate 2 (east-west, both directions) PASS.** `ping` h-a→h-b (8/8, via `qm guest exec`) appeared at a flow-based sink gretap (`type gretap external`) as BOTH `10.10.0.10 > 10.10.0.11 ICMP echo request` AND `10.10.0.11 > 10.10.0.10 ICMP echo reply` (16 each — 2× because both taps mirror the shared frames; expected in a merged capture), plus east-west ARP from both hosts.
- **Gate 3 (VLAN-tag at tap) — observed: UNTAGGED.** 0 `802.1Q` frames in the capture; frames are plain `ethertype IPv4/ARP`. In a VLAN zone the tap is an access port, so the tag (10) is applied downstream at the underlay trunk, not at the tap. Acceptable for v1's merged, no-correlation capture (the tag only mattered for the deferred per-vnet attribution feature).
- **Gate 4 (flow-based sink) — mechanism PROVEN.** The keyed tunnel (key 116.39.5.136) decapped only on a `type gretap external` iface; the lab sink's existing fixed-`remote` `gretap1` cannot match a keyed packet — confirming the migration is needed. Making it the production path (the sink's `axrange-gretap.service` → `type gretap external` + `capture.service` restart so `/captures` receives frames) is a shared-sink-host change pending operator authorization.
- **Gate 5 (two-sink split) — planner/adapter verified; live deferred.** The plan for `valid_two_sink` emits two distinct tunnels (`attach-gre-tunnel[corp@s1]`, `[corp@s2]`) with distinct per-(bridge, sink) keys + device names; `install-ovs-mirror[h-a]`→s2, `[h-b]`→s1. The end-to-end split needs a second sink endpoint (lab has one); deferred pending a 2nd sink.

**Adjacent finding (not caused by this change):** `axrange deploy --fresh` does NOT re-run ops marked `completed` under a prior deploy for the same plan — the executor's resume short-circuit (`executor.py` `get_op(op_id)`) is keyed by the plan-based op_id, not the deploy_id. With cluster resources destroyed out-of-band, a `--fresh` run can report "25 ok" without re-creating them (observed here; worked around by clearing the local state DB). CLAUDE.md claims `--fresh` forces a clean redeploy. Candidate fix: scope the resume check to the current deploy_id, or have `--fresh` clear prior op rows for the plan. Tracked separately.

## 2026-06-04 — design: SDN-native capture via per-tap `tc` mirror on the Linux vnet bridge (Option 1 adopted for v1)

**Authority:** design (chat session)

**What changed:**

Resolves the 2026-06-03 live-verify conflict (SDN-native networking silently breaks the per-VM OVS mirror) by adopting **Option 1** from `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_sdn_capture.md`: **keep SDN-native networking, and re-mechanize capture from an OVS port-mirror to a per-tap Linux `tc` mirror.** SDN-native (2026-06-03) is **retained, not reversed** — only the *mirror mechanism* changes.

- **Capture mechanism.** Each tapped VM's mirror becomes a `tc` `clsact` qdisc on the VM tap (which PVE SDN places on the materialized **Linux vnet bridge**), with `matchall` `mirred` filters on **both** the ingress and egress hooks, mirroring to a **keyed gretap netdev** (`ip link add … type gretap … key <k>`) whose remote is the sink's `vnet-transport` IP. This mirrors **at the tap, before bridge switching**, so it captures **east-west** traffic between VMs on one vnet — the common case the OVS-underlay approach structurally missed. Replaces the `ovs-vsctl … select-dst-port=<tap>` per-VM mirror.
- **The OVS-only-bridge invariant is retired.** `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §3's "every scenario bridge MUST be an OVS bridge … port mirroring depends on it" held *only* because port mirroring used OVS. With `tc` mirroring at the tap, capture no longer depends on the bridge type — it works on the SDN-materialized Linux vnet bridge directly. This dissolves the §8.2 tension the live finding confirmed (and which had earlier been mislabeled a Simple-zone-only limitation).
- **GRE transport.** The mirror target moves from an OVS GRE *port* on the underlay to a standalone **gretap netdev** fed by `tc mirred`. The **per-(bridge, sink) GRE key** + **flow-based sink** model from item B and the 2026-06-01 multi-tunnel work is **carried over unchanged** — the key now lives on the gretap netdev, and each sink still terminates on a `type gretap external` that decaps any key into one merged stream.
- **Hook lifecycle unchanged in shape.** Taps are recreated on every VM boot, so the `tc` rules + gretap are (re)installed by the VM **post-start** hook and torn down on **pre-stop**, exactly as the OVS mirror was — stateless, self-healing, rebuilt from the plan. Only the commands inside the hook change (`tc`/`ip link` instead of `ovs-vsctl`).
- **Companion fix — fail loud, not silent (recommended).** The defining defect this episode exposed is that capture died *silently* (25/25 COMPLETED, zero frames). The `mirror-ready` barrier should be hardened from a structural check (did the mirror op return success?) into a **liveness gate**: assert a `tc` filter is actually bound and that frames egress the gretap (RX-counter delta under a synthetic probe) before declaring capture ready. Mechanism-independent; it prevents the next mirror regression from being silent. *(Design recommendation — strike on review if out of v1 scope.)*

**Why:**

- Capture is the platform's reason to exist and was silently dead under SDN-native-as-shipped — the worst possible failure mode. Option 1 is the only option that **unifies** SDN-native and working capture (the mirror lives where the taps live), so v1 keeps **one** networking model and **one** mirror mechanism rather than forking into two permanent models (Option 2) or reversing SDN-native (Option 3).
- The chat's recommendation was a two-track (Opt 3 now, Opt 1 later); the user elected **Opt 1 directly in v1** to avoid carrying a throwaway bypass and to keep SDN-native intact through the transition. Trade explicitly accepted: Option 1 is **unproven on this PVE/OVS stack**, so the live-verify gates below are mandatory before it counts as done.

**Supersedes:** the **2026-05-21 "Capture path: Proxmox SDN with OVS-backed bridges and per-VM mirrors"** entry — *as to capture mechanism only*. The per-VM OVS port-mirror and the "scenario bridges must be OVS bridges" requirement are replaced by per-tap `tc` mirroring on the SDN-materialized Linux vnet bridge. The cross-host GRE-to-sink role (functional ERSPAN) and the SDN zone/vnet model (2026-06-03 SDN-native) are **preserved** and built upon.

**Resolves:** the 2026-06-03 "LIVE-VERIFY FINDING: SDN-native networking breaks the per-VM OVS mirror" and its Simple-zone sibling limitation — both Simple and VLAN zones now capture via `tc` at the tap.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` — §3 (per-VM OVS mirror → per-tap tc mirror; OVS-only-bridge invariant retired; cross-host GRE = standalone gretap netdev; post-start hook installs tc rules). §4 transport/flow-based-sink model unchanged.
- `docs/ADAPTER.md` — §3 OP-MIR capability descriptions (tc + gretap netdev); §8 historical-finding banner marked resolved.
- `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` — §5.3 OP-MIR-010/020 capability text (tc/gretap); §5.1 `create-ovs-bridge` rationale softened (mirroring no longer depends on OVS); OP-NET op-def `after:`/inputs reconciled to the shipped SDN-native registry (doc-drift fix).
- `docs/sink/README.md` — new "GRE transport (flow-based gretap)" section (the `type gretap external` requirement; lab sink's fixed-`remote` gretap must migrate).
- `CLAUDE.md` — architecture diagram + tech-stack mirror-plane row; "What NOT to Do" SDN/capture bullet; Current State + Immediate Next Actions item A; item B unblock note.
- `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` — Q5 reopened-banner → resolved.
- `docs/DESIGN_BRIEF_sdn_capture.md` — status OPEN → RESOLVED (Option 1).

**Resolve by building (implementation, Claude Code):**
- Exact `tc` command forms: `clsact` qdisc per tap; `matchall` `mirred egress mirror` on ingress + egress; gretap netdev create/teardown; MTU (+24, the §3 MTU math); idempotent re-assert in the post-start hook (Model-C-style check-then-act on `tc filter show`).
- Catalog reconciliation: whether `create-ovs-bridge` (OP-NET-020) is still required under tc-on-SDN (capture no longer needs OVS) and whether OP-MIR-020 `install-ovs-mirror` warrants a rename (kept stable for now).
- Rewrite `axrange/adapter_proxmox/ssh.py` OP-MIR ops from `ovs-vsctl` to `tc`/`ip link`; reuse `gre_key_for_bridge_sink` to stamp the key on the gretap netdev.
- The `mirror-ready` liveness gate (if adopted).

**Mandatory live-verify gates on `pxtwin1` (Option 1 is a bet until these pass):**
1. **tc-mirred-to-gretap works** on PVE 8.4 / OVS 3.1: a tapped VLAN-zone deploy → `tc filter` bound on the tap → frames decapped at a `type gretap external` sink.
2. **East-west captured:** two VMs on one vnet, bidirectional ping, **both directions** at the sink.
3. **VLAN-tag behavior at the tap:** confirm tagged-vs-untagged for a VLAN zone. *Lower-stakes for v1* — merged black-box capture has no per-scenario correlation, so an untagged merged stream is acceptable; the tag only matters for the deferred per-vnet attribution feature. Document what's observed.
4. **Sink fix (independent):** migrate the lab sink's `CAPTURE_IF` from a fixed-`remote` gretap to `type gretap external`.
5. **Re-run item B end-to-end:** two sinks, taps split across them, each capturing only its taps (unblocked once 1–2 pass).

**Tests:** none yet (design entry). Implementation lands its own `implementation (Claude Code)` entry + tests.

## 2026-06-03 — LIVE-VERIFY FINDING: SDN-native networking breaks the per-VM OVS mirror (capture plane) — design escalation

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**Status:** ⚠️ **Blocks the capture plane under SDN-native. Needs a design decision — do NOT silently patch.**

**Resolved by:** the 2026-06-04 design entry "SDN-native capture via per-tap `tc` mirror on the Linux vnet bridge" (Option 1) — capture re-mechanized onto `tc`; SDN-native retained.

**What was verified on `pxtwin1` (PVE 8.4.0, OVS 3.1.0):**

A clean `axrange deploy --fresh` of the VLAN slice fixture (after tearing down the prior bypass-era deploy) ran **25/25 ops to COMPLETED**. The SDN-native infra is correct and real:

- `/cluster/sdn/zones` → `{zone: axr3b0e3, type: vlan, bridge: vbr_3b0e37}` (VLAN zone referencing the OVS underlay).
- `/cluster/sdn/vnets` → `{vnet: vn19f4c6, zone: axr3b0e3, tag: 10}`.
- VMs attach `net0 bridge=vn19f4c6` with **no NIC `tag=`** (the vnet carries the VLAN — correct SDN-native model, vs. the old bypass `bridge=vbr_corp,tag=10`).
- GRE tunnel `gre_742705` (type=gre, `key=1948714376`, `remote_ip=10.0.0.86`) up on the underlay.
- h-a→h-b ping: **3/3, 0% loss** — east-west connectivity works.

**The problem — capture produces nothing, silently:**

- `ovs-vsctl list mirror` → **NO MIRRORS**. The per-VM mirror was never created.
- **Root cause:** PVE SDN (VLAN *and* Simple zones) materializes each vnet as a **Linux bridge** (`vn19f4c6`) holding the VM taps, *linked* to the OVS underlay via an internal port (`ln_vn19f4c6`). So `tap607525i0` is on the **Linux** vnet bridge — `ovs-vsctl port-to-br tap607525i0` → *"no port named tap607525i0"*. The mirror hook's `ovs-vsctl … select-dst-port=<tap>` has nothing to bind on the OVS underlay, so it no-ops. Compounding it: two VMs on one vnet switch **east-west on the Linux bridge**, so their traffic never traverses the OVS underlay where a mirror could live anyway.
- Net: the deploy reports success, the GRE tunnel is up, and **zero scenario frames reach the sink** — exactly the silent capture-loss failure mode the concurrency/capture design worried about.

**Why this matters / supersedes an assumption:** the 2026-06-03 "SDN-native networking" design entry and its implementation assumed the **VLAN path was "OVS-clean"** (mirror on the underlay). Live hardware shows it is **not** — the per-VM OVS mirror (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §3, "every scenario bridge MUST be an OVS bridge … port mirroring depends on it") is **incompatible with PVE SDN's Linux-vnet-on-OVS-underlay topology**. This is the §8.2 tension the bypass originally sidestepped, now confirmed to bite the SDN-native model too — not just the Simple-zone case flagged at implementation.

**Secondary finding (sink config):** the lab sink's `CAPTURE_IF=gretap1` is a **fixed-`remote` gretap**, not the flow-based `type gretap external` the keyed-tunnel model requires (`sink/README.md`). Moot while no mirror feeds it, but it must be fixed before any keyed capture works.

**Decision required (escalate to a design session — this is NOT an in-place code fix):** how to capture under SDN-native. Candidate directions, none yet ratified:
1. Mirror on the **Linux vnet bridge** via a non-OVS mechanism (tc `mirred`/` tc-flower`, or a netem/SPAN equivalent) — abandons the "OVS-only mirror" invariant.
2. Re-home scenario VMs onto a **plain OVS bridge** (the retired bypass's one virtue) — i.e. reconsider whether SDN-native is compatible with v1 capture at all.
3. A **hybrid**: SDN for addressing, but a parallel OVS tap/SPAN port the VM also sits on.
Per `CLAUDE.md` "What NOT to Do", reverting SDN-native requires a design decision — so this stays documented and **open**, code unchanged.

**Other items this session:**
- **C (cross-process locking):** VERIFIED — `scripts/verify_resource_locks.py` passes (mutual exclusion across processes + single lease winner) against a real SQLite backend.
- **B (per-tap sink routing):** plan/validate/preflight verified, but **end-to-end split-capture is BLOCKED by this finding** (no base capture to split). Resume once the SDN-native capture path is resolved.

**Affected docs:** `CLAUDE.md` (item A status → live-verified, capture broken); `docs/ADAPTER.md` §8 (live-verify note); this entry. No code changed.

## 2026-06-03 — axrange: per-tap sink routing implemented (IR sinks: + REF-200 + per-(bridge,sink) tunnels)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

Implemented per-tap sink routing — route any tap point to any sink — ratified the same day (design entry "per-tap sink routing"), Immediate Next Actions item **B**. Additive over the 2026-06-01 multi-tunnel work; each sink stays a merged black box (no per-scenario correlation).

- **IR (`ir/schema.py`, `ir/parser.py`).** New top-level `sinks:` list (`Sink{id, primary}`); `Binding` gains `tap_host` / `tap_net` / `sink` (the tap→sink kind). `sinks:` is include-mergeable. No `sinks:` block → one implicit `default` primary sink (the pre-2026-06-03 single-sink behavior, kept).
- **Validator (`catalog.py`, `checks/references.py`, `checks/structural.py`).** New rule **REF-200** — a tap→sink binding's `sink` must resolve to a `sinks:` entry, and its `tap_host`/`tap_net` to a tapped node / defined network (missing-sink and no-target also fail). `STR-100` learns the tap→sink binding kind; `STR-180` learns the `sinks:` top-level key. Catalog count **86→87 / REF 19→20**, bumped atomically with the `catalog.py` runtime assertions and the `test_catalog` / VALIDATOR.md counts (no straggler).
- **Planner (`pass1_emit.py`).** Resolves the primary sink + each tapped host's target sink (tap→sink binding, else primary), then emits one `attach-gre-tunnel` **per (bridge, distinct sink)** — scope `{net, sink}` — with `sink_id` + the resolved `sink_transport_ip`. Each `install-ovs-mirror` reads `gre_port` from *its* host's `(net, sink)` tunnel. One `mirror-ready` barrier still gates all of it.
- **Adapter (`naming.py`, `ssh.py`, `config.py`, `cluster.py`).** `gre_port_for_bridge_sink` / `gre_key_for_bridge_sink` derive a distinct OVS port + 32-bit key per `(bridge, sink)` so coexisting tunnels don't collide; `_attach_gre_tunnel` uses them via the op's `sink_id`. `AdapterConfig.sinks` (id→IP) parsed from `SINK_<ID>_TRANSPORT_IP`; `ClusterContext.sinks` + `sink_ip(id)` resolve a logical sink to its transport endpoint at deploy time (IR stays platform-agnostic). `OP-MIR-010` gains a `sink_id` input and `gretap:$sink_id` resource category (the cross-process gretap lock is now per *target sink*).

**Why:**

Per the design entry: multi-site DC twins need capture directed by topology region (one sink per site/tier) rather than one merged firehose. Per-tap granularity is the most flexible primitive and subsumes coarser groupings. It routes/shards capture; it does not add correlation — "no scenario correlation in v1" holds per sink.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/VALIDATOR.md` — REF-200 row + count lead (80 IR-side / 87 total / REF 20).
- `docs/IR_SCHEMA.md` — already carries the `sinks:` + tap→sink spec (design entry).
- `CLAUDE.md` — validator counts (87); Immediate Next Actions item B; test count.
- `adapter.conf.example` — `SINK_<ID>_TRANSPORT_IP` documented.

**Resolve by building / live-verify pending (cannot verify without `pxtwin1`):** two sinks with taps split across them, each sink capturing only its taps; the per-(bridge, sink) GRE keys decapping correctly on per-sink flow-based gretaps. Interaction with the SDN-native Simple-zone limitation (Simple = Linux bridge, no mirror) is unchanged — capture is the VLAN path.

**Refines (implements):** the 2026-06-03 design entry "per-tap sink routing."

**Tests:** +10 (`tests/test_sink_routing.py` 6 + `tests/test_validator_ref.py` 4); `test_adapter_proxmox` / `test_planner_dag` / `test_catalog` updated for the new scope/count. Full suite 202 passing.

## 2026-06-03 — axrange(adapter): SDN-native OP-NET ops implemented — bypass retired

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

Re-implemented the four OP-NET ops against `/cluster/sdn`, retiring the slice-MVP direct-`/nodes/<node>/network` bridge bypass — Immediate Next Actions item **A** / ADAPTER §8.3 route 1.

- **Zone-type-driven structure (auto-derived, OP-NET-010).** `_zone_type` (pass1) already derives VLAN-vs-Simple from whether any network declares a `vlan`. The emission + adapter now branch on it:
  - **VLAN zone:** ONE OVS **underlay** bridge per zone (`underlay_bridge_for_plan`, new in `naming.py`), created via `/nodes/<node>/network` (type=OVSBridge) + a node-network commit; an SDN **VLAN zone** referencing that bridge; each network a vnet with `tag=<vlan>`. `create-ovs-bridge` emits **once per zone** (scenario-scoped).
  - **Simple zone:** one vnet per network (PVE materializes the bridge); `create-ovs-bridge` emits **once per network** and is a logical pass-through returning the vnet bridge name. ⚠️ **Known limitation:** PVE Simple-zone vnets are **Linux** bridges, which have no OVS port-mirror — so capture on a *Simple*-zone scenario will not work as-is. The VLAN path (the live slice fixture) is OVS-clean. This is the residual of the §8.2 tension; flagged for cluster reconciliation.
- **Ordering reversed.** `create-ovs-bridge` now runs **before** `create-sdn-zone` (registry `after` updated: `create-ovs-bridge` → `()`; `create-sdn-zone` → `create-ovs-bridge[*]`) so a VLAN zone can reference an already-existing underlay. `create-vnet` drops its `bridge_name` input (SDN vnets need only zone + tag).
- **REST calls.** `op_create_sdn_zone` → `POST /cluster/sdn/zones` (`type`, `zone`, `bridge` for VLAN); `op_create_vnet` → `POST /cluster/sdn/vnets` (`vnet`, `zone`, `tag` for VLAN); `op_apply_sdn_config` → `PUT /cluster/sdn` (cluster-wide apply, replacing the per-node `/nodes/<node>/network` PUT). All keep Model-C check-then-act (`_zone_exists` / `_vnet_exists` / `_bridge_exists`).
- **NIC attach drops the tag.** `attach-vm-to-vnet` attaches the VM NIC to the SDN vnet's bridge with **no** `tag=` — the VLAN zone's vnet already carries the tag; tagging the NIC too would double-tag (pass1 now sends `vlan=None`).
- **Mirror plane re-wired (pass1).** `attach-gre-tunnel` / `install-ovs-mirror` take `bridge_name` from the VLAN underlay (VLAN) or the per-net bridge (Simple). `ssh.py` is unchanged — it operates on whatever `bridge_name` it's handed.

**Why:**

Per the same-day design entry below: SDN's cluster-wide apply is the multi-node distribution machinery the target profile (multi-site twins, eventual multi-node) needs, and `/cluster/sdn` is the surface PVE intends for programmatic network definition. Direct `/nodes/<node>/network` ownership is the most Proxmox-specific substrate and only sidestepped distribution because the slice ran on one node.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/ADAPTER.md` — §3 OP-NET note (implementation landed); §8 (implementation-landed banner).
- `CLAUDE.md` — adapter submodule bullet (bypass → SDN-native, implemented); Immediate Next Actions item A.
- `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` §5.1 — already matches (zone-type cardinality, design entry).

**Resolve by building / live-verify pending (cannot verify without `pxtwin1`):**
- **Re-run the tapped VLAN slice fixture → 25/25 + pcaps at the sink against SDN-native bridges.**
- Exact `/cluster/sdn` payloads/▸task semantics on PVE 8.x (zone/vnet POST may return a worker; apply may need polling).
- **Does an SDN apply still momentarily detach running VM taps?** The 2026-05-29 SSH tap-re-assert is **kept** in `op_apply_sdn_config` pending this check — retire it if SDN handles taps cleanly; note the taps now live on the SDN vnet bridge, not the `vbr_*` underlay the scan targets.
- Whether the bridge-before-zone ordering + node-network commit is sufficient for a VLAN zone to accept the underlay on first apply.
- **The Simple-zone Linux-bridge / mirroring limitation** above — needs a real-cluster decision (e.g. force VLAN-with-auto-tags for capture-bearing Simple scenarios).

**Refines (implements):** the 2026-06-03 design entry "SDN-native networking ratified — retire the slice-MVP bridge bypass."

**Tests:** +9 adapter SDN-native tests (`tests/test_adapter_proxmox.py`); 3 planner tests updated for the new ordering/cardinality. Full suite 192 passing. Both zone-type paths verified to emit correctly (VLAN: one scenario-scoped underlay; Simple: per-net bridges; bridge→zone→vnet ordering).

## 2026-06-03 — axrange(planner): cross-process resource locking implemented (lease + resource_locks)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

Implemented the correctness-lock layer ratified the same day (the "cross-process resource locking" design entry below; `docs/PLANNER.md` §6.2/§7.2/§8.4) — Immediate Next Actions item **C**. The executor stays internally **sequential**; this adds *inter-deploy* safety, not intra-deploy parallelism.

- **State store (`axrange/planner/state.py`).** Two new tables + atomic operations:
  - `leases` (per-scenario single-writer gate, §6.2): `acquire_lease` refuses while a valid lease exists (held by *anyone* — a fresh process never owns a still-valid lease) and takes over an absent/expired one in a single check-then-write transaction; `renew_lease` (holder-only heartbeat), `release_lease`, `get_lease`.
  - `resource_locks` (§8.4): `acquire_resource_locks` is **all-or-nothing** — one transaction checks every requested key is free (absent / expired / already this deploy's) and only then writes them all, so no process ever holds a partial set while waiting (deadlock-free per §8.3). Plus `renew_resource_locks`, `release_resource_locks_for_op`, `release_resource_locks_for_deploy`, `held_resource_locks`.
  - `PRAGMA busy_timeout=5000` so the heartbeat's second connection and the executor's connection don't trip over each other under WAL.
- **Lock module (`axrange/planner/locks.py`, new).** `correctness_lock_keys` resolves an op's correctness `resource_categories` (`ovs:`/`vm:`/`gretap:` — the §8.2 "fixed at 1, serialization is correctness" set) `$var` templates against scope + resolved inputs + `cluster.sink_id`, returning a **sorted** key list (one global acquisition order). Throughput categories (`proxmox-cluster`/`proxmox-node:*`/`ansible-pool`) are excluded. `acquire_blocking` polls until the whole set is held or a timeout elapses (a contended op then fails cleanly; a crashed holder releases via expiry). `Heartbeat` is a daemon thread on its own DB connection renewing the lease + held locks every minute.
- **Executor (`axrange/planner/executor.py`).** Before an op's first attempt it acquires that op's correctness locks (held across retries), releasing them on any terminal outcome via `finally`. New `resource_locks` / `lock_ttl_s` / `lock_poll_s` / `lock_timeout_s` knobs (locks on by default; off for sole-writer/unit-test paths). Retry loop factored into `_run_attempts`.
- **CLI (`axrange/cli/main.py`).** `axrange deploy` takes the per-scenario lease before mutating deploy state — a second concurrent deploy of the same scenario is refused with an actionable message — runs a `Heartbeat` for the deploy's lifetime, and releases the deploy's locks + lease in a `finally`.

**Why:**

- Per `docs/PLANNER.md` §8.4: the §8.1–§8.3 semaphores are in-process, but the target profile runs many concurrent deploys as separate processes, so the correctness categories did not actually serialize across them. The capture-plane races in particular fail *silently* (lost frames), which retry can't catch — hence DB-backed locks.
- Sorted-key + all-or-nothing acquisition is the minimal mechanism that is provably deadlock-free across processes, and reuses the lease machinery the design called for.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/PLANNER.md` — §6.2 / §7.2 (`leases` + `resource_locks` schema, `acquired_at` added) / §8.4 (implementation paragraph).
- `CLAUDE.md` — planner submodule bullet; Immediate Next Actions item C marked implemented; deferred-machinery prohibition updated.

**Resolve by building / pending:** **live-verify** two concurrent deploys on `pxtwin1` (lease refusal + shared-resource serialization). Lock TTL (300 s) / poll (1 s) / acquire-timeout (600 s) are first-cut defaults to tune under real churn. Throughput semaphores + the parallel intra-deploy executor remain deferred (a later authorized slice).

**Refines (does not supersede):** the 2026-06-03 design entry "cross-process resource locking — DB-backed leases for the 'correctness' concurrency categories" (this is its implementation).

**Tests:** +22 (`tests/test_planner_locks.py` 19; `tests/test_planner_executor.py` 3). Full suite 184 passing.

     entry that follows them, and are ordered SDN-native → tap-sink → resource-locks
     because the latter two reference the first. -->

## 2026-06-03 — design: SDN-native networking ratified — retire the slice-MVP bridge bypass (ADAPTER §8.3 route 1)

**Authority:** design (chat session)

**What changed:**

The Proxmox adapter's networking moves back to **SDN-native** definitions via `/cluster/sdn` with a cluster-wide apply, retiring the slice-MVP direct-bridge bypass (`docs/ADAPTER.md` §8). This resolves the question §8.3 left open, and resolves it as **route 1 (reconcile the catalog with PVE-SDN)** — not route 2 (ratify direct-bridge as permanent).

- **Zone type is auto-derived**, exactly as `OP-NET-010` already specified (derive `zone_type` from whether any network declares a `vlan`):
  - **No VLANs declared → Simple zone.** Each network's vnet materializes its own OVS bridge — one bridge per network. `create-ovs-bridge` (OP-NET-020) emits **once per network**.
  - **VLANs declared → VLAN zone.** One underlay OVS bridge for the zone; networks are VLAN-tagged vnets on it. `create-ovs-bridge` emits **once per zone**.
  This fixes the one internal inconsistency the catalog carried — `create-ovs-bridge` previously emitted per-network *regardless* of zone type, which is wrong for a VLAN zone's single underlay bridge (the precise mismatch `docs/ADAPTER.md` §8.2 named).
- `create-sdn-zone` / `create-vnet` / `apply-sdn-config` define and commit real SDN objects in `/cluster/sdn` again (no longer logical no-ops plus direct `/nodes/<node>/network` posts). Operators inspect scenario networks via `/cluster/sdn`.
- The op → capability → backend table (`docs/ADAPTER.md` §3) is unchanged in **contract** — same ops, same REST backend, same `inputs`/`outputs`. Only the OP-NET REST-side **implementation** returns to SDN-native; the per-VM mirror/GRE plane stays `ovs-vsctl`-over-SSH (SDN never exposed mirrors, so that plane is untouched).

**Why:**

- The driving requirement is **logical multi-site digital twins on a single large PVE host, with high scenario churn and eventual capacity-driven multi-node**. §8.3 route 2 names its own expiry condition — "revisit SDN integration when … a multi-node bridge-distribution need forces it." That is now the stated direction. Direct-bridge posts to `/nodes/<node>/network` (a per-node op); it only avoided a distribution problem because the slice ran on one node. SDN's cluster-wide apply *is* the multi-node distribution machinery, so route 2 would mean re-implementing it by hand.
- Even on one host, high **churn** favors SDN: direct-bridge commits via `PUT /nodes/<node>/network` + a host-wide `ifreload -a`, which momentarily detaches every running VM tap (the 2026-05-29 finding, patched by SSH tap-re-assert). A host-wide reload per network mutation, rippling across every other running scenario, is fragile at churn; SDN's incremental apply is the surface PVE intends for programmatic network definition.
- SDN-native is the least platform-locked choice and keeps the platform-agnostic story honest for future VMware/cloud adapters (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §4 already maps the transport vnet onto AWS/Azure mirror targets). Direct `/nodes/<node>/network` is the most Proxmox-specific possible substrate.
- Auto-derived zone type lets the twin dial L2 fidelity by whether VLANs are written into the IR — VLAN zones reproduce real DC VLAN structure and its attack surface (trunk misconfig, VLAN hopping), squarely a cyber-range concern; flat scenarios get clean per-bridge isolation via Simple zones.

**Supersedes:** the slice-MVP SDN-bypass — established in the 2026-05-29 "Slice MVP end-to-end on pxtwin1" entry and documented (with the question explicitly left open) in the 2026-06-01 "Doc reconciliation" entry item 3 (`docs/ADAPTER.md` §8). Direct-bridge ownership is **not** the permanent v1 model; SDN-native is. `CLAUDE.md` "What NOT to Do" flips from "don't reintroduce SDN modeling into the slice" to "SDN-native is the model; the direct-bridge bypass is retired."

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/ADAPTER.md` — §3 op-mapping note (the SDN-native model is the model again, not merely "intended"); §8 reframed as a historical slice-MVP record, now superseded.
- `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` — §5.1 OP-NET narrative + `create-ovs-bridge` cardinality (per-network in Simple / once-per-zone in VLAN), reconciling the "one bridge per network" vs VLAN-zone tension.
- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` — §3 "SDN: Zone, vnets, OVS bridge" (zone-type model made explicit).
- `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` — Q5 gains an SDN-native-ratified closure note.
- `CLAUDE.md` — adapter bullet (SDN-native, bypass retired); "What NOT to Do" SDN prohibition flipped; Immediate Next Actions.

**Resolve by building (implementation):** exact `/cluster/sdn` REST endpoints + payloads per OP-NET op; stable SDN object naming under Model C; **whether an SDN apply still momentarily detaches running VM taps** (if so, the 2026-05-29 SSH tap-re-assert survives as-is; if SDN handles it cleanly, that hook retires). Per-VM mirror behavior is unchanged (still `ovs-vsctl` on the OVS bridge, which SDN still backs).

## 2026-06-03 — design: per-tap sink routing (route any tap point to any sink)

**Authority:** design (chat session)

**What changed:**

Tap points gain a **per-tap sink assignment**: a deploy can route different tap points to different capture sinks (all taps → one sink; or taps A/B/C → sink1 and D/E → sink2; etc.). v1 previously assumed a single sink.

- **IR:** a top-level **`sinks:`** block declares logical sink ids; a **tap→sink mapping lives in `bindings:`** (matching the existing typed-binding pattern — `app→host`, `activity→host`, …). A node's `tap: true` defaults to a primary sink, so existing IRs are unchanged. The logical sink id resolves to a transport endpoint (`vnet-transport` IP) via **platform/cluster config at deploy time**, not the IR — the IR stays platform-agnostic, exactly as `vnet-transport` is platform infra today rather than an IR object.
- **Adapter/planner:** for each bridge, emit one GRE tunnel **per distinct target sink** among that bridge's taps (not just one per bridge); each per-VM mirror's `output-port` is the tunnel for *its* tap's sink. This generalizes the per-bridge GRE key (`gre_key_for_bridge`) to a **per-(bridge, sink)** key.
- **Sink:** unchanged software — run N of them, each on its own `vnet-transport` IP, each a **flow-based** gretap. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §4 already has all zones sharing one transport vnet, so multiple sinks are just multiple endpoints on it; the transport layer doesn't multiply.
- **Validator:** **one new REF-class rule** — a tap's named sink must exist in `sinks:`. Its rule number and the catalog-count bump (86 → 87 / REF 19 → 20) are an `implementation (Claude Code)` follow-up, added atomically with the runtime-assertion bump in `validator/catalog.py` (the per-category tables remain the authoritative count). It is **not** numbered here to avoid the count-straggler failure mode the 2026-05-28/06-01 entries fought.

**Why:**

- The driving use case (multi-site DC twins) needs to direct capture by topology region — one sink per logical site or tier — rather than one merged firehose. Per-tap granularity (decoupled from zone/bridge structure) is the most flexible primitive and subsumes coarser groupings.
- It is **additive, not a reversal.** Each sink still receives a merged, time-ordered black-box stream of whatever taps point at it; "no scenario correlation in v1" holds **per sink**. This routes/shards capture; it does not add correlation. It therefore **extends** the 2026-06-01 "per-tunnel GRE key + flow-based sink gretap" multi-tunnel work (multiple keyed tunnels coexisting) rather than superseding it.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/IR_SCHEMA.md` — §1.3 skeleton gains optional top-level `sinks:`; §3.2 `tap` field notes the sink reference (default primary); §6 gains the tap→sink binding + a `sinks:` block description.
- `docs/VALIDATOR.md` — §5.3 forward-reference note for the new REF rule (full numbered row added at implementation).
- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` — §4 (multiple sink endpoints on the shared transport vnet); §3 GRE note (per-(bridge, sink) tunnel/key).
- `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` — OP-MIR `attach-gre-tunnel` gains the target-sink input; `gretap:<sink>` resource category is now per-target-sink (ties to the resource-lock entry).
- `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` — Q10 sharding axis noted as partially addressed (HA/standby/replication still deferred).
- `CLAUDE.md` — capture-plane summary (per-tap sink routing; still no per-scenario correlation).

**Resolve by building (implementation):** exact `sinks:` field shape + the `bindings:` tap→sink dict schema; where sink-endpoint resolution config lives on the orchestrator (alongside `adapter.conf`); the per-(bridge, sink) key derivation; the new REF rule number + count bump.

## 2026-06-03 — design: cross-process resource locking — DB-backed leases for the "correctness" concurrency categories

**Authority:** design (chat session)

**What changed:**

Closes a gap in the §8 concurrency model surfaced by the target operational profile (many scenarios deployed/torn down concurrently). The §8 per-resource semaphores are **in-process** — they govern one planner process's own ops — but `docs/PLANNER.md` §6.1 runs an **ephemeral process per deploy** and §6.2 permits concurrent deploys of *different* scenarios. So the categories §8.2 marks "serialization is correctness, not tuning" (`ovs:<node>`, `vm:<vmid>`, `gretap:<sink>`) do **not** actually serialize across concurrent deploy processes. (The SDN-native decision above sharpens this: `apply-sdn-config` is a cluster-wide mutation two concurrent deploys would race on.)

- **Fix — generalize the lease into resource locks.** The §6.2 per-scenario lease (DB row, atomic takeover, heartbeat, 5-min expiry) is generalized from scenarios to resources: a **`resource_locks`** table in the state store. Before acting on a *correctness* resource, a planner process acquires its DB lock (insert-if-absent keyed by holder `deploy_id`; released on op completion; same heartbeat/expiry as the scenario lease). The serialization now holds across processes because it lives in the authoritative store, not process memory. SQLite is single-writer already, so this is native; contention is low because these resources are "1"-limited by definition.
- **Split — correctness vs throughput.**
  - **Correctness** categories (`ovs:<node>`, `vm:<vmid>`, `gretap:<sink>`) → **DB-backed cross-process resource locks. Required.**
  - **Throughput** categories (`proxmox-cluster`, `proxmox-node:<name>`, `ansible-pool`, global `max-ops-in-flight`) → **per-process semaphores**, as in §8. Aggregate over-subscription across concurrent deploys is a rate-limit/performance risk the cluster API absorbs, not a correctness bug; the limit *numbers* remain guesses to tune on a real cluster (`docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` Q4 follow-up).
- **Sequencing — correctness layer first, decoupled from parallelism.** The per-scenario lease + resource locks make it **safe to run many deploys at once** and are independent of *intra-deploy* parallelism. They land **first**; each deploy can keep executing **internally sequentially** (today's executor, untouched). A parallel executor + tuned throughput semaphores (which make each deploy *faster*) is separable and deferred until deploy latency on a large twin warrants it.

**Why:**

- Single-writer-per-scenario (the lease) prevents double-deploying the *same* scenario, but the profile's risk is *different* scenarios contending on *shared* resources (a node's OVS, a sink's gretap, the cluster-wide SDN apply). Only cross-process locks cover that — and the capture-plane races in particular can be **silent** (lost frames, not a clean error that retry would catch).
- Reusing the lease machinery keeps the addition small; front-loading the correctness layer buys the safety the profile needs without waiting on the larger parallel-executor work.

**Refines (does not supersede):** the 2026-05-21 "Planner: DAG construction + runtime + concurrency + re-planning" concurrency model — the §8 semaphore model stands for *intra-deploy* parallelism; this entry adds the *inter-deploy* coordination it didn't specify, and corrects the implicit assumption that the "correctness" serializations held across processes.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/PLANNER.md` — §8 (correctness-locks-cross-process vs throughput-per-process split); §6.2 (lease generalized to resource locks); §7.2 schema (`resource_locks` table).
- `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` — Q4 concurrency note + Q7 state-store schema (`resource_locks`).
- `CLAUDE.md` — planner/state-store description.

**Resolve by building (implementation):** the `resource_locks` DDL + acquisition transaction; which exact ops map to which correctness resource (from the catalog's `resource_categories`); heartbeat-cadence reuse from the scenario lease. **Current-state note:** the slice has *no* lease implemented yet (only `deploys`/`ops`/`op_attempts` tables); step zero is the §6.2 per-scenario lease, then the resource-lock generalization.

## 2026-06-03 — config-class script fast-follow: run-script (OP-APP-030) via QGA

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

The `script` fast-follow (docs/SLICE_CONFIG.md §11) is implemented + unit-tested, completing the ratified config scope (`pkg` / `pkg@version` / `script`). `{script:}` install actions now emit **`OP-APP-030 run-script`** — the catalog's home for scripts (a correction: the ratification's wording said OP-APP-010, but the catalog routes `{script:}` to run-script).

- **`pass1_emit`** now classifies an app's install actions: `pkg`/`pkg@version` → `provision-app`; `script` → `run-script` (scope `{app,host}`, gated `after host-ready[$host]` + `provision-app[$app@*]`, `vmid` from `clone-vm`). One `script:` per app this cut (multiple = trivial fast-follow); `config`/`role`/`container` still rejected at emit time.
- **scenario_dir threading** — `build_plan(…, scenario_dir=…)` → `emit_ops` reads the `{script:}` file **at emit time** and embeds its content in the op inputs (the config backend can't reach the scenario bundle; the guest is isolated). The CLI passes `scenario_path.parent`; a script with no `scenario_dir` is a clear `EmissionError`.
- **`ConfigBackend._run_script`** — writes the script into the guest via `GuestAgent.write_file` (`/var/lib/axrange/provision/scripts/<app-name>`, `0755`) and execs it **directly over QGA** (honoring the shebang). Not Ansible-wrapped — a `script:` is opaque shell (`provision-app` keeps Ansible for pkg idempotency). QGA exec is root → the script runs as root.
- **op catalog** — `OP-APP-030`'s `after` corrected to `provision-app[$app@*]` / `run-container[$app@*]` (those ops are `{app,host}`-scoped, so a bare `$app` wouldn't match pass3's exact-set resolver).

**Escalate handling (limitation carried):** `run-script` is the **first `cleanup_strategy: escalate` op**. The slice executor treats escalate as redo (escalate-walk-back is deferred planner machinery, `PLANNER §4.4/§8`), so a failed script **retries naively** (up to 3×) — operators should make scripts idempotent. This makes escalate-walk-back the clearest next planner-machinery target.

**Affected code:** `ops/registry.py` (OP-APP-030 `after`); `planner/pass1_emit.py` (classify + run-script emission + `scenario_dir` + `_read_script`); `planner/plan_builder.py` + `cli/main.py` (`scenario_dir` threading); `config_exec/backend.py` (`_run_script`); `tests/fixtures/ir/valid_app/` (+ `setup.sh`, script action); `test_config_exec.py` + `test_planner_dag.py`.

**Tests:** 162 passing (was 156; +6).

**Open / next:** live verification (deploy an app with a `script:`, confirm the side effect in-guest); then `config`/`role`/`container` kinds, overlays (`OP-OVL`), activity (`OP-ACT`).

## 2026-06-02 — config-class execution slice implemented (OP-APP provision-app via QGA Ansible)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

The config-class slice authorized earlier today (`docs/SLICE_CONFIG.md`) is implemented and unit-tested — the first `config`-class plane: install software on a deployed host. **Pending live verification on `pxtwin1`** (needs a template with ansible baked; see the tie-in below).

- **`axrange/config_exec/`** — new submodule. `ConfigBackend.run()` (same shape as the Proxmox adapter) renders an Ansible playbook from a host's `install:` actions (`render.py`) and runs it *inside the guest* over the qemu-guest-agent exec channel: check ansible present → write the playbook in → `ansible-playbook` → report. The playbook targets `localhost` / `connection: local` / `become: false` (QGA exec is already root).
- **`axrange/adapter_proxmox/guest_agent.py`** — `GuestAgent` (ping / exec / write_file) factored out of `rest.py`'s `wait-os-ready` + `wait-guest-agent`, now shared by the infra adapter and the config backend (one QGA implementation). `rest.py` refactored to use it; behavior preserved (adapter tests green).
- **`pass1_emit`** — emits `provision-app[$app@$host]` from `layers.apps` + the app→host `bindings`; scope `{app,host}` (so the catalog's `after host-ready[$host]` resolves `$host`), `vmid` dataflowed from the host's `clone-vm`, `install_actions` + `package_manager` (from the image's `install_support.pkg`) in resolved inputs.
- **`executor`** — routes ops by `idempotency_class`: `config` → `ConfigBackend`, `infra` → `ProxmoxAdapter` (`_backend_for`); CLI `cmd_deploy` wires both.
- **imagebuild tie-in** — the `debian-12-cloud` build recipe now bakes `ansible` (`provision: [{ pkg: ansible }]`), so clones can run playbooks; this also exercises the imagebuild `provision:` path for the first time.

**Scope sequencing:** the ratified scope is `pkg` / `pkg@version` / `script`; this first cut implements **`pkg` / `pkg@version`** (the dominant kinds, no in-guest file transfer). `script` (and `config`/`role`/`container`) are rejected at emit time with a clear message and are the immediate fast-follow (`script` needs scenario-dir → guest file plumbing). A strict subset of the authorized slice.

**Known constraint (scenario-design, not a code bug):** an isolated scenario host has no internet, so `apt` installs only from a reachable mirror or a pre-populated cache — package availability is the operator's scenario-topology concern. Ansible itself is solved by baking it into the template (it can't be apt-installed on an isolated host on demand).

**Affected code:**
- `axrange/config_exec/` (render / backend / __init__) — new.
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/guest_agent.py` — new; `rest.py` wait ops refactored onto it.
- `axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py` (app emission), `executor.py` (config routing), `cli/main.py` (ConfigBackend wired into deploy).
- `tests/fixtures/ir/valid_app/` — new 1-host + 1-app fixture; `debian-12-cloud` recipe bakes ansible.
- `tests/test_config_exec.py` (13 new) + `test_planner_dag.py` (app emission/rejection) + `test_imagebuild.py` (recipe provision assertion).

**Tests:** 156 passing (was 142; +14).

**Live-verified (pxtwin1, 2026-06-02):** `axrange image build debian-12-cloud` baked an ansible template (622) — cloud-init installed QGA **and** ansible (the first run of the imagebuild `provision:` path), 4/4 probes. A one-host app scenario (install `ca-certificates`) deployed against 622 completed **14/14 ops including `provision-app`** (output `{"provisioned": true, "tasks": 1}`). In-guest checks: `ansible-playbook [core 2.14.18]` present (baked), the rendered playbook delivered via QGA at `/var/lib/axrange/provision/axr-certs.yml`, `ca-certificates install ok installed`, and a re-run of the playbook reported `ok=1 changed=0` (idempotent — the Ansible-native contract, executed over QGA). Used an already-present package so the isolated host needed no mirror; installing a *new* package additionally needs a reachable package source (the documented scenario-topology constraint). Non-destructive: app clone, bridge, and test template torn down; the existing slice + template 610 untouched.

**Open / next:** the `script` / `config` / `role` / `container` kinds (script is the immediate fast-follow); overlays (`OP-OVL`) + activity (`OP-ACT`).

## 2026-06-02 — design: config-class execution slice authorized (QGA-mediated Ansible; OP-APP provision)

**Authority:** user (ratified in a Claude Code session; design + docs authored by Claude Code)

**What changed:**

The first `config`-class implementation slice is **authorized**, with its one open design call decided. After the infra + capture + imagebuild foundation landed and was live-verified, the user ratified the `docs/SLICE_CONFIG.md` proposal with the recommended options:

- **Execution channel = QGA-mediated Ansible.** Config ops (`docs/PLANNER.md` §4.3, "run the playbook") deliver over the **qemu-guest-agent exec channel**: the orchestrator pushes the rendered playbook + files into the guest and runs `ansible-playbook` locally there, reading the result back over the agent. Deployed guests sit on isolated scenario SDN networks the orchestrator can't reach, so the original "pull-mode Ansible" intent doesn't work in v1 — the same reachability wall `wait-os-ready` hit (`docs/ADAPTER.md` §3) and solved the same way. The Ansible *render* is platform-agnostic; the *delivery* is the Proxmox adapter's (a future platform adapter swaps the channel, keeps the render).
- **Ansible in the guest = baked by imagebuild.** Templates carry ansible via a `provision:` step in the build recipe (`docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md`) — a tie-in that also exercises the imagebuild `provision:` path for the first time.
- **Slice scope = `OP-APP-010 provision-app` for `pkg` / `pkg@version` / `script`.** New submodule `axrange/config_exec/`; `pass1_emit` emits `provision-app` from `layers.apps` + the app→host `bindings`, gated `after host-ready[$host]` (+ `after provision-app[$dep]`); the executor routes by `idempotency_class` (`infra` → Proxmox adapter, `config` → config backend). Validator unchanged — the IR surface (apps / install_support / app→host bindings) is already validated.

**Why:**

- This is the largest remaining v1 implementation gap and unblocks overlays + activity (also `config`-class, same channel). The channel was the one genuinely-open design call; the rest of config execution was already ratified (`PLANNER §4.3`, op signatures in `OP_CATALOG`, the validated IR surface).
- QGA-mediated delivery is forced by the same guest isolation that drove the infra slice's `wait-os-ready` pivot — consistency over re-litigating pull-mode for v1.

**Supersedes / refines:** the "cloud-init + pull-mode Ansible" *delivery* wording in the tech stack (`CLAUDE.md`) and `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §147 — **for the v1 delivery channel only.** The "first-boot, Ansible, closed action set, not a runtime channel" intent is unchanged; only the pull-vs-QGA delivery sub-choice is settled (QGA), exactly as `wait-os-ready` refined its probe channel.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/SLICE_CONFIG.md` — status DRAFT → RATIFIED; decisions + §10 checklist marked done.
- `docs/PLANNER.md` §4.3 — the QGA-mediated v1 delivery channel folded in.
- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §147 — pull-mode → QGA-mediated for v1.
- `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` Q13 — DRAFT → RATIFIED.
- `CLAUDE.md` — Current State + Immediate Next Actions (config slice authorized); What NOT to Do narrowed (`OP-APP-010` `pkg`/`pkg@version`/`script` now allowed; overlays/activity/other-kinds/planner-machinery still forbidden); tech-stack IaC row (pull-mode → QGA-mediated v1); Conventions (`axrange/config_exec/`).

**Implementation:** not started — this entry authorizes it. Done-criteria in `docs/SLICE_CONFIG.md` §8.

## 2026-06-01 — design: per-tunnel GRE key + flow-based sink gretap (multi-tunnel capture coexistence)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code) — user-directed design change

**What changed:**

Completes the capture-plane finding documented in the "per-bridge GRE port name" entry below (which fixed only the port *name* collision). The adapter now stamps a **distinct per-bridge GRE key** on each scenario tunnel (`gre_key_for_bridge`), and the sink's capture interface must be a **flow-based** gretap (`ip link add … type gretap external`) that decapsulates any key. Together this lets **2+ tapped scenario bridges** mirror to one sink without colliding.

Why it's needed: an OVS GRE tunnel's datapath flow is keyed by `(remote_ip, key)`. Two unkeyed (`key=0`) tunnels from one host to one sink IP collide — only one egresses (verified live: a distinct key made the second tunnel's frames flow, captured as `GREv0 key=0x4e34 … ICMP`). The slice (one tapped network) never hit this; it surfaces the moment a deploy has 2+ tapped bridges — **concurrent scenarios OR a single scenario with 2+ tapped networks.** Live-found on `pxtwin1` 2026-06-01 running a second tapped scenario against a rebuilt template.

**Scope — coexistence only, NOT attribution.** Capture stays a single merged, time-ordered stream (the flow-based gretap decaps every key into one pcap set). This preserves the v1 "no scenario correlation" boundary. Per-scenario attribution *by key* remains deferred (`OP-MIR-030 register-mirror-source`, ID still reserved).

**Supersedes:** the capture-transport sub-decision of the **2026-05-27 "Op catalog v1"** entry ("v1 capture attribution = one gretap per zone; shared-gretap-with-metadata-keys deferred"). The "one gretap per zone" model never addressed the **sender-side** collision — two `key=0` tunnels from one host collide before any sink gretap sees them — so it was insufficient for real multi-tunnel deploys. The keyed + flow-based-gretap model replaces it **for coexistence**; the *attribution* half it deferred (`register-mirror-source`) stays deferred. `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §4 updated.

**Operational note (coordinated change):** this adapter requires the sink's capture gretap to be flow-based first — a fixed-key (or `key 0`) gretap silently drops keyed traffic. A single-scenario lab on a `key=0` gretap keeps capturing only until the adapter is upgraded; migrate the sink gretap to `type gretap external` (`sink/README.md` "GRE transport") with the upgrade.

**Live-verified (pxtwin1, 2026-06-01):** a second tapped scenario (network `corp3`, vlan 30) deployed against template 610 **alongside** the running `key=0` slice — **25/25 ops**, its tunnel **keyed** (`gre_f1c0d6`, key `4055946952`) coexisting with the slice's `gre-out` (key 0) with **no OVS collision** (pre-fix, a second tunnel collided). A flow-based `gretap` (`type gretap external`) on the sink **captured the keyed `corp3` ICMP** (60 frames incl. `10.30.0.10 > 10.30.0.11: ICMP echo request/reply`); the production `key=0` `gretap1` saw **0** of it (a `key=0` gretap can't decap keyed traffic) while **still capturing the existing slice** (18 frames). Non-disruptive: the test scenario, its bridge, and the flow-based gretap were torn down afterward; the running slice and production `capture.service` were untouched throughout.

**Affected code:**

- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/naming.py` — `gre_key_for_bridge(bridge_name)` (32-bit, non-zero, deterministic).
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/ssh.py` — `attach-gre-tunnel` sets `options:key` on add + idempotent re-assert (which also migrates a pre-key tunnel on re-deploy).
- `tests/test_adapter_proxmox.py` — `test_gre_key_for_bridge_distinct_nonzero`, `test_attach_gre_tunnel_stamps_per_bridge_key`.

**Affected docs:**

- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` — §4 mirror-plane example (keyed, per-bridge port) + the storage-layout `tunnel-source` note (supersede the 2026-05-27 model; flow-based shared gretap; attribution still deferred).
- `sink/README.md` — new "GRE transport: use a flow-based gretap (multi-tunnel)".
- `CLAUDE.md` — capture-plane limitation note → resolved (coexistence).

**Tests:** 142 passing (was 140; +2).

---

## 2026-06-01 — adapter: per-bridge GRE port name (fix multi-bridge tap collision) + capture-plane multi-tunnel finding

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

`gre_port_name()` returned a hardcoded constant `"gre-out"`. OVS interface names are **globally unique** within one `ovs-vsctl` instance, so the moment a second scenario bridge needs a GRE tunnel, `add-port <bridge2> gre-out` fails with *"gre-out is actually attached to bridge `<bridge1>`"*. Replaced with **`gre_port_for_bridge(bridge_name)`** → `gre_<6hex>` (≤15 chars, underscore not hyphen, deterministic so Model-C check-then-act and the post-start mirror hook agree). `adapter_proxmox/ssh.py`'s `attach-gre-tunnel` and `install-ovs-mirror` pass the bridge; `rest.py` dropped the now-unused import.

This is a real bug for **any deploy with two or more tapped scenario bridges** — not just concurrent scenarios, but also a *single* scenario with 2+ tapped networks (each network → its own OVS bridge → its own GRE port).

**Live trail (pxtwin1, 2026-06-01):** surfaced while running the imagebuild §8 criterion-4 — a second tapped scenario (separate network `corp2`) deployed against the rebuilt `debian-12-cloud` template (620) alongside the existing slice. Before the fix: `attach-gre-tunnel` failed (name collision), 13/25 ops, rest halted. After the fix: **25/25**, both clones cloned from 620 and booted with working networking (`ping 20/20`), mirrors correctly wired (`select tap → output gre_<bridge> → sink`).

**Capture-plane finding (documented, NOT fixed — out of v1 single-scenario scope):**

With the name fix, two scenario bridges coexist, but their mirrored frames still don't *both* reach the sink: **two unkeyed (`key=0`) GRE tunnels to the same sink IP collide at the OVS datapath** — only one egresses. Setting a distinct per-tunnel GRE key fixes egress (verified: 180 frames incl. `GREv0 key=0x4e34 … ICMP 10.20.0.10 > 10.20.0.11` captured leaving the host), **but** the sink's capture iface is a single `key=0` gretap (`tcpdump -i gretap1`, `gretap remote 10.0.0.85 local 10.0.0.86`, no key), so it won't decap keyed traffic. End-to-end multi-tunnel capture therefore needs **per-tunnel GRE keys + a multi-key (or flow-based) sink gretap** — a coordinated adapter+sink change that also implies per-scenario correlation, which `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` / CLAUDE.md explicitly defer for v1 ("no scenario correlation in v1"). Left as a documented limitation for the multi-scenario / multi-network future; **not** changed now, because adding keys unilaterally would break the working single-`key=0` sink. The rebuilt template's clones *were* proven to produce real pcaps end-to-end via a non-destructive temporary key-matched gretap on the sink (`53 ICMP echo frames` captured), then removed.

**Affected code:**

- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/naming.py` — `gre_port_name()` → `gre_port_for_bridge(bridge_name)`.
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/ssh.py` — both GRE call sites pass the bridge; reorder bridge-None check before the fallback.
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/rest.py` — drop unused `gre_port_name` import.
- `tests/test_adapter_proxmox.py` — `test_gre_port_for_bridge_unique_and_safe`.

**Tests:** 140 passing (test updated in place; count unchanged).

## 2026-06-01 — imagebuild slice implemented: `axrange image build` (`axrange/imagebuild/`)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

The authorized imagebuild slice (`docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md` §8) is implemented, committed, unit-tested, **and live-verified end-to-end on `pxtwin1` (PVE 8.4, 2026-06-01)**. New submodule `axrange/imagebuild/` + the `axrange image build <image>` CLI verb reproduce a Proxmox template from an upstream Linux cloud image plus a `qemu-guest-agent` bake, verify it against its manifest, and write the new `template_vmid` into the Proxmox binding. Covered by 34 unit tests (REST+SSH mocked at the transport level, the same way the adapter slice is tested) plus a real-cluster build — see "Live verification" below.

Modules (8, ~1.7k LOC):

- `config.py` — `BuildConfig` layers build-scope settings on the deploy `AdapterConfig` and synthesizes REST/SSH *views* so the pipeline reuses the adapter's `RestClient` + `run_ssh`/`scp_to_remote` verbatim. Every `BUILD_*` key falls back to its deploy-scope counterpart.
- `recipe.py` — parses the binding's new `build:` section (the recipe lives in the binding per `docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md` §5.1; `manifest/` stays build-unaware).
- `cache.py` — fetch + checksum-verify + cache the upstream cloud image on the orchestrator (`~/.axrange/cache/`); `urllib`-only, injectable opener.
- `bake.py` — the cloud-init `#cloud-config` bake render (QGA install + `systemctl add-wants` auto-start + `pkg`/`pkg@version`/inline-`script` provisioning), the seal sequence, and the default per-capability probe table for the closed Linux `provides:` subset.
- `proxmox_build.py` — `BuildProxmox`: build-scope `/cluster/nextid` + guest-agent exec over REST, and the `qm` build-VM lifecycle (create/importdisk/set/start/template/destroy) over SSH; all transports injectable.
- `conformance.py` — the two-layer conformance: per-`provides:` probes via guest agent + the one-host **untapped** deploy smoke-test through the real planner+adapter, with best-effort teardown.
- `binding_io.py` — ruamel round-trip write of `versions[*].storage.template_vmid`, preserving the binding's comments.
- `builder.py` — orchestrates the `docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md` §3 flow with cleanup-on-failure (revert the binding edit + destroy the transient build VM).

Decisions made while building (the `docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md` §9 "resolved by building" items):

- **VM-create / disk-import / template-convert run as `qm` over SSH**, not REST — no portable REST surface, and it mirrors the manual runbook; `/cluster/nextid` + the guest-agent channel use REST. The disk-import verb is **`qm disk import`** (PVE 8.x renamed the old `qm importdisk`; surfaced on pxtwin1 PVE 8.4 — see "Live verification"). Output is parsed for the volid with a fallback to the conventional `<storage>:vm-<vmid>-disk-0` lvmthin name, which made the parse robust regardless of output format.
- **Probe forms** for the closed Linux subset are a built-in table (e.g. `linux` → `uname -s`; `host:uefi` → `/sys/firmware/efi`; `kernel:ebpf` → `/sys/fs/bpf`; `kernel:audit` → `/proc/self/loginuid`; `kernel:container-runtime` → cgroup2 controllers), overridable per-image via `build.probes`.
- **Build-time credential delta** = the `BUILD_*` keys (broader REST token + a `qm`-capable SSH identity, default `root`), all falling back to the deploy-scope creds for single-account labs.
- **Cloud-image source** for the slice = Debian bookworm `latest` generic-cloud + its `SHA512SUMS`.
- **Smoke-test deploys by importing the planner** (not subprocess) against a temp state DB, then tears the clone + throwaway bridge down.
- **Lifecycle for the slice = in-place rebuild of the current version.** The build rewrites the current version's `template_vmid` and leaves the prior template VMID intact on the cluster (still resolvable), keeping the *manifest* untouched (`docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md` §5.1). This makes done-criterion 4 work directly — the existing `@v1` slice fixture resolves to the rebuilt template. A true multi-version bump (append a version + flip the prior `status:` to `deprecated` in the manifest) is the general `docs/IMAGE_MANIFEST.md` §2.5 model and is **deferred** — not needed to reproduce template 610 in place, which is precisely what `docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md` §8 asks for. On conformance failure the binding edit is reverted and the build VM destroyed (`--ignore-conformance` is the debug escape hatch).

**Why:**

`docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md` §9 said these were answered by building, not further design chat. The slice closes the operator-side `qemu-guest-agent` prerequisite `CLAUDE.md` carried: QGA preinstall + auto-start is now a bake step. The static-unit nuance from the 2026-05-29 adapter bring-up (Debian's `qemu-guest-agent.service` has no `[Install]`, so `systemctl enable` is a no-op) is handled with `systemctl add-wants multi-user.target qemu-guest-agent.service`, which is what makes the template's *clones* auto-start QGA.

**Live verification (pxtwin1, PVE 8.4, 2026-06-01):**

`axrange image build debian-12-cloud --build-vmid 620` against a temp copy of the images dir (so the committed fixture stays at 610), credentials from the existing `adapter.conf` (`root@pam` token + `root` SSH), `BUILD_STORAGE=infra-lvm BUILD_BRIDGE=vmbr0`:

- Fetched + sha512-verified the upstream Debian 12 generic-cloud image; staged build VM 620 (i440fx/seabios/virtio-scsi/serial0 — the exact template-610 shape); `qm disk import` of the cloud disk; baked QGA (DHCP lease on `vmbr0` → `apt-get install qemu-guest-agent` over the internet → `systemctl add-wants` autostart) → agent answered → cloud-init done.
- **Conformance: 4/4 capability probes passed** (`linux`, `kernel:ebpf`, `kernel:audit`, `kernel:container-runtime`) against the real Debian 12 kernel; the **one-host untapped smoke-test deployed cleanly (13/13 ops)** — cloned 620 → boot → guest-agent → cloud-init → teardown (clone VM + throwaway bridge destroyed; verified gone on the cluster).
- Sealed; `qm template` 620; binding `template_vmid 610 → 620` in the temp copy. Template 610 left untouched and resolvable; the committed `tests/fixtures` binding unchanged.

This satisfies `docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md` §8 done-criteria 1-3 and the deploy-equivalence core of criterion 4 (the smoke-test clones + boots the rebuilt template through the real planner+adapter path). The tapped-fixture variant of criterion 4 (the slice fixture → 25/25 + pcaps at the sink against the rebuilt template) is an optional additional run.

Two bring-up fixes (both committed here):
- **`qm importdisk` → `qm disk import`** — PVE 8.4 renamed the verb.
- **Configurable bake IP** (`BUILD_IPCONFIG`, default `ip=dhcp`) — the bake needs internet for apt; `vmbr0`/`10.0.0.0/24` has DHCP (template 610 itself boots DHCP), so the default worked.

**Affected code:**

- `axrange/imagebuild/` — new submodule (8 modules).
- `axrange/cli/main.py` — `image build` nested subcommand + result renderer.
- `tests/test_imagebuild.py` — 34 tests (recipe, probes, cache checksum, bake YAML validity, config fallback, binding round-trip, importdisk parse, smoke-scenario validity-and-plans-without-mirror, full builder flow + failure/revert/ignore/clobber-guard paths, CLI wiring).

**Affected docs:**

- `docs/IMAGE_MANIFEST.md` — **new §3.8** documents the binding `build:` recipe field schema (the `implementation (Claude Code)` edit `docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md` §5.1/§9 directed) + a `build` row in the §3.3 table.
- `adapter.conf.example` — `BUILD_*` keys section (all optional, fall back to deploy-scope).
- `tests/fixtures/images/bindings/proxmox/debian-12-cloud.yaml` — a `build:` recipe (Debian 12 generic-cloud + QGA bake).
- `CLAUDE.md` — Current State + Immediate Next Actions reflect the implemented (pending-live-verification) imagebuild slice.

**Tests:** 140 passing (was 106; +34 imagebuild).

**Open / next:** the tapped-fixture variant of done-criterion 4 (slice fixture → 25/25 + pcaps against the rebuilt template — optional); Ubuntu target (structurally identical, unverified); multi-version lifecycle bump + `config`/`role`/`container` provisioning + `--check-upstream` are post-slice refinements.

## 2026-06-01 — Q6 resolved: image-build pipeline is `axrange image build` (CLI subcommand, no CI)

**Authority:** design (chat session)

**What changed:**

Q6 (image / template pipeline) is resolved for v1. The build pipeline is a new submodule `axrange/imagebuild/` exposing an `axrange image build <image>` CLI verb, run from the operator's workstation against the cluster's existing REST + SSH paths. **No CI / Jenkins / build-host in v1** — the pipeline is a checked-in, version-controlled, parameterized script, not standing infrastructure.

Scope and shape:

- **Linux-only for v1.** Builds on top of upstream distro cloud images (e.g. `debian-12-genericcloud-amd64.qcow2`), not from ISO. Windows image building is explicitly out of v1.
- **Build flow:** fetch + checksum upstream cloud image (cached locally) → stage a temporary build VM on the target node (create + import-disk) → boot with a cloud-init bake (preinstall + enable `qemu-guest-agent`; per-image provisioning) → wait for cloud-init via guest-agent → run conformance → seal (`cloud-init clean`, reset machine-id + SSH host keys, clean shutdown) → `qm template` → write the new `template_vmid` into the Proxmox binding.
- **Conformance is two-layer (minimal + smoke-test):** (a) one capability probe per keyword in the manifest's `provides:` set (the closed Linux subset — `linux`, `host:*`, `kernel:*`); (b) an `axrange` smoke-test that deploys a throwaway one-host (untapped) scenario against the freshly-built template through the real planner+adapter path and confirms boot / guest-agent / clean teardown. Both must pass. **Conformance failure fails the build** (the manifest is the contract); `--ignore-conformance` is a debugging-only escape hatch.
- **Cadence: manual.** The operator decides when to rebuild. No scheduled or triggered builds in v1 — that is the door to CI, deferred. An optional `--check-upstream` that *reports* (does not act on) a newer upstream point release is a possible refinement, not a v1 commitment.
- **Build recipe lives in the Proxmox binding, not the manifest.** The binding gains a build-recipe section (base image source, checksum source, bake steps); the manifest is unchanged and stays the pure capability contract. Conformance bridges the two. Lifting the recipe to the platform-independent manifest is deferred to when a second platform arrives (CHANGELOG-able then).
- **Auth:** the build pipeline uses its own credential scope on the orchestrator host — broader than the deploy-time adapter token (it needs VM create / disk import / VM destroy / template conversion). Same orchestrator-side config discipline as the adapter (never in the manifest, binding, or IR). Exact role delta is an implementation finding.
- **Lifecycle integration:** a successful build demotes the prior `template_vmid` for that image to `deprecated` (stays resolvable for older IRs) and makes the new one `current`, per the `current → deprecated → withdrawn` model in `docs/IMAGE_MANIFEST.md`. The operator reviews and commits the binding diff.

**Why:**

- Single-lab + Linux-only + a ~5–10-image catalog does not justify CI infrastructure; it justifies a script. CI's value (many builds, triggers, audit, parallelism) doesn't apply yet, and its cost (a build host's own patching, plugin upkeep, secrets plumbing, coupling the lab cluster to its own image builder) is real. A checked-in CLI verb using the same REST/SSH/config paths as the adapter gets the reproducibility, version control, and audit-via-git that matter at ~10% of the operational surface.
- Building on upstream cloud images rather than from ISO reuses the Debian cloud team's maintained, patched, minimal baseline instead of reinventing it.
- The pipeline closes the operator-side `qemu-guest-agent` prerequisite that `CLAUDE.md` carried: QGA preinstall + auto-start becomes a bake step, not a manual runbook step. The 2026-05-29 manual runbook (untemplate → modify → retemplate) remains documented as the fallback and is precisely what the pipeline automates.
- This was the last open v1 design question. With Q6 resolved, the v1 design surface is fully closed; everything remaining is implementation.

**Affected docs:**

- `docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md` — **new** formal spec (flow, conformance model, lifecycle integration, auth, the authorized first slice §8, open-resolved-by-building items §9).
- `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` — Q6 → RESOLVED (design); "still open" items moved to resolved decisions or deferred follow-ups; note that the v1 design surface is now fully closed.
- `docs/IMAGE_MANIFEST.md` — §5 conformance updated (conformance is in v1, performed by the build pipeline, not Packer); §6 first follow-up (build pipeline format) marked resolved. The concrete binding build-recipe field schema is an `implementation (Claude Code)` edit to §3 when the slice lands.
- `CLAUDE.md` — Current State (Q6 resolved, v1 design closed; QGA-prerequisite note softened to "pipeline owns it, manual runbook is the fallback"); Immediate Next Actions (imagebuild slice authorized; Q6-gated language removed); reading order (adds `docs/IMAGE_BUILD.md` as item 11); Conventions (new `axrange/imagebuild/` submodule + its dependency direction); What NOT to Do (no CI, no Windows, recipe-in-binding, keep the manual runbook).

**Authorized first slice (imagebuild):**

- New submodule `axrange/imagebuild/`. `axrange image build <image>` implementing the flow above; `debian-12-cloud` as the first target (reproduces template 610 from upstream + QGA bake).
- Minimal capability probes + the one-host smoke-test. Reuses the adapter's REST/SSH/config infrastructure.
- **Done-criteria:** `axrange image build debian-12-cloud` produces a template from the upstream image; conformance (probes + smoke-test) passes; the binding's `template_vmid` updates and the prior version demotes to `deprecated`; a subsequent `axrange deploy` of the existing tapped slice fixture against the rebuilt template completes 25/25 with pcaps at the sink.

**Open within Q6 (resolved by building, per the `docs/ADAPTER.md` §7 pattern):**

- Exact REST endpoints for VM-create / disk-import / template-convert.
- Exact per-capability probe command forms.
- The exact build-time REST role delta over the deploy-time adapter token.
- Cloud-image source URLs + checksum sources per distro.
- Ubuntu support (Debian is the slice target; Ubuntu cloud images are structurally identical and should fall out, unverified).
- The concrete binding build-recipe field schema (documented in `docs/IMAGE_MANIFEST.md` §3 when the slice lands).
