## 2026-05-29 — `axrange deploy` resume-from-state-DB

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

`axrange deploy` is now resumable. Re-running it against an unchanged scenario picks up the existing deploy in the state DB instead of failing on a primary-key conflict (the previous behavior required `rm ~/.axrange/state.db` between runs).

Semantics:

- **Re-run against a `completed` deploy** → CLI reports `Scenario already deployed: <deploy_id>` and exits 0. Use `--fresh` if you genuinely want to re-deploy (e.g. cluster state was destroyed externally).
- **Re-run against a `running` / `failed` / `halted` deploy** → resume the same `deploy_id`, reset all non-`completed` ops back to `pending`, and walk the topo order. Completed ops are skipped (no adapter call, no per-op retry); the executor pre-loads their outputs from the state store so downstream consumers don't round-trip the DB for each input lookup. Failed/halted ops re-run normally.
- **`--fresh`** → force a brand-new `deploy_id` even if a prior deploy exists. This is the "I know what I'm doing, start over" escape hatch.

This implements the resume-from-partial path that `docs/PLANNER.md` §5.4 always said was first-class ("A partial deploy is a normal state, not a weird one … Re-running `axrange deploy` resumes from where the previous attempt left off"). Until now the executor's design supported it but the CLI didn't wire it up — every run created a fresh `deploy_id`, which collided with the deterministic `op_id`s of the prior run on `persist_plan`.

Implementation:

- `StateStore.find_deploy_for_plan(plan_id)` — most recent deploy for a given plan_id, the resume substrate.
- `StateStore.reset_incomplete_ops(deploy_id)` — flip every non-`completed` op back to `pending` for a clean re-walk. Returns the count for user-visible logging.
- `StateStore.insert_ops` is now `INSERT OR IGNORE` — re-`persist_plan` after resume is a no-op for existing rows, lets the rare new op (e.g. someone added a host to the IR mid-flight) slip in cleanly.
- `Executor.run` checks each op's state-DB status before dispatching to the adapter; rows in `completed` state become zero-attempt outcomes with their stored outputs.
- `Executor._prime_completed_outputs` pre-fills `self._outputs` from the DB once at start of `run`, so input resolution for ops downstream of completed ones doesn't issue N SQLite SELECTs.
- `cmd_deploy` is auto-resume by default with the explicit `--fresh` opt-out.

**Live verification on `pxtwin1`:**

1. `axrange deploy <slice>` (resumes existing completed deploy) → reports already-deployed, exits 0.
2. `axrange deploy <slice> --fresh` → creates new deploy, 25/25 ops complete, COMPLETED.
3. `axrange deploy <slice>` again → finds the new completed deploy, reports already-deployed, exits 0.

**Affected code:**

- `axrange/planner/state.py` — `find_deploy_for_plan`, `reset_incomplete_ops`, `INSERT OR IGNORE` on `insert_ops`.
- `axrange/planner/executor.py` — completed-op skip in `run`; `_prime_completed_outputs`.
- `axrange/cli/main.py` — auto-resume in `cmd_deploy`; `--fresh` flag.
- `tests/test_planner_state.py` — 3 new tests covering `find_deploy_for_plan`, idempotent `insert_ops`, and `reset_incomplete_ops` preserving completed rows.
- `tests/test_planner_executor.py` — 3 new tests covering skip-already-completed, partial-failure-then-resume, and `_prime_completed_outputs`.

**Tests:** 106 passing (was 100; +6 covering the resume paths).

---

## 2026-05-29 — Sink: re-tighten sandboxing with a focused syscall allowlist

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

`sink/systemd/capture.service` re-enables both `MemoryDenyWriteExecute=true` and `SystemCallFilter=@system-service / ~@privileged @resources @debug @mount`, then adds a focused re-allow line for the specific syscalls tcpdump actually needs:

```
SystemCallFilter=@chown bpf setuid setgid setgroups capset
```

The earlier slice MVP commit shipped with both directives cleared as a known security regression after the first bring-up showed coreutils 9 `install -d -o capture` getting SIGSYS. That diagnosis was incomplete: `install -d` was actually fine, and the SIGSYS was happening one step later in `tcpdump` itself. Binary-search testing on the live sink VM (Debian 12 / glibc 2.36, the bring-up target) traced the offender to the `@privileged` group, where tcpdump needs:

- `bpf` for kernel-side packet filter programs (modern eBPF path)
- `setuid` / `setgid` / `setgroups` / `capset` for the `-Z $CAPTURE_USER` privilege drop
- `@chown` for the pcap files post-drop-priv

Live-verified end-to-end: `systemctl restart capture.service` with the new unit → `active` immediately, tcpdump listening on `gretap1`, 50 pings h-a → h-b → pcap file grew to 12 KB. `systemctl show capture.service --property=SystemCallFilter` confirms the full hardening is in effect (the four denied categories minus the six explicitly re-allowed syscalls).

**Why:**

Closes the v1.1 follow-up flagged in the 2026-05-29 "Sink runbook fixes from real-cluster bring-up" CHANGELOG entry. The hardening is back to the ratified level, with the smallest possible exception surface — six syscall names, each documented inline with the tcpdump behavior that needs them.

**Affected files:**

- `sink/systemd/capture.service` — `MemoryDenyWriteExecute=true` restored; `SystemCallFilter` allowlist starts at `@system-service`, denies `@privileged @resources @debug @mount`, re-allows the six tcpdump-specific syscalls. Inline comments explain each.

100 pytest tests passing.

---

## 2026-05-29 — `apply-sdn-config`: re-assert VM taps on `vbr_*` bridges after host network reload

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

`adapter_proxmox/rest.py:op_apply_sdn_config` now follows its `PUT /nodes/<node>/network` call with an SSH-driven scan that re-asserts every running VM's tap iface on every axrange-managed (`vbr_*`) OVS bridge it should be on. The scan walks `ovs-vsctl list-br | grep '^vbr_'`, iterates the running VMs from `qm list`, parses their net<i> config for `bridge=vbr_*` references, and `ovs-vsctl --may-exist add-port` with the right `tag=<vlan>` for any tap that's missing.

The problem: PVE's `ifreload -a` (triggered by the network apply) momentarily down/up's OVS bridges. PVE's `pve-bridge` hook only runs at VM start, so on a re-deploy where Model C adopts already-running VMs, the taps stay detached after the reload — ping between scenario hosts silently breaks even though the deploy reports COMPLETED. Surfaced 2026-05-29 right after the same-day MVP demo; documented as a manual workaround in that day's "Slice MVP end-to-end on pxtwin1" CHANGELOG entry. This commit closes it.

Scope is limited to `vbr_*` bridges (axrange's naming convention) so the scan never touches `vmbr0` or any other operator bridge. Running VMs that don't reference an axrange bridge in their net config are ignored. Idempotent on a healthy bridge (the `list-ports | grep -qx` check skips taps that are already present).

**Why:**

Closes the third of the three manual workarounds called out in the 2026-05-29 "Slice MVP end-to-end" entry, alongside the same-day mirror-at-deploy-time fix and the template-rebuild-with-QGA-preinstalled operator action. axrange now drives the slice end-to-end on `pxtwin1` with no manual intervention required: redeploy → run ping → see pcaps grow.

**Live verification:** detached `tap607525i0` from `vbr_corp`, invoked `op_apply_sdn_config` against the live REST + SSH backends, tap reappeared on the bridge; ping h-a → h-b succeeded immediately afterward.

**Affected code:**

- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/rest.py:RestBackend.op_apply_sdn_config` — added SSH-driven post-apply tap re-assert.

100 pytest tests still passing.

---

## 2026-05-29 — OVS mirror hook: `add bridge mirrors`, not `set bridge mirrors=` (fix multi-host collision)

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

The post-start hook script template in `adapter_proxmox/ssh.py:_HOOK_SCRIPT_TEMPLATE` now uses `add bridge "$bridge" mirrors @m` (append) instead of `set bridge "$bridge" mirrors=@m` (replace). The pre-stop branch now also calls `ovs-vsctl remove bridge "$bridge" mirrors <uuid>` before destroying the Mirror row, so the bridge's `mirrors` column never holds dangling UUIDs.

The original `set` form unconditionally replaces the entire `mirrors=...` column on the bridge. With multiple tapped hosts on the same bridge (the normal slice case — two tapped Linux hosts on `vbr_corp`), the *second* VM's post-start hook overwrote the *first* VM's mirror, leaving only one host's traffic mirrored to the sink. The slice MVP demo masked this because the surviving mirror captured both directions of traffic between the two hosts on the same subnet, but a real scenario with three or more tapped hosts (or asymmetric paths) would silently lose coverage.

Symmetric cleanup in pre-stop is the same idea in reverse: PVE 8.x's OVS doesn't always cascade-delete the bridge ref when you destroy a Mirror row, leaving dangling UUIDs that `ovs-vsctl list-mirrors` reports as warnings.

**Why:**

Surfaced 2026-05-29 right after the template-rebuild verification deploy: only `mir-851340` appeared on `vbr_corp` even though both VMs were freshly started and both hooks ran. Manual `set bridge ... mirrors=@m` from each hook was overwriting the previous one. Live-verified the fix by destroying both mirrors and running the corrected hooks against the live cluster: both mirrors persisted.

**Affected code:**

- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/ssh.py:_HOOK_SCRIPT_TEMPLATE` — `add` not `set`, plus explicit remove-then-destroy in both branches.

100 pytest tests still passing. The deploy-time check-then-act path (the previous CHANGELOG entry) already used the correct `add` form; only the hook generated for first-time-boot mirror creation was affected.

---

## 2026-05-29 — `install-ovs-mirror`: assert mirror at deploy time, not only at boot

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

`adapter_proxmox/ssh.py:_install_ovs_mirror` now asserts the OVS mirror exists in-band when the VM is already running, in addition to installing the post-start/pre-stop hook script for future boots.

Previously the op only installed the hook. The hook fires on PVE's `post-start` event, which means on a re-deploy where Model C adopts an already-running VM (start-vm is a no-op), the hook never fires and the mirror stays absent — pcaps silently stop flowing even though the deploy reports COMPLETED. Surfaced on the 2026-05-29 pxtwin1 bring-up: after the slice deploy adopted previously-cloned VMs, the mirror wasn't recreated and the operator had to `ovs-vsctl ... create mirror ... add bridge ... mirrors @m` by hand.

The new check-then-act path:

1. Install the hook + register via `qm set --hookscript` (unchanged).
2. `qm status <vmid>`; if `stopped`, leave mirror creation to the hook on next boot.
3. If `running`, `ovs-vsctl --columns=name find Mirror name=mir-<vmid>` — if the named mirror is already present, no-op (Model C "resource exists in expected end-state"); if absent, create it with the same `select-dst-port=@p select-src-port=@p output-port=@g` form the hook uses, and `add bridge <bridge> mirrors @m` (add, not set — preserves other tapped hosts' mirrors).

The mirror name is deterministic per VMID (`mir-<vmid>`) so the lookup is exact and the create is collision-free across hosts on the same bridge.

**Why:**

Captured as a manual workaround in the same-day "Slice MVP end-to-end on pxtwin1" CHANGELOG entry; this commit closes that gap. Mirror coverage is a deploy-time correctness property — the slice's done-criteria 4 (pcaps grow under traffic) depends on the mirror being present whenever the VM is running, regardless of whether this deploy started the VM or adopted an already-running one.

**Live verification:** removed `mir-607525` from `vbr_corp` and destroyed it; invoked `_install_ovs_mirror` against the live SSH backend with `(vmid=607525, bridge=vbr_corp, gre_port=gre-out)`; mirror reappeared on the bridge. Subsequent invocations are no-ops (the check-then-act path catches the present mirror).

**Affected code:**

- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/ssh.py:SshBackend._install_ovs_mirror` — new check-then-act block after hook install.

100 pytest tests still passing.

---

## 2026-05-29 — Slice MVP end-to-end on pxtwin1: refinements from real-cluster bring-up

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

Real-cluster bring-up of the planner+adapter slice (`docs/ADAPTER.md` §6) against the operator's `pxtwin1` lab cluster (PVE 8.4, infra-lvm storage, Debian 12 generic-cloud template VMID 610). All five §6 done-criteria now pass end-to-end: validate, plan (21 ops + 4 barriers + 56 edges), deploy (25 ops completed, 0 failed), ping h-a↔h-b at the VLAN-10 OVS bridge, pcap growing at the capture sink. Eight discrete code/spec issues surfaced; the load-bearing fixes ship in this commit (`axrange`) and the companion `sink:` commit alongside.

`axrange` adapter / planner fixes:

- **SDN bypass for the slice** (`adapter_proxmox/rest.py`). The OP_CATALOG §5.1 model — "one OVS bridge per scenario network" — does not fit PVE SDN's VLAN-zone model, which requires a single underlay bridge specified at zone creation. The slice now treats `create-sdn-zone` and `create-vnet` as logical no-ops returning synthetic ids (zone_id, vnet_id=bridge_name), with the actual OVS bridge created directly via `/nodes/<node>/network` (`create-ovs-bridge`) and committed by `apply-sdn-config`'s `PUT /nodes/<node>/network`. Operators see scenario bridges via `pvesh get /nodes/pxtwin1/network` instead of `/cluster/sdn`. Catalog signatures unchanged — only the implementation of `infra`-class plumbing changes.
- **VLAN tagging at NIC attach** (`adapter_proxmox/rest.py`, `planner/pass1_emit.py`). With SDN bypassed, the per-net VLAN is applied at `attach-vm-to-vnet` time via `tag=<vlan>` on the VM NIC. Pass-1 plumbs the network's `vlan` field through to the op's `inputs_resolved`.
- **Bridge naming corrected for PVE iface regex** (`adapter_proxmox/naming.py`). PVE rejects iface names with hyphens (`[a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9_]+` per `PVE::Network`), so `bridge_name_for_net` now substitutes non-alphanumerics with underscore and prefixes `vbr_`. Updated test_adapter_proxmox accordingly (added `test_bridge_name_no_hyphens`).
- **Machine type `pc` (i440fx) for Debian generic-cloud** (`tests/fixtures/images/bindings/proxmox/debian-12-cloud.yaml`). PVE's Q35 machine type emulates IDE via ICH9 AHCI in a way the Debian 6.1 cloud kernel doesn't expose as `/dev/sr0` — so `ds-identify` can't find the cidata, the systemd generator masks all cloud-init services, and the entire pipeline is silent. Switching the binding to `machine: pc` makes `/dev/sr0` show up and unblocks the whole sequence.
- **`/agent/ping` is POST, not GET** (`adapter_proxmox/rest.py`). PVE returns HTTP 501 "Method not implemented" for GET; my code silently retried until the 180s timeout fired three times, then halted the deploy. Fixed `op_wait_guest_agent` and the phase-1 ping in `op_wait_os_ready`.
- **`/agent/exec` array body serialization** (`adapter_proxmox/rest.py`). `RestClient.request` now flattens list-valued body keys into repeated `key=val` pairs so PVE's array-typed parameters (e.g. `command=cloud-init&command=status&command=--wait`) serialize correctly.
- **Model C drift detection on `attach-gre-tunnel`** (`adapter_proxmox/ssh.py`). Re-deploys after an operator updates `SINK_TRANSPORT_IP` in `adapter.conf` now detect the `options:remote_ip` drift on the existing `gre-out` port and update in place rather than no-op'ing into a stale destination.
- **Cloud-init user-data snippet via `cicustom`** (`adapter_proxmox/rest.py`, `adapter_proxmox/config.py`, `planner/pass1_emit.py`). `op_configure_cloud_init` now renders a per-VM `#cloud-config` snippet (`hostname`, sudo'd user with the binding's `cloud_init.user`, ssh authorized_keys, `runcmd` to `systemctl start qemu-guest-agent`), `scp`s it to `/var/lib/vz/snippets/`, and sets `cicustom=user=local:snippets/...` on the VM config. Pass-1 now also threads the network's cidr+gateway through to `ipconfig0` (previously empty `gw=`). New optional `GUEST_SSH_PUBKEY_FILE` field in `AdapterConfig` controls which pubkey gets injected (distinct from `OVS_SSH_KEY` which authenticates to the Proxmox host).

`adapter.conf` and `state.db` are now in `.gitignore` to keep operator-local secrets and stateful files out of version control.

**Manual workarounds NOT captured in code** (documented here for the next operator):

- The template VMID 610 ships `qemu-guest-agent` *installed* but not in `multi-user.target.wants` and not running. Cloud-init's `systemctl start qemu-guest-agent` would normally fix this, but the slice template package isn't there at all — the deb wasn't pre-installed in the user's template. On a real-cluster bring-up the operator needs to either (a) rebuild the template with `qemu-guest-agent` pre-installed and enabled, or (b) attach a temporary route to the scenario network and `apt install qemu-guest-agent` once. The slice MVP used (b) plus `cloud-init clean` + re-run of modules:final to clear the runcmd-failed state so `wait-os-ready` saw `cloud-init status: done`. Tracking as a template-side runbook item, not an axrange fix.
- After `apply-sdn-config`'s `PUT /nodes/<node>/network`, the host re-applies its network config which can momentarily detach scenario VM NICs from the OVS bridge. Re-adding via `ovs-vsctl --may-exist add-port ... tag=<vlan>` restores them. A small adapter refinement (`apply-sdn-config` re-asserts each scenario host's tap iface on the bridge after the network apply settles) would close this without operator intervention.
- The OVS mirrors built by the post-start/pre-stop hook fire only at VM start. On a re-deploy where Model C adopts already-running VMs, the hook never runs and the mirror is absent. The slice MVP created the mirrors manually via `ovs-vsctl ... add bridge ... mirrors @m`. A correct fix: `install-ovs-mirror` should also assert the mirror exists at deploy time, not only at boot.

**Why:**

These are exactly the `docs/ADAPTER.md` §7 "empirical refinements" the slice was designed to surface. Eight bugs, six of which are real-cluster-only (no unit test would have caught them) and two of which (`agent/ping POST`, bridge naming) are testable but I didn't model the PVE behavior closely enough up front. All landed as `implementation (Claude Code)` Authority — no design pivots required.

**Affected code:**

- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/rest.py` — SDN bypass; VLAN tag; agent ping POST; cloud-init snippet renderer; agent/exec list-body serialization.
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/ssh.py` — `attach-gre-tunnel` drift detection.
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/naming.py` — underscore-separator bridge names.
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/config.py` — `GUEST_SSH_PUBKEY_FILE`.
- `axrange/planner/pass1_emit.py` — ip_config enrichment; VLAN tag plumbing; ci_user.
- `tests/fixtures/images/bindings/proxmox/debian-12-cloud.yaml` — `machine: pc`.
- `tests/test_adapter_proxmox.py` — bridge-name underscore test.
- `adapter.conf.example` — `GUEST_SSH_PUBKEY_FILE` placeholder.
- `.gitignore` — `adapter.conf`, `state.db`, `.axrange/`.

100 pytest tests passing.

## 2026-05-29 — Sink runbook fixes from real-cluster bring-up

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**

Two sink-side fixes uncovered during the slice MVP bring-up (companion to the same-day `axrange` entry above):

- **`tcpdump -W 0` → `-W 999999999`** (`sink/bin/capture-start`, `docs/sink/README.md` §4 + §10). The spec ratified `-W 0` for "unlimited rotation" — historically tcpdump treated 0 as a sentinel that preserved the strftime template in `-w`. tcpdump 4.99+ (Debian 12 default) rejects 0 with `invalid number of output files 0`. A very large finite value behaves unlimited for any realistic deploy *and* keeps the strftime regime, so the slice MVP ships `-W 999999999`. The "do not break the strftime template" rule is unchanged — only the magic value moves. Spec text updated to match.
- **systemd sandboxing relaxed for Debian 12 glibc** (`sink/systemd/capture.service`). The ratified hardening (`SystemCallFilter=@system-service / ~@privileged @resources @debug @mount` + `MemoryDenyWriteExecute=true`) blocks coreutils 9's `install -d -o capture` on Debian 12 glibc 2.36 — the process gets SIGSYS (status 159) and the unit crashes in a restart loop. Both filters disabled for the slice MVP with TODO comments noting the right next step (narrow which syscalls are denied, don't drop the filter category wholesale). This is a known security regression captured here so it doesn't slip; a focused syscall allowlist test is the v1.1 work.

**Why:**

Both bugs are first-real-deploy failures of the sink — pre-existing in the committed code, not introduced by the slice. The choice to relax sandboxing rather than diagnose-and-allowlist is a deliberate slice-MVP-vs-hardening tradeoff: the slice needs the sink running today; the hardening can be re-tightened in a follow-up without changing the contract.

**Affected files:**

- `sink/bin/capture-start` — `-W 999999999`, expanded comment.
- `sink/systemd/capture.service` — `MemoryDenyWriteExecute=false`, `SystemCallFilter=` cleared; explanatory comments.
- `docs/sink/README.md` §4 + §10 — updated value + rationale.

## 2026-05-28 — Planner execution layer + Proxmox adapter: first slice implemented

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- New submodule `axrange/planner/` implementing the slice subset of the planner per `docs/PLANNER.md` and `docs/ADAPTER.md` §6:
  - **DAG types** (`dag.py`): `Plan`, `OpInstance`, `BarrierInstance`, `Edge`, deterministic `op_id` derivation (`<plan_id>:<op-name><scope>`), `derive_plan_id`/`hash_ir` for plan-id determinism, Kahn-topo with stable wave ordering, descendants for halt-subtree.
  - **Pass-1 emission** (`pass1_emit.py`): walks IR + image bindings + cluster ctx; emits the 16-op slice subset (all 4 OP-NET; 7 OP-HOST infra ops; both OP-MIR; 3 OP-BAR). Rejects out-of-slice IR up front (routers/switches, apps, overlays, activity, missing sink IP when tapped). Resolves manifest-side inputs at plan time; defers output-derived inputs to executor time.
  - **Pass-2 dataflow** (`pass2_dataflow.py`): one edge per `inputs_from_outputs` entry.
  - **Pass-3 ordering** (`pass3_ordering.py`): catalog `after:` clause resolution with `[$var]` / `[<x>@<y>]` / `[*]` / `[$var@*]` pattern grammar; barrier predecessor wiring; the OP_CATALOG §5.3 conditional `start-vm` ← `install-ovs-mirror` + `attach-gre-tunnel` for tapped hosts.
  - **Pass-4 placement** (`pass4_placement.py`): trivial single-node placement; the union-cap satisfiability algorithm of `docs/PLANNER.md` §3.4 is deferred to a later slice.
  - **Pass-5 DAG validation** (`validation.py`): cycle, reachability, type consistency (producer existence), placement satisfiability.
  - **State store** (`state.py`): SQLite-backed `deploys` / `ops` / `op_attempts` per `docs/PLANNER.md` §7.2 with atomic op-completion per §7.3. Deferred: `leases`, `placement` JSON projection beyond inline, re-plan / plan-diff. The slice schema is also the `axrange status` surface.
  - **Sequential executor** (`executor.py`): topological walk, per-op input resolution (`inputs_resolved` + lookups from prior outputs), retry loop with exponential backoff (default 5s → 15s → 45s, ±10% jitter; `RetryConfig` overrides), halt-subtree on terminal failure, barrier short-circuit when preds halted. `Adapter` protocol + `AdapterError`/`AdapterTimeout`. Concurrency semaphores and the escalate walk-back are deferred per `docs/ADAPTER.md` §6.
- New submodule `axrange/adapter_proxmox/` implementing the adapter contract per `docs/ADAPTER.md` §3–§5:
  - **One interface, two backends + one probe** (`adapter.py`): `ProxmoxAdapter.run()` routes by op name to REST (10 ops), SSH (2 ops), or guest-probe (1 op).
  - **REST backend** (`rest.py`): stdlib-only HTTP (urllib + custom SSL ctx); `RestClient` with token auth and `wait_task` UPID polling; one method per REST-routed op, each implementing Model C check-then-act via SDN/VM `GET` probes before mutation.
  - **SSH backend** (`ssh.py`): shells out to `ssh`/`scp` (no paramiko dep). `attach-gre-tunnel` calls `ovs-vsctl --may-exist add-port … type=gre options:remote_ip=…`; `install-ovs-mirror` scps a deterministic per-VM hook script to `/var/lib/vz/snippets/` and registers it via `qm set <vmid> --hookscript`. The hook body is the post-start/pre-stop pattern from `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §288.
  - **Guest probe** (`probe_os_ready` in `ssh.py`): orchestrator-side SSH check against the guest's mgmt IP, bounded by `first_boot_budget`. Linux-only in the slice; Windows/WinRM path noted as a follow-up.
  - **Deterministic naming** (`naming.py`): Model C names — 6-char plan-id slug; VMID in 100000–999999; 8-char SDN zone/vnet ids; ≤15-char OVS bridge names (IFNAMSIZ); 63-char VM display names.
  - **Config** (`config.py`): `AdapterConfig` + `load_config` with precedence overrides > env > `adapter.conf` > defaults; `adapter.conf.example` ships at the repo root.
- CLI extended with three new subcommands (`axrange/cli/main.py`): `plan`, `deploy`, `status`, `list-deploys`. Shared planning preamble: validate → load adapter config → build `ClusterContext` → `build_plan`. `--sink-ip` and `--node` flags override `adapter.conf`.
- Slice fixture at `tests/fixtures/ir/valid_slice/scenario.yaml` — two tapped Linux hosts on one network, matching the `docs/ADAPTER.md` §6 done-criteria scenario.
- 40 new pytest tests across `tests/test_planner_dag.py`, `tests/test_planner_executor.py`, `tests/test_planner_state.py`, `tests/test_adapter_proxmox.py`. Total suite: 99 passing.

**Validates done-criteria 1 + 2 from `docs/ADAPTER.md` §6:**
- `axrange validate tests/fixtures/ir/valid_slice/scenario.yaml --images tests/fixtures` → PASS.
- `axrange plan tests/fixtures/ir/valid_slice/scenario.yaml --images tests/fixtures --sink-ip … --node …` → 21 ops + 4 barriers + 56 edges; correct ordering (clone-vm parallel to SDN; attach-vm-to-vnet joining on `network-ready`; tapped `start-vm` gated on `install-ovs-mirror` + `attach-gre-tunnel`; `mirror-ready` collecting all GRE tunnels + mirror hooks).
- Done-criteria 3-5 (real deploy + manual `ping`/`iperf` + pcaps + `axrange status`) require a live Proxmox cluster and the operator's Proxmox API token (not yet captured); pending the operator wiring `adapter.conf` and the demo run.

**Why:**
- This is the implementation of the slice authorized in the same-day "Q5 resolved" entry — the smallest end-to-end thread that demonstrates tap + capture, built planner + adapter together so no stub layer ships untested.
- Stdlib-only HTTP + shelling out to `ssh`/`scp` keeps the install footprint minimal (no new package dependencies) and uses the operator's existing key material as-is.
- Catalog signatures are the source of truth: Pass-1 reads `inputs`/`outputs` from `axrange/ops/registry.py`; Pass-3 reads `after:` clauses from the same; Pass-1 emission is the only place where catalog rules are interpreted, not duplicated.
- Sequential execution + N-retry covers everything the slice's done-criteria need; the deferred concurrency machinery (semaphore-per-resource, etc.) is genuinely orthogonal and lands in a later slice with no rewrite of what's here.

**Affected code:**
- `axrange/planner/` — new (10 files).
- `axrange/adapter_proxmox/` — new (6 files) + `adapter.conf.example` at repo root.
- `axrange/cli/main.py` — extended with `plan`, `deploy`, `status`, `list-deploys`; existing `validate` surface unchanged.
- `tests/test_planner_dag.py`, `tests/test_planner_executor.py`, `tests/test_planner_state.py`, `tests/test_adapter_proxmox.py` — new.
- `tests/fixtures/ir/valid_slice/scenario.yaml` — new.

**Open within the slice (resolved by deploy, per `docs/ADAPTER.md` §7):**
- Exact REST endpoints/parameters per op — first implementation is plausible but unverified against PVE 7/8 differences; corrections will be `implementation (Claude Code)` follow-ups during real-cluster bring-up.
- `ovs-vsctl add-port` form for `attach-gre-tunnel` and hook script body for `install-ovs-mirror` — written to ARCHITECTURE §288/§299; real cluster will surface any tweaks.
- SSH connection reuse — slice opens a fresh `ssh` subprocess per op (correct but per-op connection setup cost); pooling is a performance refinement, not a correctness gap.

## 2026-05-28 — Q5 resolved; planner-execution + Proxmox-adapter slice authorized

**Authority:** design (chat session)

**What changed:**
- **Q5 (Proxmox adapter) design is resolved.** New formal spec `docs/ADAPTER.md`. Ratified decisions:
  - **Transport split:** REST (Proxmox API) for everything the API supports; SSH for the OVS/mirror plane (`ovs-vsctl`, hook-script install) because Proxmox SDN does not expose OVS mirror config via REST (`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §3). Forced, not preferred.
  - **One adapter interface, two backends** (`rest`, `ssh`); the planner calls `adapter.run(op, binding, ctx)` and does not know the transport.
  - **Op → capability → backend mapping** for all v1 `infra` ops (`docs/ADAPTER.md` §3).
  - **Auth:** limited-role Proxmox API token for REST; dedicated `ovs-operator` SSH key for SSH ops; credentials on the orchestrator host, never in the IR/state store. Rotation explicitly out of v1 scope.
  - **Idempotency:** the adapter signs up to PLANNER §4.2 Model C (deterministic naming from `{plan_id, op_id}`, check-then-act, cleanup-on-partial) for every `infra` op.
- **The Q5 gate is lifted on the planner execution layer + Proxmox adapter, to be built together** — exactly the joint slice the 2026-05-27 gate was holding for ("planner and adapter are to be built together once Q5 ratifies"). User authorized this in the 2026-05-28 chat session.
- **First slice scoped** (`docs/ADAPTER.md` §6): the smallest end-to-end thread that demonstrates tap + capture — two tapped Linux hosts on one network, validate → plan → deploy to one Proxmox node → manual `ping`/`iperf` → pcaps at the sink. 16 of 31 ops (all OP-NET, 7 OP-HOST, both OP-MIR, 3 OP-BAR); all `infra`-class, so no Ansible path needed. Planner-execution subset: sequential execution (semaphores deferred), no lease/lock, no plan-diff, no escalate machinery; minimal-but-real state store (`deploys`/`ops`/`op_attempts` with atomic completion); trivial single-node placement.

**Why:**
- Q5's remaining items (REST-vs-SSH, auth, op-by-op mapping) were largely pre-decided by ARCHITECTURE §3 and IMAGE_MANIFEST; resolving them in one pass lets the joint slice start. The op-by-op API/`ovs-vsctl` call details are genuinely empirical and are answered by building the slice, not by more design chat.
- Building the planner execution layer and adapter together (not the planner against a stub) was the explicit purpose of the 2026-05-27 gate. With Q5 resolved, that condition is met.
- A thin vertical slice (deferring concurrency, leases, re-planning, escalation — the heaviest planner machinery) converts a stalled design track into something the operator can see running, which was the session's stated goal.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/ADAPTER.md` — new formal spec (Q5 resolution + slice authorization).
- `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` — Q5 marked resolved (design); Q1 and Q8 moved to out-of-v1-scope (see companion entry below).
- `CLAUDE.md` — Current State (planner-execution + adapter no longer gated; slice authorized), Immediate Next Actions (rewritten for the adapter slice), reading order (adds `docs/ADAPTER.md`), What NOT to Do (planner-execution/adapter lines removed).

**Supersedes:** the implementation-authorization state set by the 2026-05-27 "Clarify implementation authorization" entry, specifically the part holding the planner execution layer + Proxmox adapter gated on Q5. The gate is now lifted with Q5 resolved; this does not reverse the *reason* for the gate (don't build the planner against a stub) — it satisfies it.

The rule *catalog itself* (IDs, severities, passes) is unchanged; only the count headline is corrected.

## 2026-05-28 — `axrange/` package: IR parser + validator + op registry + CLI scaffold

**Authority:** implementation (Claude Code)

**What changed:**
- New top-level Python package `axrange/` (Python 3.11+, Pydantic v2, ruamel.yaml, argparse). Console entry point `axrange` exposed via `pyproject.toml`. Layout:
  - `axrange/ir/`        — typed IR models (Pydantic v2), capability/action closed sets, YAML loader with strict-no-override includes splice.
  - `axrange/manifest/`  — typed manifest + per-platform binding models, `ManifestRegistry` directory loader.
  - `axrange/ops/`       — the op catalog encoded as `OpDefinition`/`Barrier` dataclasses; 26 action/wait ops + 5 barriers = 31, per `docs/OP_CATALOG.md`. Illustrative `implementation:` refs are intentionally **omitted** (gated on Q5 Proxmox adapter).
  - `axrange/validator/` — rule catalog as `Rule` dataclasses, `checks/<category>.py` for each of STR/INC/REF/CAP/LIF/NET/OVR/ACT/DEF, four-pass engine, four entry points (`validate_structural`, `validate_full`, `validate_with_cluster`, `explain_rule`).
  - `axrange/cli/`       — `axrange validate <scenario.yaml>` with `--explain <RULE-ID>` and `--rules`; output format per `docs/VALIDATOR.md` §8.
- 59 pytest tests covering: catalog integrity, op-registry invariants (only `run-script` is `escalate`; `infra` ops never use `ansible-pool`; `config` ops always do), IR parser (includes, INC-010/020/040), each STR/REF/Pass-3 rule category with at least one fail-path and the rptoe promotion ladder.
- Fixtures: passing `minimal.yaml` and `ransomware-hospital-corrected.yaml`; deliberately-failing `ransomware-hospital-as-specced.yaml` (verbatim from IR_SCHEMA §10) demonstrating the dangling-defense-host REF-070 failure.

**Why:**
- The validator is the v1 implementation slice CLAUDE.md cleared on 2026-05-27. The IR parser is in-scope with it (validator's required input). The op registry is the smallest planner-adjacent piece cleared independently of Q5 — it's pure data (signature dataclasses), no execution layer, and gives both the validator's CAP/REF cross-checks and the future planner a concrete operand set to build against.
- Single-package monorepo (`axrange/` with `ir/`, `validator/`, `ops/`, `cli/` submodules) keeps the import graph tight as the planner and adapter come online into the same package; matches the `axrange validate` / `axrange deploy` CLI surface specified in PLANNER §6.3.
- Python 3.11+ with Pydantic v2 / ruamel.yaml / argparse — ubiquitous on Debian/Ubuntu (matches sink target OS), no compile step, fast iteration on the ~80-rule catalog.

**Affected docs:**
- None — this entry records new code, not a doc change. (See companion `docs:` correction below to VALIDATOR.md / IR_SCHEMA.md for issues surfaced *during* implementation.)

**Links:**

- Open follow-ups (implementation-Authority, CHANGELOG-able when done):

## 2026-05-27 — Op catalog v1

**Authority:** design (chat session)

**What changed:**
- New ratified spec `docs/OP_CATALOG.md`: the closed set of planner op types for v1 — **26 action/wait ops + 5 barriers = 31 op types** across 7 categories (`OP-NET` 4, `OP-HOST` 8, `OP-MIR` 2, `OP-APP` 3, `OP-OVL` 6, `OP-ACT` 3, `OP-BAR` 5). Each op fully specified: `id`, `name`, `kind`, `inputs`/`outputs`, `after`, `cleanup_strategy`, `idempotency_class`, `resource_categories`, `redo_on`. Resolves the op-catalog deliverable deferred in PLANNER §2.3/§11.
- **Op-definition schema extended:** added `resource_categories` to PLANNER §2.3 (promoted from §8.1, which previously described categories without making each op declare them); added two catalog-side fields `id` (stable `OP-<CAT>-<NNN>` handle) and `redo_on` (IR field paths that redo an op — makes PLANNER §9.2's deferred redo-rules table a *derived view* rather than a second source of truth).
- **New semaphore `gretap:<sink>`** added to PLANNER §8.1/§8.2 (fixed limit 1) — serializes kernel netlink ops on a sink's gretap interface; used by `OP-MIR`.
- **`idempotency_class` confirmed at two values** (`infra`/`config`); `wait`/`barrier` ops omit it (corrects an earlier chat-session proposal of four values that contradicted ratified PLANNER §2.3).

**Key per-op decisions (deviations/inferences flagged):**
- `create-ovs-bridge` split from `create-vnet` — refines PLANNER §3.1's "1 op per network" to 2 (OVS-bridge creation is a distinct non-negotiable side effect, ARCHITECTURE §250). Bridge materializes cluster-wide → declares only `proxmox-cluster`.
- No `create-transport-vnet` op — `vnet-transport` is pre-existing cluster infra; its absence is a CLU validator failure.
- `provision-app` is **one coarse op per app** bundling its declarative install actions via one Ansible convergence — deliberate deviation from PLANNER §2.1's one-op-per-side-effect, justified by the first-boot single-Ansible-run execution model. `run-container` and `run-script` split out for differing idempotency; `run-script` is the only **`escalate`** op (opaque, non-idempotent).
- `join-host-to-domain` is **planner-inferred** (IR has no explicit join field): a host joins domain D when an activity places a D-domain actor on it. Single largest "IR doesn't say it" inference.
- `external: true` identities are op-less. `deploy-behavior-agent`/`start-activity` split (adds one op beyond PLANNER §9.2's `start-activity`); scheduling delegated to the runtime agent (deploy DAG never blocks on schedule windows); `start-activity` gated on `mirror-ready` so capture is structurally complete before traffic.
- **`tap_from: bridge` sensors reuse the sink's OVS mirror infra** (one mirror, N consumers) → §5.3 emission rule widened: `attach-gre-tunnel`/mirror emitted when any host is tapped **OR** any sensor observes the net via `tap_from: bridge`. `per-vm` sensors consume per-VM mirrors; `external-mirror` operator-wired.
- **v1 capture attribution = one gretap per zone** (resolves the either/or in ARCHITECTURE §434). Shared-gretap-with-metadata-keys deferred; would add `OP-MIR-030 register-mirror-source` (ID reserved).

**Why:**
- Validator and planner implementation are both gated on the closed op set; enumerating in one pass (as with the validator catalog) keeps cross-op decisions — wait-vs-barrier, redo-vs-escalate, shared resource categories — consistent.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/OP_CATALOG.md` — new, all sections.
- `docs/PLANNER.md` — §2.3 (`resource_categories` field + catalog pointer), §8.1/§8.2 (`gretap:<sink>` semaphore).
- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` — §434 (v1 per-zone-gretap decision marked).
- `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` — op-catalog question marked resolved.
- `docs/CLAUDE.md` — reading order updated to include OP_CATALOG.md.

**Supersedes:** none (additive; refines PLANNER §3.1 "1 op per network" and §9.2 redo-table approach without reversing them).

## 2026-05-26 — Validator: rule catalog v1

**Authority:** design (chat session)

**What changed:** the validator is now fully specified as a rule catalog of 53 rules across 9 categories, plus 7 cluster-pass rules executed by the planner. Full surface in `docs/VALIDATOR.md`; the major decisions:

**Rule schema (13 fields per rule)**
- `id` (stable across versions; never renumber, never reuse retired IDs), `category`, `severity` (`error` \| `warning` \| `info`), `reproducible_promotes_to_error` (bool), `pass` (1\|2\|3\|4), `title`, `description`, `rationale`, `input` (JSONPath-style field list), `message_template` (with `{placeholder}` substitution), `fixable` (canvas one-click fix hint), `examples` (`fails:` + `passes:` YAML snippets, doubling as test data), `related` (adjacent rule IDs for navigation).
- Numbering: `<CAT>-<NNN>` in tens (`STR-010`, `STR-020`, …) with gaps for insertion. Nine category prefixes: `STR` structural, `INC` include-consistency, `REF` reference-integrity, `CAP` capability, `LIF` lifecycle, `NET` network-sanity, `OVR` overlay, `ACT` activity, `DEF` defense, plus `CLU` cluster (planner-side).

**Severity ladder**
- Three levels: `error` (blocks deploy), `warning` (proceeds but flags), `info` (observational; suppressed in CI by default).
- `reproducible_promotes_to_error: true` on individual rules promotes severity by one step when the scenario has `reproducible: true`. Used for unpinned image refs (`REF-130`), unpinned behavior refs (`REF-150`), missing seeds (`LIF-060`), deprecated-behavior refs (`LIF-030`). Models "one rule, conditional severity" cleanly without doubling the catalog.

**Four-pass execution**
- **Pass 1** structural + include consistency. Halt on first error (downstream rules can't trust shape).
- **Pass 2** reference integrity. Halt on first error (capability checks need real refs).
- **Pass 3** capability, lifecycle, network, overlay, activity, defense. Accumulate findings.
- **Pass 4** cluster. Planner-side only — needs a live Proxmox snapshot. `axrange validate` skips it; `axrange deploy` runs it as the validator's final phase before DAG construction.

**Code structure: shared library**
- Validator is a `validator` package consumed by both `axrange validate` (passes 1–3) and `axrange deploy` (passes 1–4 via the planner). No dependency on planner internals; the planner depends on the validator, not vice versa.
- Four entry points: `validate_structural(ir)`, `validate_full(ir, manifests)`, `validate_with_cluster(ir, manifests, cluster_snapshot)`, `explain_rule(rule_id)`.
- Same rule code path runs in CLI, canvas, CI, and deploy — operators never see inconsistent results between surfaces.
- DAG-shape errors (cycle detection, reachability, type consistency among ops) stay in the planner per `docs/PLANNER.md` §3.5. Clean line: validator checks IR; planner checks DAG.

**Rule count by category** (53 IR-side + 7 planner-side):
- STR 18, INC 5, REF 19, CAP 6, LIF 6, NET 10, OVR 4, ACT 5, DEF 5, CLU 7.

**Explicit non-goals**
- Validator does not check: semantic realism of attacker scenarios; behavior library content; image content beyond manifest claims (delegated to image build pipeline conformance tests — Q6); runtime behavior; defensive-tool efficacy; CIDR collisions with operator's home/management network; license/IP/classification of artifacts; spelling of IDs.

**Forward-compatible placeholders (dormant in v1)**
- `REF-190` (phase references) — activates when phases ratify in IR (see IR_SCHEMA §11 open follow-ups).
- `OVR-040` (data classification taxonomy) — activates when classification taxonomy ratifies.

**Operator UX**
- Terminal output: errors first, warnings second, info last; each finding shows rule ID, severity, templated message with substituted values, and `→ see also:` / `→ fix:` lines.
- Exit code 0 for PASS or PASS-WITH-WARNINGS; non-zero for FAIL.
- `axrange validate --explain <id>` prints the full rule definition.
- Canvas output is structurally identical, rendered as inline annotations.

**Why:**
- The validator's input contract has been fully defined since the 2026-05-21 IR + manifest + planner ratifications. Q3's remaining work was enumeration, not design — and enumeration with 53 interacting rules benefits from being done in one pass so severity decisions stay consistent across categories.
- A closed rule catalog with stable IDs lets operators, CI, and the canvas reference findings deterministically. `axrange validate --explain CAP-010` is a contract; the rule's ID never changes.
- Three-level severity with conditional promotion (`reproducible_promotes_to_error`) is the smallest expressive form: it covers the warn-in-casual-authoring / error-under-strict-reproducibility pattern without doubling the rule count.
- Shared library + four entry points keeps validator and planner from drifting. The planner runs the same rule code; an operator who fixes their scenario against `axrange validate` will not see a different verdict at deploy time (modulo cluster-pass rules, which fundamentally can't run without a snapshot).
- Halt-on-error for Passes 1–2 is correctness, not policy: structural and reference checks gate downstream rules' ability to read the IR safely.
- Stable rule IDs prevent the catalog from becoming brittle as it evolves. Adding rules and tuning severities is CHANGELOG-able; renumbering is forbidden.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/VALIDATOR.md` — new formal spec; this is the authoritative surface.
- `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` Q3 — marked resolved.
- `CLAUDE.md` — reading order updated to include `docs/VALIDATOR.md`.

**Links:**
- Resolves OPEN_QUESTIONS Q3. Advances Q11 (operator UX details now specified for validator output).
- **Sibling deliverables deferred for future sessions:** op catalog (still pending; the closed list of ~30–50 planner op types — independent of validator); redo rules table (planner-side, smaller); rule-by-rule severity re-tuning based on real operator experience; severity escalation in CI mode (`--strict`); rule-disable mechanism (`--disable-rule`); localization of error messages.

## 2026-05-21 — Planner: DAG construction + runtime + concurrency + re-planning

**Authority:** design (chat session)

**What changed:** the planner is now fully specified across operation model, execution semantics, runtime structure, and re-planning behavior. Full surface in `docs/PLANNER.md`; the major decisions:

**Operation model**
- **Fine granularity** — one op per externally-observable side effect. ~12 ops per host + 1 per `install:` action + sibling ops for overlays/identity/data/activity. A typical 8-host AD scenario produces ~150-250 ops.
- **Three op kinds:** `action` (mutate state, single attempt), `wait` (poll with per-attempt timeout that resets on retry), `barrier` (synthetic milestone with no execution).
- **Typed inputs and outputs** on every op definition; data-flow edges are automatic from input/output matching.
- **`after:` annotations** declare non-data-flow ordering (e.g. mirror before OS-ready).
- **Synthetic barriers** (`host-ready[<id>]`, `network-ready[<id>]`, `domain-ready[<fqdn>]`) absorb cross-entity dependencies; downstream ops depend on the barrier, not on specific implementation ops.
- **Data-flow edges may bypass barriers** when downstream ops need an upstream output directly. Barriers are pure ordering; outputs travel along their own edges.
- **App install-action ops are strictly sequenced in IR order.** Authors who want parallelism split into multiple apps.
- **The op catalog itself is a deferred deliverable**, not part of v1 ratification. The set of op types will be enumerated in a follow-up session.

**DAG construction**
- Three-pass deterministic build: (1) emit op instances + barriers, (2) data-flow edges from output/input matching, (3) ordering edges from `after:` + IR bindings.
- Placement decision per host node: planner takes the **union** of IR `host:*` requirements and binding `requires_host_caps:`, filters live Proxmox nodes by that union, picks one via least-loaded heuristic (alphabetic tiebreak), records the choice in the state store. Re-deploys land on the same physical node unless constraints have changed.
- DAG-level validation runs before any external state is touched: cycle detection, reachability, type consistency, placement satisfiability.
- **Same IR + same manifests + same cluster snapshot → same DAG, deterministic and hashable.**

**Idempotency — two-layer model**
- External contract: every action op is idempotent on retry from the planner's perspective.
- **`infra` ops** (Proxmox API, OVS, anything the adapter implements directly): **Model C** — deterministic resource naming from `{plan_id, op_id}`, check-then-act on entry, cleanup-on-partial-state-before-redo.
- **`config` ops** (Ansible-wrapped): Ansible-native idempotency. Adapter just re-runs the playbook.
- **`cleanup_strategy: redo | escalate`** on op definitions. `redo` (default) means the op self-cleans and retries. `escalate` means the op cannot self-clean; failure walks back to the preceding barrier and redoes the subtree.

**Failure semantics — platform defaults, operator overrides**
- **Retry policy:** 3 attempts (one initial + two retries), exponential backoff 5s → 15s → 45s with ±10% jitter. Overrides: `--max-retries N`, `--retry-backoff TYPE`.
- **Escalation policy:** auto-escalate once on `escalate`-marked failure; halt subtree on second escalate-failure. Override: `--no-auto-escalate`.
- **Halt scope:** failing subtree + transitive dependents; independent subtrees continue. Overrides: `--halt-scope subtree | nothing | everything`.
- **Partial deploys are first-class.** State store records per-op status (`completed | failed | not-started | halted-due-to-upstream`); re-running `axrange deploy` resumes from where the previous attempt left off.
- **Operator-facing failure output:** tree view of per-op status with status symbols (✓ ✗ ⊘ ⋯).

**Runtime — ephemeral process per deploy**
- `axrange deploy <scenario.yaml>` starts a planner process that runs to completion or terminal failure, then exits. By default attached; `--detach` forks to background; `axrange status <deploy-id>` queries the state store.
- **Lease/lock per scenario.** Planner takes a lease in the state store, heartbeats every minute, lease expires after 5 minutes. Concurrent invocations on the same scenario refuse to start while a valid lease exists; crash-restart resumes after lease expiry.
- The adapter is a library the planner imports, not a separate process.

**State store — SQLite**
- Single file on the orchestrator host (default `/var/lib/axrange/state.db`).
- Tables: `deploys`, `leases`, `ops`, `op_attempts`. Schema sketched in `docs/PLANNER.md` §7.2.
- **Atomic op completion + output persistence** (single transaction).
- Op outputs are typed JSON; downstream ops consume them through the data-flow edges.
- Inspection: SQLite tooling against the file. The state store doubles as a diagnostics surface.
- PostgreSQL migration is a clean port if v1 SQLite hits scale limits.

**Concurrency — semaphore-per-resource**
- Each op declares which resource categories it uses. Categories: `proxmox-cluster`, `proxmox-node:<name>`, `ovs:<name>`, `vm:<vmid>`, `ansible-pool`.
- Default limits: `proxmox-cluster: 16`, `proxmox-node:<name>: 4`, `ovs:<name>: 1` (fixed, correctness), `vm:<vmid>: 1` (fixed, correctness), `ansible-pool: 8`. Global `max-ops-in-flight: 32` defense-in-depth cap.
- Ops wait for slots in **every** category they declare, simultaneously. FIFO per category.
- Operator overrides: `--limit-cluster`, `--limit-per-node`, `--limit-ansible`, `--max-ops-in-flight`.

**Re-planning — plan diff with confirmation**
- Every `axrange deploy` against an existing scenario computes a diff between the new DAG and the prior state-store record (matched by `ir_hash`).
- Per-entity change classification: `Unchanged` (skip), `Mutated` (redo per the redo rules table), `Added` (deploy normally), `Removed` (decommission, gated behind `--allow-destroy`).
- **Redo rules table** is a sibling deliverable, not yet written. Examples: `nodes[*].image` change → full host rebuild; `nodes[*].resources` change → `configure-vm-hardware` only; `apps[*].install` change → affected install actions only.
- Default behavior: show diff, require operator confirmation. Diff display surfaces every redo, add, and remove inline.

**CLI surface (consolidated)**
- Commands: `axrange deploy`, `axrange status`, `axrange list-deploys`, `axrange cancel`, `axrange validate`, `axrange plan`.
- Deploy-time flags: `--detach`, `--dry-run`, `--max-retries`, `--retry-backoff`, `--no-auto-escalate`, `--halt-scope`, `--allow-destroy`, plus the concurrency overrides above.

**Why:**

- **Fine granularity** trades modeling surface for surgical retry and surgical partial-redeploy. Coarse granularity doesn't reduce the underlying number of Proxmox/OVS operations that need idempotency — it just hides them inside larger envelopes and makes recovery from partial failure more expensive.
- **Synthetic barriers + typed I/O** decouple consumers from producer details. Op composition can change (split one op into two; add a new wait step) without rippling through downstream ops.
- **The two-layer idempotency model** honestly captures the reality: Ansible's idempotency really does work for configuration ops, and we shouldn't pay the cost of reinventing it. Infrastructure ops genuinely need explicit deterministic-naming machinery; that work doesn't go away by hiding it.
- **`escalate` semantics** acknowledge that some ops can't self-clean (arbitrary scripts, irreversible side effects). Auto-escalating once + halting on second-escalate gives transient-failure resilience without re-cloning VMs five times.
- **Halt-failing-subtree + transitive dependents** uses the DAG's own dependency information to scope failure. The principled answer is just walking the graph.
- **Ephemeral process + state store as truth + lease/lock** gets us daemon-like resilience (deploys survive terminal disconnect; crash-restart resumes) without operating a daemon. The lease is one well-defined piece of mechanism rather than ongoing daemon management.
- **SQLite** is the right v1 choice: zero deployment overhead, transactional, single-writer fits our model, inspectable with standard tooling. Migration path to Postgres remains clean.
- **Semaphore-per-resource** is the standard pattern for this shape of problem. Resource categories map directly to the actual contention points (Proxmox API, per-node clone cost, per-OVS-instance serialization, per-VM serialization, Ansible pool).
- **Plan-diff with per-field redo rules + confirmation** is the principled middle ground between "rebuild everything every time" (wasteful) and "automatic Terraform-style diff-and-apply" (surprising and dangerous). The diff is visible before destructive actions; the operator approves.
- **Determinism is a load-bearing property.** Reproducible DAGs enable `reproducible: true` IR scenarios, plan caching, test reproducibility, and the per-op idempotency contract (which requires stable `op_id`s across replays).

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/PLANNER.md` — new formal spec; this is the authoritative surface.
- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §4 (Planner) — updated to point at `docs/PLANNER.md`; the previous bullet-list sketch of "DAG, idempotent, retry, etc." is now the planner's actual surface, not a sketch.
- `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` — Q4 marked resolved; Q7 effectively resolved (SQLite + schema sketched); Q5 advanced (adapter is a library, runtime model known); Q3 advanced (validator may share DAG-construction code with planner).

**Links:**
- Resolves OPEN_QUESTIONS Q4. Substantially resolves Q7. Advances Q3, Q5, Q11.
- **Sibling deliverables deferred for future sessions:** op catalog (closed list of op types with full signatures); redo rules table (per-IR-field redo specifications); validator rule catalog (Q3); plan-diff confirmation UX details; concurrency-limit empirical defaults (current values are guesses).

## 2026-05-21 — Image manifest + binding: two-tier model

**Authority:** design (chat session)

**What changed:**
- Images are described by **two files per image**: a platform-independent **manifest** (`images/manifests/<image>.yaml`) and one **binding** per supported platform (`images/bindings/<platform>/<image>.yaml`). v1 ships Proxmox bindings only.
- **Repo layout:** `images/manifests/` and `images/bindings/proxmox/` (with `aws/`, `azure/` etc. as future siblings). One file per logical image; all versions of that image live inline in the same file.
- **Manifest schema** carries: `id`, `description`, `os: { family, distribution, version, architecture }`, and a `versions:` list. Each version has `id`, `released`, `status` (`current` | `deprecated` | `withdrawn`), `provides:` (capability set drawn from the v1 16-keyword taxonomy), `install_support:` (per install-action kind: `pkg`, `config`, `role`, `container`, `script`), `first_boot_budget` (seconds, planner hint), `size_estimate`. Manifests carry **no `requires:`** — images provide capabilities, they don't require them.
- **Binding schema** is per-platform, per-version: Proxmox `storage` (template volid, vmid, node constraints), `clone_mode` (linked | full), `cloud_init` (default user, sysprep flag), `hardware` (machine type, BIOS, NIC/disk models, agent, TPM), `placement_constraints.requires_host_caps:` (which `host:*` keywords the deployed image needs from the physical Proxmox node), `clone_estimate` (seconds, planner hint).
- **Lifecycle:** `current` → `deprecated` (warning) → `withdrawn` (validator error with replacement pointer). Deprecated versions remain in the manifest so older IRs can still validate for reproducibility purposes.
- **Host-capability union model:** the planner constraint on a node's physical placement is the **union** of (a) IR-node-`requires:` host-prefixed keywords and (b) the binding's `requires_host_caps:`. IR authors may omit obvious requirements (`host:uefi` is implied by a modern Windows image's binding); bindings will fill them in. Either source is authoritative; both contribute.
- **Validator → planner → adapter handoff:** validator reads manifest (does image `provides:` ⊇ node `requires:`? does each app's `install:` action match the image's `install_support:`?). Planner reads binding (which Proxmox nodes satisfy the union of host-caps?). Adapter executes binding (clone the right template_vmid, configure hardware, push cloud-init).

**Why:**
- Separating platform-independent facts (what an image *is*) from platform-specific facts (how it deploys on Proxmox / AWS / Azure) prevents the manifest schema from being coupled to the list of known platforms. New platforms add binding directories, not manifest schema changes.
- Inlining versions in one file gives a single readable history per image — diffs show evolution, deprecation is a status change rather than a file deletion.
- Per-version `provides:` lets capability sets evolve across versions (v4 adds `kernel:edr-hooks` because that version pre-installs Defender ETW hooks; v3 doesn't have it). The IR validator can then give actionable errors ("v3 lacks `kernel:edr-hooks`; try v4").
- Per-version `install_support:` lets the validator catch IR mistakes before deploy (an IR saying `install: { container: ... }` on a Windows desktop image that doesn't run containers errors at validate time, not at deploy time).
- The host-capability union model lets IRs stay terse (don't repeat what the image binding already declares) while still letting authors over-declare for clarity. Either way the planner sees the complete constraint set.
- Planner hints (`first_boot_budget`, `clone_estimate`) live in the manifest/binding rather than being hard-coded in the planner — operators tune image-specific values without code changes.
- Files-in-git for v1 keeps the platform simple; the data model is registry-shaped enough that a future migration to a registry service is a straightforward export/import.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §2 — adds image-manifest/binding subsection
- `docs/IMAGE_MANIFEST.md` — new formal spec
- Future directories: `images/manifests/`, `images/bindings/proxmox/`

**Links:**
- Companion to the IR-shape and capability-taxonomy entries below. Together these three resolve OPEN_QUESTIONS Q2 and substantially inform Q3 (validator rules — input now fully defined), Q4 (planner DAG — operand metadata defined), Q5 (Proxmox adapter — binding fields define its translation table), Q6 (image pipeline — manifest is the contract image builds must satisfy).
- Open follow-ups (deferred): image build pipeline (separate concern from manifest); manifest signing / supply-chain integrity; image deltas / patching across versions; declaration of pre-installed behavior agents (possibly via a `behavior-agent:*` capability prefix or a separate `installed_agents:` field); validator UX for suggesting candidate versions when a requested version lacks needed capabilities.

## 2026-05-21 — IR capability taxonomy v1: closed 16-keyword set

**Authority:** design (chat session)

**What changed:**
- The `requires:` field on a node is a **flat list of prefixed keywords**. Bare keywords name OS families; `host:*` names physical-host capabilities; `kernel:*` names kernel/runtime capabilities.
- **OS family (3, bare, mutually exclusive — exactly one per node):** `windows`, `linux`, `bsd`.
- **Host capabilities (7, `host:*`):** `host:nested-virt`, `host:tpm`, `host:secure-boot`, `host:uefi`, `host:gpu`, `host:cpu-aes-ni`, `host:hugepages`.
- **Kernel/runtime capabilities (6, `kernel:*`):** `kernel:edr-hooks`, `kernel:ebpf`, `kernel:audit`, `kernel:container-runtime`, `kernel:dotnet-clr`, `kernel:powershell-5plus`.
- **No implicit inheritance** — `windows` does not imply `host:uefi`. Every required capability is named explicitly.
- The keyword set is **strictly closed for v1**. Adding a 17th keyword requires a CHANGELOG entry and review of any schema-version implications. Unknown keywords are validator errors, not warnings.
- **Image manifests declare `provides:`** using the same keyword set. Validator checks: image-`provides:` ⊇ node-`requires:`.
- **Dispatch by prefix:** validator handles bare (OS) keywords against the image catalog; `kernel:*` against image `provides:`; `host:*` is deferred to the planner, which checks against live Proxmox node capabilities at placement time.
- OS family distinguishes only `windows` / `linux` / `bsd` — not specific versions or distros. Image IDs are version-specific (`win11-finance@v3`); the validator's question is "can a Windows image be selected here?", not "which Windows."
- Categorical distinction: `requires:` is yes/no facts. `resources:` is amount-requests (CPU, RAM, disk). They do not overlap.

**Why:**
- Capabilities are the IR's mechanism for "can this scenario deploy on this platform with these images" — narrower than node role or app behavior, broader than image-internal detail.
- A closed set of 16 keywords is small enough to memorize, large enough to cover known v1 scenarios (Windows AD ransomware, Linux container workloads, IDS/EDR defense overlays, hardware-accelerated workloads).
- Flat-with-prefix keeps simple cases compact (`requires: [windows]`) while letting unusual scenarios get specific (`requires: [windows, host:tpm, host:secure-boot, kernel:edr-hooks]`) — without the asymmetry of a typed `{os:, host:, kernel:}` structure.
- No implicit inheritance means the IR is fully explicit about what it needs — no surprises when an image gets rebuilt with different defaults.
- Closed set + CHANGELOG-gated additions prevents silent capability drift: every keyword has a contract the validator/planner must honor; an unrecognized keyword would be a silent gap.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §2 — adds capability taxonomy subsection
- `docs/IR_SCHEMA.md` — new formal spec

**Links:**
- Companion to the IR-shape entry below. Both entries together resolve OPEN_QUESTIONS Q2's capability-related sub-questions.
- Open follow-ups (deferred): `requires-preset:` bundles, capability negation (`!host:tpm`), confidential-compute keywords (`host:sev`, `host:tdx`, `host:sgx`) — all CHANGELOG-able additions when a real scenario needs them.

---

## 2026-05-21 — IR concrete schema: structural shape and section field model

**Authority:** design (chat session)

**What changed:**
- IR is a **single canonical `scenario.yaml`** with optional `includes:` for reusable fragments. Include semantics are **strict-no-override**: a fragment and the main file may not define the same ID. (Future-work: a deliberate `patches:` mechanism if override-pain becomes real.)
- Top-level sections (siblings): `scenario:` header, `layers:` (with `infra`, `apps`, `activity`), `bindings:`, `overlays:` (with `identity`, `data`, `defense`). Overlays are optional per scenario.
- **Bindings are explicit** — a dedicated top-level `bindings:` list of typed dicts (`app→host`, `activity→host`, `activity→targets`, overlay bindings). No inline `on: <host-id>` shorthand. This makes bindings queryable and annotatable.
- **Identifiers are human-meaningful strings.** Validator catches dangling refs; optional `aliases:` field per node for rename continuity. No opaque/ULID IDs.
- **Two version fields:** `scenario_schema_version` (the IR spec version, monotonic int) and `scenario.version` (per-scenario monotonic int). They are distinct fields and bump independently.
- **One top-level `seed:`** at the `scenario:` header. Per-actor seeds derive from it deterministically; no explicit per-actor seeds in v1.
- **Routers and switches are just `nodes:` with `kind: router` / `kind: switch`** — no separate `routers:` list. A node has exactly one of `net: <id>` (single-homed) or `nets: [{net, ip}, ...]` (multi-homed); validator enforces.
- **`reproducible: true`** scenario flag promotes unpinned image refs, unpinned behavior refs, and missing `seed:` from warnings to errors. Default `false` for ergonomic authoring.
- **`install:` action kinds are a closed set of six for v1:** `pkg`, `pkg@version`, `config`, `role`, `container`, `script`. Actions run at first-boot only via cloud-init + pull-mode Ansible — they are not a runtime configuration channel. Mid-run reconfiguration must be modeled as activity.
- **Activity actors resolve in two modes:** if `overlays.identity` contains the actor ID, it's the overlay's identity (domain user, etc.); otherwise it's an implicit local user on the bound host. Validator warns when an activity references an actor that's missing from a present `overlays.identity` overlay (forces explicit decision).
- **Defense overlays bind via `observes:` (network mode, with `tap_from: bridge|per-vm|external-mirror`) or `installed_on:` (host-agent mode), or both.** Validator enforces that a tool's host can actually see the network it observes; capability-checks fire on `installed_on:` hosts.
- **Activity DSL accepts three forms:** library reference (`behavior: office-workday@v2`), library + `overrides:` block, or pure `inline:` definition. Library is the dominant case.

**Why:**
- The IR is the platform-agnostic boundary in the six-stage pipeline. Pinning its shape early prevents rework in canvas, validator, planner, and adapter — all of which consume or emit it.
- Explicit bindings let bindings be a queryable, annotatable first-class concept (phase-gating, conditions) without coupling them to either endpoint.
- Strict-no-override includes preserve the "git-diffable YAML" promise: the IR is what you read, not the result of mental merging.
- Human IDs preserve diff readability at the cost of rename-pain; the validator and `aliases:` make rename-pain manageable.
- A closed `install:` action set prevents the IR from drifting into a Turing-complete provisioning DSL (we have cloud-init + Ansible for that).
- Two-version-field model lets the IR spec evolve independently of any particular scenario's history.

**Affected docs:**
- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §2 — replaces the early YAML sketch with the ratified shape
- `docs/IR_SCHEMA.md` — new formal spec (companion to this entry)
- `docs/OPEN_QUESTIONS.md` Q2 — marked resolved

**Links:**
- Resolves OPEN_QUESTIONS Q2; partially informs Q1 (canvas serialization target), Q3 (validator rules), Q4 (planner DAG operand shape).

**Supersedes:** the early IR YAML sketch in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` §2 (lines 67–90 in the prior revision), which was explicitly marked "open — to be ratified before any IR parser is written." That sketch is replaced by the schema described in this entry and detailed in `docs/IR_SCHEMA.md`.
